- **AWS Skill Builder: 600+ free courses and certification exam prep** (medium) — AWS Skill Builder (skillbuilder.aws, read live 2026-10-22): 'an online learning center where you can learn from AWS experts... With access to 600+ free courses, certification exam prep, and training that allows you to build practical skills.' Free for self-directed learners, including students; no enrollment gate, no credit card. This is the successor to much of AWS's formerly free training content.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, for free; no .edu requirement, no card. Free content is the baseline; AWS has added paid professional subscription tiers in recent years (not verified in today's read - treat as a gotcha, not a fact).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at skillbuilder.aws and take courses/digital badges at no cost.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not assume everything is free if you later see 'Professional' tier marketing; today's read confirms the free 600+ course base only.
  - *Source:* https://skillbuilder.aws/
- **AWS Free Plan (new accounts, credit-based, in effect since 2025-07-15)** (high) — New accounts get $100 in credits immediately at sign-up, plus up to $100 more by completing 'Explore AWS' service-exploration activities in the Console Home widget (filtered to 'Earn AWS credits') = up to $200 total usable over 6 months. Covers 90+ services for up to 6 months, including Amazon RDS, Aurora Serverless, and DynamoDB. 'No charges and no surprise bills on the Free plan' unless you convert to a Paid plan or activate paid-only services. Account auto-closes at the earlier of 6 months after account opening or when credits run out; on expiration AWS suspends the account and retains your data 90 days (upgradable to Paid within that window), then account/content is permanently erased. Free plan is NOT eligible for promotional credits (e.g. AWS Activate).
  - *Eligibility:* One free grant per identity: 'ineligible for the Free plan or Free Tier credits if you have an existing AWS account or have previously had one'. Payment method NOT required for most new signups (AWS may still request one for identity verification). Credits are USD, apply across participating global regions; NOT available in China regions or AWS GovCloud (US). No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up for a new AWS account choosing the Free plan at aws.amazon.com/free -> $100 credit applied automatically. Complete service-exploration activities in the 'Explore AWS' widget on Console Home (filter: 'Earn AWS credits') within 6 months of account creation to earn up to $100 more.
  - *Expiry:* Initial $100 credit expires when the 6-month Free plan window ends (credit validity stated as 'up to 12 months' on service pages; activity-earned credits expire 12 months after account creation per FAQ). Account auto-closes at 6 months or when credits are exhausted, whichever is first; 90-day data retention after closure; no renewal, no second grant on a new identity-linked account.
  - *Gotchas:* Auto-close at 6 months means long-lived free work must be migrated/billed; data permanently deleted 90 days after closure. Free plan excludes promotional credits (Activate) and some paid-only services; upgrading to Paid to unlock those is irreversible (cannot revert to Free plan). Credits apply across regions but China/GovCloud excluded. Upgrading converts to pay-as-you-go once credits burn.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Paid Plan new-customer credits** (medium) — New customers choosing the Paid plan get 'up to $200 USD free credits' plus full access to all AWS services (including paid-only services like Bedrock AgentCore) and eligibility for promotional credits. Selected services can be trialed via 'limited free trials'; the trial clock starts at activation and remaining credits 'automatically apply to usage beyond the trial limits'. Pay-as-you-go billing applies beyond credit exhaustion.
  - *Eligibility:* Same one-free-grant-per-identity rule; a billing/payment method is in practice expected for a Paid plan (Free plan needed none). No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up choosing the Paid plan, or convert an existing Free-plan account to Paid (irreversible).
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable 'for up to 12 months' on the Paid plan per service pages (e.g. Aurora pricing page), vs the 6-month Free-plan window; trial clocks start at service activation.
  - *Gotchas:* Pay-as-you-go charges begin the moment credits run out; Free-to-Paid conversion is one-way; trials are time-boxed per service.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/aurora/pricing/
- **Legacy 12-month Free Tier for RDS (accounts created before 2025-07-15)** (high) — Grandfathered pre-2025-07-15 accounts keep the service-level 12-month free tier: up to 750 hours/month of single-AZ instance databases (aggregated across instance types), engines limited to MySQL, MariaDB, PostgreSQL, or SQL Server (Express Edition only), 20 GB/month of gp2 general-purpose storage, and 20 GB/month of automated backup storage for 1 year.
  - *Eligibility:* AWS accounts created before July 15, 2025 only; not the credit-based model.
  - *How to claim:* N/A - grandfathered automatically; nothing to claim.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months from account creation, then billed.
  - *Gotchas:* No free-tier credits available in AWS GovCloud (US) regions or the China (Beijing) region; SQL Server only in Express edition; 750 hours aggregate means running multiple RDS instances splits the hours.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/rds/pricing/
- **DynamoDB always-free allowances plus credit boost** (high) — Per the DynamoDB pricing page today: 25 WCUs and 25 RCUs of provisioned capacity (per Region, per payer account, monthly), 25 GB of data storage, 25 rWCUs for global tables deployed across two regions, 2.5 million stream read requests from DynamoDB Streams, 1 GB data transfer out per month (15 GB for the first 12 months, aggregated across AWS services); plus 'up to $200 USD in credits' to use the full DynamoDB feature set for up to 6 months on the Free plan.
  - *Eligibility:* Always-free allowances apply while you are an AWS customer (Free or Paid plan); the $200 credit boost is for new accounts under the credit-based model. No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Always-free allowances apply automatically; the credit boost comes with a new Free/Paid plan account (see the main Free plan finding).
  - *Expiry:* Monthly allowances recur; the 12-month egress allowance lapses at month 12; the $200 credit is within the 6-month Free plan window (or 12-month credit validity on Paid plan).
  - *Gotchas:* The page presents allowances without an explicit legacy/new split - the '15 GB first 12 months' egress language is legacy-style; request-based allowances (25 WCUs/25 RCUs) are the current always-free shape. Old (pre-2025) free tier was expressed as 25 GB + 2.5M write/5M read requests, so verify the exact current RCU/WCU mix against AWS Billing if cost-critical.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/pricing/
- **Aurora free availability (Free plan) + credit validity** (high) — The Free plan 'provides Aurora PostgreSQL serverless instances with up to 4 ACUs and 1 GiB of storage per cluster' (paid plans scale to 256 ACUs and 256 TiB). New customers receive '$100 in AWS credits at sign-up' usable 'for up to 12 months, in both Free and Paid plans' plus 'an additional $100 in credits, for a total of $200'.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS accounts (Free or Paid plan); no .edu requirement; no payment method required for most Free-plan signups.
  - *How to claim:* Automatic with new-account signup ($100); extra $100 via service-exploration activities.
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable up to 12 months per the Aurora page; the Free plan account itself auto-closes at 6 months - so the 12-month credit validity practically matters only if you convert to the Paid plan before closure.
  - *Gotchas:* Only Aurora PostgreSQL Serverless is named as a free-plan offering (not MySQL, not provisioned instances, no I/O/storage free GB figures on today's page - the legacy 120 hours db.t3.medium/10 GB storage/100 GB backup tier is not listed on the current Aurora page).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/aurora/pricing/
- **AWS Activate Founders (self-funded / bootstrapped startups)** (high) — 'Up to $5,000 USD in Activate Credits', with 'Start with $1,000 USD in Activate Credits' - select participants may qualify for the full $5,000. Credits offset infrastructure, data services, and AI/ML models, and are redeemable on third-party models on Amazon Bedrock. Includes mentorship, AWS Startup Advisor, and partner-network co-build/co-market access.
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-Series B stage; founded in the last 10 years; must have an AWS account on a Paid-plan tier; must be 'new to Activate Credits or requesting more credits than previously received'; apply with an AWS Builder ID using a professional email - no .edu requirement and no country restrictions stated on the page. USD credits subject to AWS Promotional Credit T&C.
  - *How to claim:* Create an AWS Builder ID -> choose self-funded (Founders) tier at aws.amazon.com/startups/join?destination=/credits/apply -> provide product, funding stage, and company details -> link/create a Paid-plan AWS account and submit; response in 5-10 business days.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the page - governed by the AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions (typically ~12-month burn-down, unverified today).
  - *Gotchas:* Amount is 'up to' $5,000 - default grant is $1,000; must run a Paid plan (the Free plan excludes promotional credits entirely); no .edu, but a professional email is required.

- **AWS Educate (free student/individual training, not a credit program)** (high) — AWS Educate (successor of Cloud Academy) is active and 'completely for free': free self-paced online training and hands-on labs covering Storage (S3), Compute (EC2), Networking (VPC), Databases (RDS) and Cloud Operations, with shareable digital badges. Open to any individual, 'no credit card needed'; learners as young as 13 can register with just an email address; 18+ also get job-board access; 'AWS Educate for employers' gives recruiters access to the trained talent pool. The page (copyright 2026) contains no dollar-credit amounts and no mention of granting personal AWS Free Tier accounts.

- **AWS Free Plan (new-customer Free Tier): up to $200 credits over 6 months** (high) — New AWS customers get $100 in Free Tier credits immediately on account creation, plus up to $100 more earned by completing 5 console activities in the 'Explore AWS' widget ($20 each: launch & terminate an EC2 instance; configure an RDS database; build a Lambda function with a function URL; submit a prompt in the Bedrock text playground; create an AWS Budgets alert). The Free plan covers 90+ services for up to 6 months with 'no charges and no surprise bills'; 30+ services remain always-free within monthly limits on both plans. Credits are auto-applied to eligible usage, including always-free overages. Free-plan accounts cannot use 'certain AWS services that would rapidly consume the entire credit amount, or hardware purchases'; Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is paid-plan exclusive. Announcement: AWS 'what's new' July 16, 2025; AWS News Blog July 15, 2025. Eligible EC2 free-tier instance types for accounts on/after Jul 15, 2025: t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large (t2.micro no longer eligible). RDS free on db.t3.micro / db.t4g.micro (MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB, SQL Server Express); Aurora PostgreSQL serverless up to 4 ACU + 1 GiB storage; Aurora DSQL always free at 100k DPU + 1 GiB storage/month.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS customers only (any existing or previously-held AWS account disqualifies you). All customers can sign up online; no .edu or startup requirement. Excluded regions: China (ZHY) and China (BJS) regions, and AWS GovCloud (US) regions (with a carve-out for certain services such as Lambda in GovCloud). Credit card NOT required to sign up for most new customers (AWS may request a payment method to verify identity in some cases).
  - *How to claim:* Go to https://aws.amazon.com/free/ -> 'Create a free account' -> choose the Free account plan during signup. $100 credit posts automatically (may take up to ~10 min to appear on the Credits page). Complete the 5 Explore AWS activities inside 6 months to earn the remaining $100. Monitor balance via the Cost & Usage widget on console home.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan expires at the earlier of (a) 6 months from account creation or (b) credits exhausted. Unused credits stay usable up to 12 months from account creation if you upgrade to a Paid plan within the window. After the Free plan period, you have 90 days to upgrade; then AWS permanently closes the account and deletes content/resources. Earned-credit activities must be completed within 6 months of creation; eligibility to earn ends permanently if you join AWS Organizations or set up a Control Tower landing zone.
  - *Gotchas:* Free plan is NOT eligible for promotional/incentive credits (e.g., AWS Activate) - those require a Paid plan; enrolling a Free-plan account in AWS Organizations auto-upgrades it to Paid. Usage is aggregated across all regions; no monthly free-usage rollover. AWS may reclaim resources unused for 3 months after 30 days' notice. Free plan is for evaluation only - 'should not be used for processing sensitive data'. Data transfer/egress beyond always-free allowances draws on credits, then pay-as-you-go once credits run out. New sign-up experience launched October 2026 (credit expiry view moved to Settings Portal; older accounts use the Credits page in the Billing console).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/(plushttps://aws.amazon.com/free/free-tier-faqs/,https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2025/07/aws-free-tier-credits-month-free-plan/,https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/aws/aws-free-tier-update-new-customers-can-get-started-and-explore-aws-with-up-to-200-in-credits/)
- **AWS Always Free offers: 30+ services with monthly free usage limits (all customers)** (high) — On both Free and Paid plans, '30+ AWS services are always free within monthly usage limits' (ongoing monthly allowances, e.g., Lambda free requests/month, Aurora DSQL 100k DPU + 1 GiB storage/month). If usage exceeds the monthly allowance, remaining Free Tier credits auto-apply to cover eligible costs (Free plan); on Paid plans you pay standard rates beyond the credit balance. Per-service numeric limits are listed on the Free Tier page filters ('Explore our offers' by offer type + product category) and on individual product pricing pages; the main page does not publish the full numeric list.
  - *Eligibility:* All AWS customers, new and existing, on either plan. No credit card needed to keep an account. No regional restrictions stated beyond the general Free Tier exclusions.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim - always-free allowances are automatically active on any AWS account. Find each service's allowance at https://aws.amazon.com/free/ via the offer-type/category filters, or on the product's pricing page.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing for as long as you have an AWS account (no 12-month sunset). Unused monthly allowances do not roll over. AWS may terminate any offer at any time (Free Tier Terms, last updated Jul 9, 2025).
  - *Gotchas:* Overage handling differs by plan (credits vs pay-as-you-go). A few services/trials are paid-plan exclusive. AWS can reclaim resources idle for 3 months (30 days notice); usage is aggregated across regions.
  - *Source:* https://docs.aws.amazon.com/awsaccountbilling/latest/aboutv2/free-tier.htmlandhttps://aws.amazon.com/free/terms/
- **Legacy 12-month Free Tier (accounts created before July 15, 2025)** (high) — Accounts created before Jul 15, 2025 keep the classic per-service 12-month Free Tier instead of the credit model: e.g., EC2 750 hrs/month of t2.micro/t3.micro with free-tier-eligible EBS (standard, st1, sc1, gp2, gp3); legacy allowances like 30 GB EBS / 2M I/Os / 1 GB snapshot storage and 750 hrs/month ELB (Classic+ALB shared, 15 GB data processing, 15 LCUs). Exceeding limits bills pay-as-you-go. RDS legacy free usage (db.t3.micro/db.t4g.micro) runs up to 12 months from signup for such accounts. The legacy EC2 free-tier box in the console only appears for pre-Jul-15-2025 accounts.
  - *Eligibility:* Only AWS accounts created before July 15, 2025.
  - *How to claim:* No claim - applied automatically at legacy signup; track via the EC2 'Free Tier usage' console box (end-of-month forecast / exceeds-free-tier warnings).
  - *Expiry:* 12 months from account creation; then standard pay-as-you-go rates with no credit-based protection.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse the two regimes: new accounts (on/after Jul 15, 2025) have NO per-service 12-month allowances - usage is instead covered by the $200 credit pool and always-free offers. t2.micro is not free-tier eligible for new accounts.
  - *Source:* https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ec2-free-tier-usage.htmlandhttps://aws.amazon.com/rds/free/
- **AWS Activate Founders (self-funded startups): $1,000, up to $5,000 credits** (high) — Activate Founders tier: start with $1,000 USD in AWS Activate credits; 'select participants may qualify for additional credits up to $5,000' over time. Credits offset costs on 200+ AWS services incl. infrastructure, data services, and third-party models on Amazon Bedrock (OpenAI, Mistral, Anthropic, Meta). Includes technical guidance, business mentorship, and go-to-market resources. AWS Activate has served 350k+ startups and granted $8B+ in credits since 2013.

Response in 5-10 business days.
  - *Expiry:* Govered by AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions; check the specific award notice for the credit's expiry window (not stated on the program page).
  - *Gotchas:* Paid-plan account required - convert your Free plan account first; using an inconsistent email domain hampers validation; if you previously received equal-or-greater Activate credits you cannot re-claim. Kiro Startup Credits exist as an on-ramp for startups that do NOT yet hold Activate credits. Credits are USD and non-transferable between programs.

- **AWS Activate Portfolio (VC / accelerator / angel-backed startups): up to $200,000 credits** (high) — Activate Portfolio tier: up to $200,000 USD in AWS Activate credits, applied through the backing provider's Organizational ID (Org ID). Named provider examples on the page: Andreessen Horowitz, Brex, Carta, Greylock Partners, Sequoia, Y Combinator, NVIDIA (among 'thousands' of VCs, accelerators, and startup-enabling organizations). Credits work across 200+ services and Bedrock third-party models. Program page headlines 'Get up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits' and notes AI startups 'may be eligible for additional credits beyond Activate'.

Pre-Series B requirement and 10-year founding-age cap apply. The Free plan does not qualify (Paid plan required), and Free-plan accounts became ineligible for promotional credits effective Feb 16, 2026.

- **AWS Credits for AI Startups (invite-only): $200,000+ credits** (high) — Separate, invite-only track on the official credits page: '$200,000+ in AWS Credits' for 'startups ready to scale post Activate Portfolio', accessed by 'talk with your Account Manager'.   - *Expiry:* Skill Builder Premium lasts 12 months from claim. The 21 badges include time-gated ones (7-day, 30-day, 90-day streaks), so the program effectively requires sustained engagement over ~90 days to capture the full value; credits/voucher per promotional terms.
  - *Gotchas:* The credits/voucher are NOT handed at signup - they require earning 7/14/21 Builder Center activity badges (community badges, not course-completion badges). Start long-timer badges on day 1 or you can stall at 15-18. Verification is individual; your college does not need to be an AWS Academy member.

- **AWS Educate ('Cloud Skills for Education'): free self-paced learning, hands-on labs, no credit card** (high) — AWS Educate is open to any individual regardless of education, experience, or career stage: free curated learning paths in cloud and AI with five hands-on 'Getting Started' lab tracks (Storage/S3 static website, Compute/EC2, Networking/VPC, Databases/RDS, Cloud Operations/cost estimation), shareable digital badges, and a Job Board (learners 18+; separate recruiter portal). Learners as young as 13 can register with just an email address - 'no credit card needed'. Complemented by free self-paced digital training on AWS Skill Builder (500+ on-demand courses, 17 languages) and free Builder Center workshops in real AWS environments.
  - *Eligibility:* No .edu email required - any valid email; age 13+ to register, 18+ for the Job Board. Global.

  - *Expiry:* Ongoing program - no expiry. (Legacy monetary grants no longer exist; see sunset notes.)
  - *Gotchas:* Since Feb 21, 2023 AWS Educate focuses on pre-professional learners and no longer offers grants to institutions or educators; classroom credit allotments now run through AWS Academy/instructor-managed labs. Educator tools were migrated to AWS Academy. Hands-on lab accounts are restricted/sandboxed (third-party docs describe ~3-hour activity logoff and feature limits).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/education/awseducate/
- **AWS Academy: free ready-to-teach curriculum + educator certification vouchers (instructor/education track)** (medium) — AWS Academy provides higher-education institutions a free, ready-to-teach cloud/AI-ML curriculum (6 foundational courses incl. Generative AI Foundations and ML Foundations, 5 associate-level courses, 2 lab sets, plus an ad-hoc 'Learner Lab' sandbox). Educators: complimentary enablement sessions, 100% discount vouchers on AWS Certification exams, and complimentary 12-month AWS Skill Builder subscription. Students: complimentary 12-month Skill Builder subscription and, on course completion, access to the Emerging Talent Community (challenges redeemable for discounted certification exam vouchers). Institutions: 50% discount voucher on certification exams (redeems to students for 100% of value). No cost to join. Third-party university IT docs (Univ. of Iowa) describe 'Learning Labs' giving instructors $100 of AWS credit per student for the course duration, no credit card from students, instructor-funded.

Educators must be nominated by the member institution and complete the mandatory Educator Getting Started course (associate-level courses additionally require educator certification). Courses may only be delivered to students enrolled at the institution.

  - *Expiry:* 12-month Skill Builder subscriptions from award; certification vouchers per program terms ('all rewards subject to change'); Emerging Talent Community rewards vary.
  - *Gotchas:* This is an education-license/curriculum offer, not direct cash credits - the per-student classroom credits (~$100, per institution docs) are not published on AWS's national pages and depend on your institution's enrollment; confirm amounts with your AWS regional team. No cost to join, but delivery requirements (accredited educators, full curriculum) apply.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/training/awsacademy/faq/(supportingthird-partydoc:https://its.uiowa.edu/services/amazon-web-services-aws/using-aws-your-classroom)
- **AWS Cloud Credit for Research: up to $5,000 for students, uncapped for faculty/staff** (high) — AWS Public Sector Cloud Credit for Research Program grants AWS Promotional Credit to support finite research projects: building cloud-hosted science-as-a-service applications/tools, proof-of-concept or benchmark tests of moving research workloads/open data to the cloud, or training the community via workshops/tutorials. Student awards capped at $5,000; faculty and staff awards are not capped; recipients may receive less than requested. EC2 usage is on-demand and spot instances only. Awards are AWS Promotional Credit under the AWS Promotional Credit T&Cs, valid one year from application to the account or until fully utilized.
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time faculty, full-time research staff, or graduate/post-graduate/PhD students at an accredited research institution. An institution-issued email (.edu, .org, or equivalent) is REQUIRED - non-institution domains are not accepted. An existing AWS account is required. Program is global except the greater China region (India administered by AISPL). Effective Feb 16, 2026, Free Tier (Free plan) accounts are ineligible for promotional credits.
  - *How to claim:* Submit a short single-page proposal (problem statement, project summary, specific AWS services, timeline/milestones, plan to share outcomes, future use, AWS contacts, any AWS Public Datasets used) via https://pages.awscloud.com/aws-cloud-credit-for-research.html; rolling review; decisions via aws-research-credit@amazon.com.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 1 year from application to the account or until fully used - plan the project timeline accordingly.
  - *Gotchas:* Does NOT fund administrative workloads, operations, ongoing/established research, or general lab funding. Review cycle is listed as 30-60 days on the apply page (the main page says 90-120 days) - plan ahead. Promotional credits cannot cover upfront fees of services. Free-plan accounts are excluded as of Feb 16, 2026 - you need a Paid-plan account to hold the award.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/government-education/research-and-technical-computing/cloud-credit-for-research/(FAQ:.../faqs/;apply:https://pages.awscloud.com/aws-cloud-credit-for-research.html)
- **Amazon SageMaker Studio Lab: free hosted Jupyter ML environment (CPU/GPU sessions, no AWS account)** (medium) — SageMaker Studio Lab is a completely free notebook environment for learning/experimenting with ML code and models: Jupyter-based with terminal access and GitHub integration; per university ITS documentation, access to CPU (12 hours per user session) and GPU (4 hours per user session) plus 15 GB of persistent storage that survives between sessions.   - *Gotchas:* For learning/experimentation only - not a production or long-term workload environment. Exact current session caps should be confirmed at sign-in (the site blocked automated fetching today, 403; figures above are from a 2024 university IT page).

- **AWS Free Tier (2025 revamp) — Free account plan: up to $200 credits, 6 months no-cost** (high) — Since July 15, 2025, new AWS accounts get $100 USD in credits immediately at sign-up, and can earn up to $100 more (5 activities x $20: launch/terminate an EC2 instance, RDS basics, Lambda function URL, Bedrock text playground, AWS Budgets) = up to $200 total. The Free plan incurs no charges for up to 6 months or until credits are exhausted, whichever comes first; the account then auto-closes unless converted to a Paid plan (upgrade must happen within 90 days of expiry notice or AWS permanently deletes account content/resources). 30+ services remain 'Always Free' on both plans. Not available in GovCloud (US) or China regions. A new sign-up experience launched October 2026 (credits tracked in Settings Portal instead of Billing console).
  - *Eligibility:* All new AWS customers (no prior/existing AWS account). No .edu required, no funding cap, no minimum funding; explicitly aimed at students, developers and early entrepreneurs. Payment method/credit card NOT required to sign up for most new customers (AWS may request one for identity verification in some cases). Free plan excludes 'hardware purchases' and services that would rapidly burn credits; enrolling the free account in AWS Organizations upgrades it to Paid and forfeits Free Tier credits.
  - *How to claim:* Create a new AWS account, choose the Free account plan (or Paid plan, which also gets the $200 credits), $100 is credited automatically; complete the five 5-activity tasks in the 'Explore AWS' widget to earn the remaining up to $100; upgrade to Paid at any time one-click. No application, no CC needed.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan period ends 6 months after account creation or when credits are used up. Terms state Free Tier credits expire 12 months from account opening; Free Tier FAQ says credits expire 6 months — treat 6 months (Free plan) as the practical window; on the Paid plan unused credits remain usable up to 12 months from sign-up. 'Always Free' offers never expire.
  - *Gotchas:* Legacy 12-month tier (750 hrs/mo t2.micro/t3.micro, 30 GB EBS, 100 GB egress/mo) applies ONLY to accounts created before July 15, 2025. Credits cannot be combined with other promotional offers, one account only, not transferable. Exceeding Always-Free limits auto-applies credits; beyond that you must be on Paid plan. Free plan accounts are 'for evaluation purposes, not for sensitive data'. Unused monthly allowances don't roll over; AWS may reclaim resources idle for 3 months on 30 days' notice. EC2 free-tier-eligible instance types under new model are t3.micro/t3.small/t4g.micro/t4g.small/c7i-flex.large/m7i-flex.large — no bare-metal (e.g. c7i-metal) or dedicated-host free offer exists; those are billable to credits only. No separate VPS product: the VPS front is covered by EC2 micro/small instances charged against credits rather than the old 750-hour cap.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Activate Founders (self-funded startups): $1,000 credits, up to $5,000** (high) — Activate Founders package grants $1,000 USD in AWS Activate Credits; 'select participants may qualify for additional credits up to $5,000' total over time. Credits are USD, governed by the AWS Promotional Credit T&Cs, and can offset 200+ AWS services including Amazon Bedrock third-party models. Decision within 5-10 business days of submission.
  - *Eligibility:* Self-funded (bootstrapped) startup; founded in the past 10 years; fully functioning company website; new to AWS Activate Credits; pre-Series B by definition (no external round). No .edu email requirement, no published HQ-country restriction. Must hold an AWS account on a Paid plan to receive credits.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create/verify an AWS Builder ID (personal email). 2) Complete the one-time AWS Activate profile using your business email (matching startup domain). 3) Choose the Founders tier on the credits application page. 4) Provide product/funding details, link your AWS account, submit; track status on the Credit Application Status page. Apply at aws.amazon.com/startups/join?destination=/credits/apply. A new 'apply using your AI agent' path also exists.
  - *Expiry:* Validity window not stated on the current official credits page (deferred to Promotional Credit T&Cs). Historical materials: 2-year validity windows for pre-seed tiers in 2024 AWS Japan materials; CNBC (June 2024) reported the legacy $100K tranche expired in 1 year vs 3 years for the $200K. Verify at grant time.
  - *Gotchas:* Requires a paid AWS account (payment method implications), so the 'no credit card' property of the Free Tier does not carry over. Inaccurate funding info can cause denial. Credits are one-way: amount is fixed once selected (per 2024 materials). Support plan fees (Developer/Business) have historically been credit-eligible per AWS materials, while partial/all-upfront Reserved Instances are NOT eligible.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **AWS Activate Portfolio (VC/accelerator-backed): up to $200,000 credits** (high) — Activate Portfolio grants up to $200,000 USD in credits to provider-backed startups. The ceiling was $100,000 and was doubled to $200,000 in June 2026 (confirmed by the redesigned live credits page and awslabs/startups content update, June 2026). Credits offset infrastructure, data services and AI/ML models; redeemable on third-party foundation models in Amazon Bedrock. AWS has provided over $7-8B in Activate credits since 2013 to 350,000+ startups.
  - *Eligibility:* Associated with an AWS Activate Provider (VC, CVC, accelerator, angel, e.g. a16z, Greylock, Sequoia, YC, Brex, Carta) and holding that provider's Organizational ID; has not previously received AWS Activate Credits of equal or greater value; pre-Series B with the most recent funding round within the last 12 months (if funded); founded in the past 10 years; fully functioning company website; AWS account on a Paid plan. No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Founder creates/verifies AWS Builder ID. 2) Complete AWS Activate profile with business email. 3) Choose Portfolio tier and enter the provider's Org ID. 4) Provide startup/funding details, link an existing or new AWS account, submit; decision in 5-10 business days. If funded after a pre-seed grant, top-up to the higher tier is possible (historically the system grants the difference, validity reset per terms).
  - *Expiry:* Not published per-tier on the official page (governed by Promotional Credit T&Cs). CNBC (June 28, 2024) reported the $200,000 tranche valid for 3 years vs 1 year for the old $100,000 — treat as indicative only. June 2026 cap doubling is documented on the live page (© 2026) and in the awslabs/startups repo changelog.
  - *Gotchas:* The June 2024 CNBC report ('$200K for Series A funded in past year, effective July 1, 2024') was not reflected in 2025 official materials (which still said 'up to $100,000'); the official cap only moved to $200,000 in June 2026. Credits cannot be applied to partial/all-upfront Reserved Instances; some services are excluded. No self-service: you need a provider Org ID — cold-start founders without VC ties get Founders ($1k-5k) only.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **AWS Credits for AI Startups (invite-only, above Activate)** (medium) — A new tier announced alongside the June 2026 credits-page redesign: 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' offering $200,000+ in AWS Credits, invite-only, for AI startups 'ready to scale post Activate Portfolio.' Access is via your AWS Account Manager; no public application form. Amount has no stated cap.
  - *Eligibility:* AI startups that have completed/exhausted the Activate Portfolio tier and are scaling beyond it; engagement is AM-driven (invite-only). Implied pre-Series B / within Activate program terms, but no published caps.
  - *How to claim:* No self-serve path: talk with your AWS Account Manager (or request via the AWS Startups team) to be referred for the AI startup credit offer. Application still anchored on the AWS Builder ID + Activate profile.
  - *Expiry:* Not published (invite-only, per-account). Governed by AWS Promotional Credit T&Cs like all Activate credits.
  - *Gotchas:* Amounts not disclosed publicly; eligibility is at AWS's discretion; 'apply using your AI agent' from the IDE (AWS Startup Advisor) is a new 2026 convenience but the >$200K tier itself requires AM engagement.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/awslabs/startups/pull/75
- **Kiro Startup Credits: up to 1 year of free Kiro Pro+ (2-30 users)** (high) — Kiro (AWS's agentic IDE) offers early-stage startups up to a 1-year's worth of free Kiro Pro+ usage credits in three tiers: Starter (up to 2 users), Growth (up to 10 users), Scale (up to 30 users). Credits are delivered to the AWS account and expire one year after issuance; when depleted the account auto-switches to standard Kiro Pro+ billing. Not refundable, re-issuable, transferable or sellable; can be shared among authorized users of the same company.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage to Series A startups; matching business-domain email and corresponding AWS Account ID required; companies with ACTIVE AWS Activate Credits are excluded from this promotion (though Activate credits can still be applied to Kiro usage); a list of excluded countries/regions applies (not itemized on the page).
  - *How to claim:* Sign in at kiro.dev/startups with your AWS account, select a tier (2/10/30 users), submit; review on rolling basis with follow-up email; approved credits are added automatically to your AWS account. Use Kiro via AWS IAM Identity Center (not Builder ID or social login).
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire 12 months after issuance to the AWS account; tier caps usage seats, not dollar amount.
  - *Gotchas:* If your startup already holds AWS Activate Credits, you are not eligible for this Kiro promo — plan which benefit to take. 'Kiro' is a tool-specific credit, not general AWS infrastructure credit.
  - *Source:* https://kiro.dev/startups
- **AWS Impact Accelerator (launched April 2025): $125,000 cash + up to $100,000 AWS credits** (medium) — Under a >$30M, three-year commitment, each qualifying startup receives $125,000 cash plus up to $100,000 in AWS credits (up to $225,000 combined), an 8-week personalized program with AWS startup experts, mentoring, investor networking and Amazon-leader introductions. Runs in founder-demographic tracks: Black Founders (inaugural cohort, June 2025, US-based), Women Founders (H2 2025, US-based), with Latino and LGBTQIA+ tracks following.
  - *Eligibility:* High-potential, pre-seed early-stage startups led by Black, women, Latino, or LGBTQIA+ founders (track-dependent); US-based cohorts at launch. No .edu or funding-stage exception beyond pre-seed.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through the program's dedicated application page when a track opens (inaugural Black Founders track applied via aws-startup-lofts.com, linked from the AWS announcement). Cohort-based: application windows are per track, not rolling.
  - *Expiry:* Program commitment spans three years from the April 2025 launch; individual cohort windows open/close per track. Whether tracks remained open in 2026 could not be re-verified (web-search budget exhausted this session).
  - *Gotchas:* Competitive cohort selection, not a self-serve credit grant. Cash leg ($125k) is separate from the AWS-credit leg. Demographic eligibility narrows applicant pool vs generic Activate.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/aws-startups/learn/aws-launches-30m-impact-accelerator-for-underrepresented-founders/
- **AWS Generative AI Accelerator (GAIA): up to $1,000,000 AWS credits (cohort program)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — GAIA is AWS's flagship cohort accelerator for generative-AI startups. The official 'What's New' page states the 2025 cohort 'will receive unmatched benefits designed to accelerate both technical growth and market traction, including up to $1 million in AWS credits' (per-startup figure cited by third-party trackers; the official page only states 'up to $1 million in AWS credits' for the cohort). Includes hands-on AI expertise, GTM resources, and investor/mentor connections. 80 startups were selected for the 2024 program.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage generative-AI startups; cohort is competitive/selected (applications open in cycles; 2025 cohort applications were open at time of the What's New entry). No .edu requirement. No public funding cap stated.
  - *How to claim:* Apply during open application windows on the GAIA program page (linked from AWS Startups news; the deep URL I attempted returned 404, so use the AWS Startups 'What's New' / Generative AI Accelerator landing when live).
  - *Expiry:* Cohort-based; 2025 cohort cited; a 2026 cycle likely but not verified in this session (search budget exhausted). Credit validity per-grant not published on fetched pages.
  - *Gotchas:* The exact per-startup dollar figure (commonly reported as up to $1M for top cohorts) is not confirmed on the pages I could load; cohort status for 2026 unverified.
  - *Source:* https://startups.aws.com/learn/whats-new
- **AWS education / student offers: free learning (no .edu required), no student-specific credits** (medium) — AWS has no student-specific free credit program and no 'education license' VPS offer. Free offers for students are: (1) the standard AWS Free Tier (up to $200 credits, 6 months, explicitly marketed to students — no .edu email and no credit card required for most new customers); (2) free digital training / courses and certification-voucher programs (AWS Academy resources, AWS Educate-style free self-paced content); (3) institutions can get 'special pricing for education customers' via sales (discounts, not free). The education page confirms 17,000+ education customers but lists no student credit dollar amounts.
  - *Eligibility:* Students: same as general Free Tier — new AWS account, no .edu requirement, no CC for most customers. Institutions: separate education customer pricing negotiated via AWS sales. No funding caps (N/A).
  - *How to claim:* Students: sign up for AWS Free Tier (Free or Paid plan) at aws.amazon.com/free and complete the credit-earning activities. Training: enroll in free AWS digital courses / Academy resources (free signup, no .edu). No application steps for credits themselves.
  - *Expiry:* Free Tier: 6-month plan / 12-month credit expiry (see Free Tier finding). Free training content has no expiry. Education-institution special pricing has no published sunset.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse 'AWS Academy' (partner-facilitated, some paid programs) with 'free digital training'. There is no dedicated .edu-gated offer; a school email is NOT required anywhere in the AWS free stack. Any 'student voucher' deals are ad-hoc campaign offers, not standing programs.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/education/
- **AWS Free Plan (new accounts, since Jul 15 2025): up to $200 credits, 6-month free plan** (high) — New AWS customers get $100 USD in credits immediately at signup and can earn up to $100 more by completing five 'explore' activities ($20 each: launch/terminate an EC2 instance, launch an RDS database, build a Lambda web app with a function URL, run a Bedrock prompt in the text playground, and create an AWS Budgets cost budget). Total up to $200. Choosing the Free account plan (the default at signup) means zero charges: 'no surprise bills', access to 90+ services for up to 6 months. The account auto-closes 6 months after opening or when credits run out, whichever is first. AWS suspends the account at expiry, retains your data 90 days, then permanently deletes account + content. Free plan includes Basic Support. Free plan cannot receive other promotional/incentive credits (e.g., AWS Activate), cannot buy hardware, and some 'large enterprise' services are unavailable; upgrade to Paid plan anytime (credits then remain valid until 12 months from signup).
  - *Eligibility:* All new AWS customers (anyone creating a brand-new AWS account); students and startups qualify exactly like everyone else — no .edu email, no student verification required. A credit/payment method is NOT required for most new customers (FAQ: 'A payment method isn't required to sign up for most new customers'; AWS may still request one or other identity info for verification in some cases). Excludes accounts that already have (or ever had) an AWS account; one Free Tier credit grant per person. Excluded regions: AWS GovCloud (US) and China (Beijing/Ningxia) regions; all other commercial regions GA.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to https://aws.amazon.com/free/ and create a new AWS account, selecting the Free account plan during sign-up. 2) $100 credit appears automatically. 3) In the AWS Management Console, complete the 5 explore activities (shown in the 'Explore AWS' widget / Credits page in Billing & Cost Management console; credits may take up to 10 minutes to post). 4) Track balance/expiry on the Credits page; AWS emails reminders as expiry approaches. 5) To keep using AWS after the 6-month free window, upgrade to Paid plan within the 90-day data-retention window (Settings portal > Payment > Upgrade).
  - *Expiry:* Free plan period: 6 months from account creation or credit exhaustion, whichever is first — no extension possible. Credits expire 12 months from account creation (if you upgrade to Paid, unused credits stay valid to that 12-month mark). After free-plan expiry: 90 days of data retention, then permanent deletion. A new sign-up experience is noted to launch in October 2026 (FAQ).
  - *Gotchas:* This replaces the old free 750 h/month EC2 VPS: on the new plan EC2 usage is credit-based (eligible instance types changed — t2.micro no longer listed; free-tier-eligible types are t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large), so a running VM burns your $100-$200 credits. Credits are non-transferable and non-stackable with any other AWS promo (Activate/Partner) credit. Enrolling in AWS Organizations or a Control Tower landing zone voids remaining explore activities and extra credits (account auto-upgrades to Paid). Resources unused for 3 consecutive months can be reclaimed on 30 days' notice. Go beyond always-free monthly caps on the Free plan and remaining credits are auto-deducted; once depleted you must upgrade to Paid. Free plan is intended for evaluation and 'should not be used for processing sensitive data'.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **Legacy 12-month Free Tier (accounts created before July 15, 2025)** (high) — Accounts created before 15 Jul 2025 still hold the old 12-month offers: EC2 free-tier-eligible instances t2.micro and t3.micro (the classic 750 h/month 'free VPS'), plus legacy per-service 12-month allowances (e.g., RDS db.t3.micro/db.t4g.micro for MySQL/PostgreSQL/MariaDB/SQL Server Express; S3, Lambda, etc. per the old terms, last updated Aug 14, 2018). Usage past limits is billed pay-as-you-go during the 12 months. This is now strictly legacy: the cutoff date is 15 Jul 2025 — new accounts cannot obtain it.
  - *Eligibility:* Only AWS customers who created their account before July 15, 2025. Not claimable by new accounts under any circumstances.
  - *How to claim:* Already enrolled automatically for pre-cutoff accounts; nothing to do. For new accounts: not claimable.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months from account creation (legacy terms, last updated 2018-08-14); after that, standard pay-as-you-go. Legacy RDS/other service offers follow their own per-service legacy pages (e.g., https://aws.amazon.com/rds/free/ shows 'Legacy Free Tier' for accounts activated before Jul 15, 2025).
  - *Gotchas:* Any 2024-era guide saying 'free t2.micro for 12 months' is stale for new signups — since 15 Jul 2025 there is no hours-based EC2 free tier for new accounts; new accounts are on the credit-based Free Plan instead. Exceeding legacy monthly limits before the 12-month end triggers pay-as-you-go charges.
  - *Source:* https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ec2-free-tier-usage.html
- **Always Free tier (30+ services, monthly caps, indefinite, both plans)** (medium) — 30+ AWS services remain 'always free within monthly usage limits' on both the Free and Paid plans, indefinitely. Verified example today: AWS Lambda free tier = 1,000,000 requests + 400,000 GB-seconds per month, free 512 MB ephemeral storage, first 6 MB streamed response per request free (100 GB/month streamed bytes). Other long-standing always-free allowances across the catalog (e.g., S3 storage, DynamoDB, CloudFront egress) live on individual service pricing pages; the /free/ page now routes per-service limits to those pages. Usage is aggregated across all regions; unused monthly amounts do not roll over.
  - *Eligibility:* All AWS customers (new AND existing) — including Paid-plan customers who skip the free plan. No credit card required for the always-free usage itself; no region/industry exclusions stated.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim — automatically applies to any account on eligible services within the monthly caps. Monitor via service dashboards/Billing console.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; perpetual monthly allowances. Amounts do not roll over between months. AWS may reclaim resources unused for 3 months (30-day notice) and may terminate any offer at any time.
  - *Gotchas:* On the Free plan, overflow beyond always-free caps automatically consumes your remaining Free Tier credits (and once credits are gone, you must upgrade to Paid to keep resources running). On the Paid plan, overflow is billed at standard rates. Concrete always-free numbers for each of the 30+ services must be checked per-service (today's /free/ page no longer prints them); only the Lambda figures above were re-verified on an official page today. Lambda provisioned concurrency has NO free tier.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/pricing/
- **AWS Activate / AWS for Startups — up to $200,000 in credits + partner offers** (high) — AWS for Startups (includes the former AWS Activate): headline is 'up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits' (credits offset infrastructure, data services, and AI/ML model costs, and are redeemable on third-party models on Amazon Bedrock). The /free/offers/ page states the Activate grant as '$1,000 to $100,000 in AWS credits' (tier-dependent) plus 'access to 80+ exclusive offers' in third-party products, memberships, and education programs, valued at up to $800,000. 'AI startups ready to grow may be eligible for additional credits beyond Activate' (no amount published). A separate 'Kiro credits' offer for startups is referenced (amount not published). Application is processed in 7-10 business days; an 'AWS Startup Advisor' can scaffold architecture and apply from an IDE.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup must be self-funded or externally funded up to and including Pre-Seed, Angel, Seed, Debt Finance, or Series A; must have a company website or public company profile; must have been founded in the past 10 years. No .edu/student track is published — student/educator access is via the general new-account Free Plan (above) or, as a new customer, AWS Free Tier + activation.
  - *How to claim:* 1) https://aws.amazon.com/startups/ → 'Join now' (/startups/join); for credits specifically: /startups/join?destination=/credits/apply. 2) Submit application (company details, public website); AWS reviews in 7-10 business days; decision by email. 3) Important: an account on the Free Plan is NOT eligible for Activate/promotional credits — you must be on the Paid plan (the new-account $200 Free Tier credits exist but the Free Plan 'cannot receive' other incentives).
  - *Expiry:* Per-tier credit durations are not published on the pages read today (legacy program historically allowed credits over 12-36 months depending on stage; not verifiable today due to web-search budget exhaustion — treat durations as unconfirmed). Credits governed by AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions.
  - *Gotchas:* Cannot combine with the Free Plan: AWS Promotional Credit terms bar combining Free Tier credits with other promo/incentive offers, and the Free Plan FAQ says Free-plan accounts can't receive other promotional credits like AWS Activate — so to collect Activate you must run a Paid plan and rely on standard pricing. Amounts: the public range is $1,000-$100,000 (Activate tiers) with a program headline of 'up to $200,000' plus unspecified extra AI credits. 80+ partner offers are third-party value, not AWS dollars. Kiro credits banner references an AI IDE credit program whose amount isn't disclosed on the pages read.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/
- **Education: no standalone free student/educator VPS or credit program published (AWS for Students/Faculty)** (medium) — The AWS education pages publish no student- or educator-only free instance/VM program and no .edu-gated credit offer. What exists: (1) students get the same universal new-account AWS Free Plan ($100 + up to $100 credits, 6 months) as anyone — no .edu verification; (2) free hands-on training content (AWS Training & Certification: self-paced digital courses, free digital certificates via the Skill Builder / free-course paths); (3) institutions: 'special pricing for education customers' by contacting public-sector sales (/government-education/contact/), on-campus training, and an education catalog on AWS Marketplace. The former dedicated AWS Activate portal for students/educators (historically up to $100k/$150k credits for verified students/faculty and accelerators) is no longer at its old URL — /activate/ now serves the AWS for Startups program page. No per-service 'education license' free-tier (like GitHub Education's free cloud credits) exists on AWS pages read today.
  - *Eligibility:* Students: standard new AWS account requirements (no .edu email required, no payment method required for most). Institutions/faculty: engage AWS public-sector sales for education pricing; free courses open to anyone via AWS Training.
  - *How to claim:* Students: create an account at https://aws.amazon.com/free/ (same as any new customer) to get the $100-$200 Free Plan; take free courses at https://aws.amazon.com/training/. Educators/institutions: request education pricing through /government-education/contact/; no self-serve credit application exists.
  - *Expiry:* N/A — no published education-only credit program to expire. Universal Free Plan rules apply (6 months/credits, 12-month credit expiry) to student accounts exactly as to all new accounts.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not expect a perpetual free VPS for students from AWS: the old 'free for 12 months' EC2 offer is gone for new accounts of any kind (15 Jul 2025 cutover). AWS has no .edu-verified free-credits program on its public pages today; the startup-track (Activate/Startups) is the only published large-credit program and it targets companies, not individuals (a solo founder's startup can qualify if it has a public company profile and fits the 10-year/ Series-A funding caps). Web search budget was exhausted mid-research, so absence-of-offer claims reflect the official pages fetched today, not an exhaustive search of AWS announcements.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/education/
- **CloudWatch / X-Ray / Synthetics Always-Free offers (Monitoring + Uptime)** (high) — Unlimited-duration, per-account monthly allowances confirmed on the CloudWatch pricing page today: 10 custom or Detailed Monitoring metrics; 1M CloudWatch API requests/month (excluding GetMetricData, GetInsightRuleReport, GetMetricWidgetImage, which are always charged); CloudWatch Logs 5 GB/month covering ingestion + archive storage + Logs Insights data scanned, plus 1,800 Live Tail minutes/month, 1 Contributor Insights rule/month and first 1M matching log events/month free; 3 custom dashboards referencing up to 50 metrics each (all Automatic Dashboards free); 10 alarm metrics (Standard resolution, direct metric listing, no Metrics Insights queries); Synthetics 100 canary runs/month (uptime checks); X-Ray first 100,000 traces recorded/month and 1,000,000 traces retrieved/scanned/month; RUM one-time trial of 1M web events; Application Observability: 3-month trial per account OR caps of 100 GB ingested / 1M X-Ray trace-summary spans / 100M signals, whichever first. The 'known so far' amounts (1M API requests, 10 metrics, 5 GB logs, 100 canaries, 3 dashboards, 10 alarms, 100K X-Ray traces) are all still correct today.
  - *Eligibility:* All AWS customers, new and existing, indefinitely; no .edu email, no startup status, no funding cap, no credit card required for most sign-ups (payment method may be requested for identity verification in some cases). Not available in China (Beijing/Ningbo) or AWS GovCloud (US) regions; limits are aggregated across all regions per account, no monthly rollover.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim or activate: create a normal AWS account (Free plan at sign-up gets zero charges) and usage under these caps bills as $0 automatically. Verify in Billing console > Free Tier page; CloudWatch Free Tier usage alerts fire at 85% of a limit.
  - *Expiry:* Always-free: no sunset, caps reset monthly, do not roll over. Under the new Free plan (accounts after Jul 15 2025), Always-Free offers are the only offers active on the Free plan; on the Paid plan, overage past these caps is automatically covered by remaining Free Tier credits, then standard pay-as-you-go.
  - *Gotchas:* GetMetricData / GetInsightRuleReport / GetMetricWidgetImage API calls are ALWAYS charged even within the 1M limit; High/Composite alarms, Container Insights observations, OTel metrics ingestion ($0.50/GB), Metrics Streams and Anomaly Detection alarms cost extra; exceeding a cap on the Free plan suspends the account once credits run out (no overage bills possible, but your monitoring dies); 3-month Application Observability trial starts on first use.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/cloudwatch/pricing/
- **AWS Free Tier credit model (up to $200 credits, 6-month Free plan)** (high) — As of Jul 15, 2025 the 12-month free tier for NEW accounts was replaced by a credit model: $100 credit at sign-up + up to $100 more earned via 5 activities in the Explore AWS widget (EC2 launch/terminate, RDS config, Lambda web app, Bedrock prompt, AWS Budgets budget = $20 each), max $200. Free plan: no charges ever, expires at earlier of 6 months or credit exhaustion, then account is suspended with a 90-day window to upgrade to Paid before AWS permanently deletes the account and data. Paid plan: same credits but pay-as-you-go after they run out; new customers on Paid can still redeem promotional credits (e.g. AWS Activate). 30+ always-free services remain on both plans.
  - *Eligibility:* All new customers worldwide, students included; no .edu email required; new AWS customers ONLY — anyone who has ever had an AWS account is ineligible; payment method not required for most new customers (social sign-in via Google/GitHub/Apple/Amazon supported since the Oct 2026 new sign-up experience); excluded from China (ZHY/BJS) and GovCloud regions.
  - *How to claim:* Create account at aws.amazon.com (or new sign-up flow) choosing Free or Paid plan; complete the 5 activities within 6 months to earn the extra $100; upgrade to Paid within 90 days of suspension to keep resources and remaining credits.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan: 6 months from account creation or credit depletion, whichever first; 90-day post-suspension grace then permanent deletion. Credit expiry is inconsistent across AWS pages: FAQ says base $100 expires 6 months and earned credits 12 months from account creation (upgrade to Paid shifts remaining credits to a 12-month window per Jul 2025 terms), while the Jul 9 2025 Terms say 12 months — plan on the EARLIER date and check the Credits page / Settings Portal in your account.
  - *Gotchas:* Free plan accounts are NOT eligible for promotional/Activate credits and cannot purchase paid offers — startups needing Activate credits must run a Paid plan; joining AWS Organizations or a Control Tower landing zone instantly upgrades Free to Paid and you stop earning credits; on Free plan only the ~90 services on the restricted list are available and overage on always-free items consumes your credit pool; no credit transfer between accounts; Free plan is for evaluation only (no sensitive data).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/free-tier-faqs/
- **AWS Activate Credits for startups (Founders up to $5K, Portfolio up to $200K)** (high) — Two tiers read from aws.amazon.com/startups/credits today: (1) Activate Founders — self-funded/seeded: $1,000 at start, select participants up to $5,000 total; (2) Activate Portfolio — must use an Organization ID (Org ID) from an Activate Provider (accelerator/VC/angel, e.g. Y Combinator, Sequoia, a16z, Brex, Carta, Rho): up to $200,000. Credits apply to 200+ AWS services, third-party foundation models on Amazon Bedrock, Kiro, and eligible Support plans. An invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier offers $200,000+ post-Activate via your Account Manager. Application turnaround 5-10 business days.
  - *Eligibility:* Startups: pre-Series B (most recent funding round within last 12 months, if applicable), founded in the last 10 years, fully functioning company website, AWS Account on Paid Tier Plan required, and must be new to Activate Credits or requesting more than previously received; no .edu email involved; no hard 'funding cap' — stage gates it. Portfolio tier additionally requires being affiliated with an Activate Provider and holding their Org ID.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create/verify an AWS Builder ID (personal email); 2) complete the AWS Activate profile (use your business-domain email); 3) pick tier — Founders directly, or Portfolio with your provider's Org ID; 4) link your AWS account and submit; AWS responds in 5-10 business days and credits are applied to the account.
  - *Expiry:* Promotional-credit expiry by size (since Jun 2024): $100K-class credits expire in 1 year, $200K-class in 3 years from receipt; confirm exact dates on the Credits page. The program itself is ongoing, not a trial; repeated applications are allowed only when requesting more than previously received.
  - *Gotchas:* AWS Account must be on a Paid plan (so a real billing payment method is effectively required — the Free plan cannot hold Activate credits); Free-plan accounts get none of this; credits do not cover AWS Marketplace purchases, RI upfront, training/cert fees or professional services; AWS has final say even with a provider vouch; multiple startups per founder or equal/higher prior credits can make a startup ineligible; credit exhaustion auto-switches to pay-as-you-go.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **Kiro Startup Credits (AI IDE, for early-stage startups)** (high) — Relaunched Apr 7, 2026: up to 1 year of Kiro Pro+ (agentic AI IDE by AWS) across three tiers — Starter (up to 2 users), Growth (up to 10), Scale (up to 30). Approved credits are deposited into the startup's AWS account and bill Kiro usage for free until exhausted or expired, then standard Kiro Pro+ billing starts automatically on the card on file. The original v1 (launched Dec 3, 2025 at re:Invent, up to 100 users, pre-seed to Series B, apply by Dec 31, 2025) is closed and was replaced.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage to Series A startups; current promotion states VC-backed; official business email whose domain matches the startup's company website AND an AWS Account ID using that same email; 18+; excludes residents of Argentina, Belarus, Brazil, China/GCR, Cuba, France, Germany, Iran, Italy, Mexico, North Korea, Poland, Russia, Spain, Syria, Crimea, DNR, LNR, UAE. Startups with ACTIVE AWS Activate Credits are NOT eligible (Activate credits can instead be used to pay for Kiro; Kiro first, then Apply to Activate is allowed).
  - *How to claim:* Apply at aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/kiro (window Apr 7 – Dec 31, 2026, rolling review, email notification). On approval: enable IAM Identity Center in AWS (required first step), create users/groups, activate the Kiro subscription in the AWS console, and sign developers in to Kiro via the org identity (Builder ID or Google/GitHub sign-in will NOT redeem the credits).
  - *Expiry:* Application deadline Dec 31, 2026; credits expire exactly 1 year after issuance to the AWS account; non-refundable, non-reissuable, one subscription per startup; overage past your tier's user count or upgrade above Pro+ incurs charges.
  - *Gotchas:* Team members must cancel their individual paid Kiro accounts after setup or they get double-billed; choosing the wrong region when enabling IAM Identity Center causes silent sign-in auth failures; credits activate on provision, not on your schedule — claim only when you'll use it within the year.
  - *Source:* https://kiro.dev/startups/terms/
- **AWS Educate + Student Rewards on AWS Builder Center (students)** (medium) — AWS Educate is now a free, self-paced learning platform (labs for S3/EC2/VPC/RDS + job board) open to anyone 13+ with just an email address — no credit card needed, no .edu required, and no universal credit grant (institute credit grants were discontinued Feb 2023; some member institutions still run their own grants, e.g. $100/yr per student, refreshed until graduation; legacy qwikLABS 'Starter Account' gave $75 credits no-CC). The new student money program is Student Rewards (launched Aug 2026, part of a $500M commitment): verified university students 18+ get 12 months Skill Builder Premium (900+ courses/labs, $449 value) for a complete profile, $10 AWS credits at 7 badges, $20 at 14 badges, and a $100 AWS Foundational exam voucher at 21 badges — up to $579 total per student. Credits redeem as promo codes in the Billing console and apply to eligible AWS services (not Marketplace, exam fees, RI upfront, or Pro Services).
  - *Eligibility:* Student Rewards: verified higher-ed enrollment via SheerID (enrollment check, not a .edu email), 18+; open to students worldwide in 60+ countries. Educate: anyone 13+ with an email (verified), no payment method; institutional grant variants require enrollment at a participating school.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in to AWS Builder Center, complete SheerID student verification, fill the profile (unlocks Skill Builder premium), then earn badges by publishing articles/comments/engagement to unlock the $10 + $20 credit codes and the $100 exam voucher; redeem codes in AWS Billing & Cost Management > Credits.
  - *Expiry:* Skill Builder access lasts 12 months from unlock; the program is ongoing (no published end date; rolling); institutional grants renew annually until graduation where the school still participates.
  - *Gotchas:* The $30 in credits is modest — the real value is Skill Builder + exam voucher; to redeem into a standalone AWS account, AWS may still ask for a credit card on file; do NOT plan a project around Educate credits that no longer exist; legacy 12-month and pre-Jul-2025 free tier limits are gone — use the Free Tier credit model instead.
  - *Source:* https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/aws/aws-student-rewards-free-cloud-ai-training
- **CloudFront Free plan (CDN/Uptime front)** (high) — CloudFront now ships a standing $0/month 'Free' plan tier (alongside Pro/Business/Premium/Custom): 100 GB of data transfer out per month + 1M requests/month, 'no overage charges', 5 GB of S3 Standard storage credits, default WAF/DDoS/TLS with 5 WAF rules. This is the successor presentation of the classic 'always-free 100 GB/month CloudFront' allowance. Missing from Free tier: Uptime SLA, logging, bot management, advanced DDoS, private origins, origin failover, high-speed origin routing, and >5 WAF rules.
  - *Eligibility:* All AWS customers indefinitely (standing plan tier, not a trial or 12-month offer); no .edu or startup status; no credit card required for most sign-ups; same region/policy constraints as standard CloudFront.
  - *How to claim:* Create a CloudFront distribution and leave it on the Free plan (default); upgrade to Pro/Business/Premium/Custom when you need the missing features — overage does not exist on Free, you simply upgrade.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite; no sunset date. 'May not be combined with other offers, promotions, or discounts.'
  - *Gotchas:* No Uptime SLA or CloudWatch access logs on Free (your monitoring/dashboards that rely on them lose data — relevant to the Uptime & CDN fronts); 5-WAF-rule cap; the S3 'credit' is a 5 GB storage allotment, not $ value; if your distribution outgrows 100 GB you must move to a paid plan, which has its own egress pricing.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/cloudfront/pricing/
- **AWS Academy (accredited-institution education license)** (medium) — For AWS-accredited universities/institutions: free instructor-led curriculum (Cloud Foundations, ML Foundations; Associate level adds Cloud Development/Architecting/Cloud Operations/Data Analytics), free lab environments, a 50% discount exam voucher for AWS certification exams for students, and labs provisioned with up to US$100 of AWS credits per student per lab (budget provided by the institution, not a universal student grant).
  - *Eligibility:* Students and faculty at AWS-accredited institutions; faculty must complete AWS Academy instructor accreditation for Associate-level courses; no individual self-serve enrollment — it is an institution license, so eligibility is effectively 'your school must be an AWS Academy partner.'
  - *How to claim:* Institution becomes AWS Academy accredited; faculty enroll students into class; students access learning + lab environment with institution-provisioned credits.
  - *Expiry:* Institution license term; the 50% exam voucher is one-time per license grant; lab credits refresh per lab as provisioned.
  - *Gotchas:* Not a universal student offer — if your institution is not accredited, there is no personal grant; credits are per-lab, not a standing balance; exam discount is 50%, not free.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/training/awsacademy/
- **AWS Education Equity Initiative (education-focused orgs)** (medium) — Part of a $100M commitment of AWS technology: edtech companies, non-profits, governments and CSR teams building digital learning solutions for underserved learners can apply for up to $10,000 USD in AWS credits plus technical advising from AWS Solutions Architects (advice/demos only — no build service). Applications reviewed on a rolling basis, responses ~3 weeks; typically each organization is supported once.
  - *Eligibility:* The ORGANIZATION applying (edtech/startup/non-profit/government), not individual students; pro-social enterprises welcome; new-to-AWS orgs self-serve the application; existing customers with an Account Manager go through the AM.
  - *How to claim:* Submit the application form (or ask your AM), describe underserved audience + quantified reach + proposed solution; credits + SA advising issued on award.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing program; credits are standard AWS promotional credits (subject to AWS Promotional Credit T&Cs).
  - *Gotchas:* Not monitoring/observability-specific and not a student offer — included only because it sits under the education front; advising is guidance, not implementation; 'we typically support organizations once.'
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/our-impact/education-equity-initiative/
- **AWS Free Plan (credit-based Free Tier, 2025 revamp) — up to $200 in trial credits, 6 months** (high) — New customers get $100 in AWS credits immediately on signup, plus up to $100 more earned by completing 'Explore' activities in key services (Explore AWS widget); total up to $200 usable over up to 6 months. Covers access to 90+ services on the Free plan for up to 6 months (the start-here page says 100+ services, incl. EC2, S3, DynamoDB, Lambda, Bedrock, SageMaker AI). Going beyond always-free monthly limits or using paid features is auto-covered by remaining credits. No charges unless you convert to the Paid plan. New customers on the Paid plan are also eligible for up to $200 in credits; the Paid plan offers limited free trials of selected services and a monthly spend limit starting at $20 (alerts at 50/75/90%, workload paused at limit).
  - *Eligibility:* All new customers; NOT eligible if you have ever had an AWS account before. No credit card required to start for most new customers (AWS may run a fraud check that places a USD $1 hold for 3-5 days, no charge). Region limits: credits not available in China (ZHY) and China (BJS) or in AWS GovCloud (US) except certain services (e.g., Lambda for GovCloud). Credits apply across global regions, calculated monthly in USD.
  - *How to claim:* Create a new AWS account (Fast sign-up with Google/GitHub/Apple/Amazon login creates an 'AWS Builder ID' personal profile; or advanced account creation for region control) at aws.amazon.com/free or signin.aws.amazon.com/signup?request_type=builderId — Free plan auto-enrolled, $100 credit lands in the billing console; earn the second $100 within 6 months by completing Explore activities (may take up to 30 minutes to appear).
  - *Expiry:* Free plan lasts the EARLIER of 6 months from account creation or credit exhaustion; account then auto-closes, cannot be extended. On expiry AWS suspends the account; data retained 90 days, then permanently erased. You can upgrade to Paid within the 90-day window; remaining credits roll onto Paid bills (FAQ states they expire 12 months after account creation, while the Advanced-Features section states 6 months — treat as 6-12 months, check billing console). No downgrade back to Free plan.
  - *Gotchas:* Free plan is a LIMITED subset of services (Bedrock AgentCore is Paid-plan exclusive) and excludes services that would immediately consume the whole credit amount or require hardware purchases. Free-plan accounts are INELIGIBLE for promotional/incentive credits (e.g., AWS Activate) — startups need a Paid-plan account. Joining an AWS Organization/Control Tower or activating Advanced Features (multi-region, enterprise services) makes you ineligible to earn extra Explore credits and credits then expire 6 months after account creation. Credits are not transferable. GPU/ML note: no GPU-specific free allotment; GPU instances (EC2 P-series, SageMaker GPU notebooks) are payable with the $200 credits rather than always-free.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/free-tier-faqs/
- **Always Free layer — 30+ services free within monthly usage limits, indefinite** (high) — Separate from the 6-month credit-based Free plan: '30+ AWS services are always free within monthly usage limits on both the Free and Paid plans.' Applies to ALL customers (new and existing), no application. If usage exceeds the monthly limits, remaining Free Tier credits automatically cover the cost (on Free plan). The hub page (aws.amazon.com/free) lists flagship services (EC2, S3, Aurora, RDS, DynamoDB, AWS Transform, Bedrock, SageMaker AI) as available on both plans but does NOT publish the per-service monthly quota numbers on the page fetched today; per-service limits live on each product pricing page.
  - *Eligibility:* All AWS customers on Free or Paid plan; not restricted by region except the same China/GovCloud caveats as the Free plan.
  - *How to claim:* Automatic — no signup step beyond having an AWS account; available on aws.amazon.com/free.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite; no renewal, no sunset date published.
  - *Gotchas:* Quota figures (hours/GB/requests) are not on the free-plan hub; verify per-service numbers on each service pricing page before relying on a specific limit. Egress/data transfer beyond limits and any charged feature draws on credits (Free plan) or bill (Paid plan).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **Amazon SageMaker free notebook allowance (ML notebooks front) — 250 hours sc.t3.medium, first 2 months** (high) — Per the SageMaker pricing page (SageMaker Unified Studio era): AWS provides 250 hours of sc.t3.medium notebook instances during the FIRST 2 MONTHS of a new account, for Amazon SageMaker notebooks. Also always-free monthly items for the SageMaker Catalog (built on DataZone): 20 MB metadata storage, 4,000 API requests, and 0.2 compute units per account per billing month; plus 'always-free' core Unified Studio requests (IdC-based domain setup, user/project management, policy grants). SageMaker Data Agent (new) is pay-as-you-go at $0.04 per credit, no free allowance. Non-GPU only (sc.t3.medium); GPU notebook/training instances (p3/p4/p5, Trainium) are paid and can be covered by Free Plan credits or Activate credits.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS account, first 2 months only; available to Free and Paid plans (notebook hours) per the pricing page; notebook service 'honors your current AWS Free Tier allocations'.
  - *How to claim:* Create a SageMaker notebook (Unified Studio) on a new account within the first 2 months; the 250 sc.t3.medium hours apply automatically; no separate application.
  - *Expiry:* 250-hour notebook allowance: first 2 months of account, no renewal. Catalog always-free allowances: recurring monthly, indefinite.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a material shrink vs the legacy free tier's 750 hours/month of ml.t3.medium for 12 months (old figure from the pre-revamp page, not re-verified today). Storage (S3), EKS clusters, endpoints, and GPU instances cost extra; SageMaker usage through Unified Studio is billed per underlying service. Data/prep jobs are not listed as free in the section fetched.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/pricing/
- **AWS Activate (AWS for Startups) credits — Founders up to $5,000, Portfolio up to $200,000, AI startups $200,000+** (high) — Three tiers on the current credits page: (1) Activate Founders (self-funded/bootstrapped): start with $1,000 in credits, earn up to $5,000 total; apply directly. (2) Activate Portfolio (VC/accelerator/angel-group backed, use the provider's Organization ID): up to $200,000 in credits. (3) AWS Credits for AI Startups: $200,000+ for 'AI startups ready to scale', invite-only, via your AWS account manager. All credits in USD, redeemable on AWS services including third-party models on Amazon Bedrock. Beyond credits: mentorship (ex-founders, CTOs, VCs, angels), technical/business guidance, co-build/co-market/co-sell, AWS Marketplace listing, and the new 'AWS Startup Advisor' (IDE-integrated cost tracking and credit applications). The separate offers page still lists the program as '$1,000 to $100,000' plus '80+ exclusive third-party offers valued at up to $800,000' — figures now inconsistent with the credits page (up to $200,000).
  - *Eligibility:* Founded in the last 10 years; pre-Series B stage (offers page says funded up to Series A); company website required; needs an AWS account ON THE PAID plan; must be new to Activate credits or requesting more than previously received. Apply with a Builder ID using a professional (company) email. No funding-amount cap is printed; no credit-card detail stated (Paid plan implies a payment method).
  - *How to claim:* 4 steps from aws.amazon.com/startups/credits: 1) create AWS Builder ID with professional email; 2) pick tier — Founders (self-funded, apply directly) or Portfolio (with provider's Activate Org ID); 3) provide product, funding-stage, company details; 4) link an AWS Paid-plan account and submit. Response within 5-10 business days (credits page; offers page says 7-10).
  - *Expiry:* No renewal mechanism published; credits are one-time grants (duration-of-use window not stated on page fetched today).
  - *Gotchas:* Free-plan accounts are INELIGIBLE for Activate (FAQ confirms Free plan excludes promotional/incentive credits) — you need a Paid account, i.e., the Free Plan's 6-month account that auto-closes is the wrong vehicle; startups should stay on Paid. Kiro Startup Credits are mutually exclusive with active Activate credits. The '/free/offers' page figures ($1K-$100K, 7-10 days) look stale versus the dedicated credits page ($5K/$200K, 5-10 days) — trust the credits page.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **Kiro Startup Credits (separate, non-Activate track, 2025/2026 new)** (high) — Up to one year of Kiro Pro+ credits (Kiro is AWS's agentic IDE) delivered in three tiers: Starter — up to 2 Kiro users; Growth — up to 10; Scale — up to 30. Credits are automatically added to the linked AWS account on approval. Not AWS infrastructure credits — covers Kiro Pro+ subscription only.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage through Series A startups, global, but EXCLUDED: Argentina, Belarus, Brazil, China/GCR, Cuba, France, Germany, Iran, Italy, Mexico, North Korea, Poland, Russia, Spain, Syria, Crimea, DNR, LNR, UAE. Requires a business email matching the startup's domain and an AWS Account ID tied to that email. Startups with active AWS Activate Credits are excluded (Activate credits can still be spent on Kiro separately).
  - *How to claim:* Submit the form at kiro.dev/startups (or the AWS Startups Kiro credits page); reviewed on rolling basis, follow-up email; one application at a time per startup; redeem via Kiro using AWS IAM Identity Center sign-in (not Builder ID or social login).
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire one year after issuance to the AWS account. Not refundable, re-issuable, transferable, or sellable; shareable across authorized users of associated accounts in the same company. Once depleted, standard Kiro Pro+ billing applies.
