  - *Gotchas:* Only relevant if you build in Kiro; does not fund GPU/ML compute. Heavy region exclusions (many of the EU, LatAm, ex-USSR).
  - *Source:* https://kiro.dev/startups/
- **AWS Educate (education license / student path) — free digital training and credentials, no .edu email required** (high) — Free, curated learning paths and hands-on labs (starter labs in Storage, Compute, Networking, Databases, Cloud Operations); earn shareable digital badges (Credly network). Job board access for learners 18+. Recruiter accounts for employers to source talent. No institutional enrollment needed: 'open to any individual'.
  - *Eligibility:* Any individual — NO .edu email required; learners as young as 13 can register with just an email address; 'No credit card needed'.
  - *How to claim:* Register at aws.amazon.com/education/awseducate with any email address; take labs; earn digital badges. No application or license to claim.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite; no renewal or sunset noted. (A historical faculty/classroom-licensing front: aws.amazon.com/education/awsclassroom returns 404 — no public classroom-license page found today; institutions are instead directed to contact AWS for 'special pricing for education customers', i.e., not a public free offer.)
  - *Gotchas:* This is learning content, not compute: no GPU/ML training credits; no AWS account credits. The education landing page is high-level; program details live on the awseducate page. AWS Academy/partner-institution programs are separate and not free-publicly.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/education/awseducate/
- **AWS Free Account: up to $200 in Free Tier credits covering Amazon SES (new signup experience, live ~Oct 2026)** (high) — New AWS accounts get a credit-based free tier: $100 in credits immediately at sign-up, plus up to $100 more for completing 'Explore AWS' activities = up to $200 total. Credits are redeemable on eligible services, explicitly including Amazon SES (replaces the old per-service SES allowance of 3,000 emails/month). Sign-up is a choice between a Free plan and a Paid plan; Free plan lasts 6 months after account creation and the account auto-closes '6 months after you open it or when your credits run out, whichever comes first'. On the Free plan, 'no charges incurred unless you upgrade to a Paid plan or activate paid-only services'. Base credits expire 6 months from account creation; if you upgrade to Paid, remaining credits keep applying to bills and expire 12 months from account creation. After the Free plan ends, AWS retains your data 90 days before deletion. A separate 'always free' bucket of 30+ services with monthly limits continues on both plans, with credits covering overages. A payment method is NOT required for most new customers ('AWS may still ask for one to verify identity in some cases').
  - *Eligibility:* Any new AWS account under the new sign-up experience (rolled out October 2026). No credit card required in most cases; no .edu or startup status needed. No region limit stated on the credits themselves.
  - *How to claim:* Create a new AWS account at the new sign-up flow, choose the Free plan (or Paid and let credits apply to bills), and use SES/SMS/push messaging within the $100-$200 credit envelope; upgrade to Paid if you want to continue past 6 months. Credits apply automatically to new eligible charges.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan: 6 months from account creation (account auto-closes). Base credits: 6 months; credits on Paid plan: 12 months from account creation. Data retained 90 days post-closure.
  - *Gotchas:* The old per-service 12-month free tiers (incl. SES 3,000 emails/month) are no longer listed on the official pages — they were replaced by this credit model. Free Plan auto-CLOSES the account, so anything not migrated by month 6 is gone after the 90-day data retention. Credits apply to new eligible charges only, not retroactively.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/andhttps://aws.amazon.com/free/faqs/
- **Amazon SES pricing/plan model (2025-2026): new accounts default to 'Essentials' plan from July 21, 2026; à-la-carte $0.10/1,000 emails** (high) — SES pricing is 'per account per region per month'. À-la-carte outbound email is $0.10 per 1,000 emails (matching the 'then $0.10/1K' figure) and inbound $0.10/1,000; attachment data $0.12/GB; dedicated IPs $24.95/IP/month; Mail Manager email processing $0.15/1,000. NEW: 'New SES accounts and account x region combinations with no metered SES activity since June 1, 2025 will start on the Essentials plan beginning July 21, 2026'. Plans: Essentials $0.16/1K (0-10M), no monthly fee; Pro $0.22/1K with $105/mo fee; Enterprise $0.23/1K with $500/mo fee (all with virtual deliverability manager; volume tiers drop to $0.14/$0.17/$0.18 at 10-100M and $0.11/$0.12/$0.13 above 100M). 'You can upgrade or switch to à-la-carte pricing at any time'; downgrades take effect at the next billing cycle.
  - *Eligibility:* All SES users in commercial regions (per-account-per-region pricing; GovCloud/sovereign clouds excluded from some features). No card/edu/startup requirement — a standard AWS account (which under the new model may need no card for 6 months on Free plan).
  - *How to claim:* Just start sending in the AWS console or via API; new/inactive accounts are put on Essentials by default from 2026-07-21 — if you want the cheaper $0.10/1K à-la-carte rate, switch to à-la-carte pricing in the console; Free Account credits (up to $200) offset early usage.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; pricing structure. Note the old 3,000 emails/month SES free allowance is no longer published on the pricing page — it is superseded by the $200 credit model and the Essentials/à-la-carte structure.
  - *Gotchas:* Gotcha: the default Essentials rate ($0.16/1K) is ~60% MORE than à-la-carte ($0.10/1K) — switch deliberately, downgrades lag a billing cycle. Extra metered costs alongside SES: S3 storage for message transfer, SNS notifications, CloudWatch custom metrics/dashboards. Regional price differences apply per account per region.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/ses/pricing/
- **Push notifications free tier: first 1,000,000 push notifications per month free (AWS End User Messaging)** (high) — On the AWS End User Messaging pricing page: 'You pay $0 for the first 1,000,000 push notifications that you send each month', then $0.000001 each. No time limit or region restriction is stated on the page (language is recurring 'each month'), implying a standing allowance for End User Messaging. This is the successor to Amazon Pinpoint's push channel: AWS 'will end support for Amazon Pinpoint on October 30, 2026'; console and Pinpoint resources (endpoints, segments, campaigns, journeys, analytics) end, while 'APIs related to SMS, voice, mobile push, OTP, and phone number validate are not impacted' and continue under AWS End User Messaging. (Legacy Pinpoint console also offered $0 for 1M pushes/month and $0 for first 100M events collected, first 5,000 targeted endpoints, first 15,000 in-app message requests.)
  - *Eligibility:* Any AWS customer sending push notifications through End User Messaging (post-Pinpoint APIs). No card/edu requirement beyond normal AWS account rules (new accounts can be card-free for 6 months on Free plan). No region limit stated.
  - *How to claim:* Use the End User Messaging mobile push API; the first 1M pushes/month are $0 automatically — nothing to redeem. Migration users: keep API usage (APNs/FCM/WNS) intact past 2026-10-30 since campaigns/journeys/console state in Pinpoint does NOT survive the sunset.
  - *Expiry:* Standing monthly allowance, no stated expiry. Hard date to act on: 2026-10-30 Pinpoint support ends — move off Pinpoint console/journeys before that.
  - *Gotchas:* Post-1M pushes cost $0.000001 each. Push is one channel of EUM; SMS/voice/email in the same app are billed separately (email via SES; no free SMS). Inbound/egress data and any EC2/S3 behind your app add cost.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/end-user-messaging/pricing/andhttps://aws.amazon.com/pinpoint/(deprecationnotice)
- **SMS (End User Messaging): no free SMS quota published — pay-per-message with carrier surcharges** (medium) — The End User Messaging pricing page lists NO free SMS/MMS/voice message allowance (unlike push's 1M free). SMS: 'price varies by destination country and, in some cases, carrier within a country' (e.g., Canada toll-free: $0.00581 AWS + $0.00705 carrier). MMS (US/Canada outbound): $0.02 base + $0.01 carrier. Voice (outbound): US $0.013/min, requires a dedicated voice-enabled long code. OTP: $0.045 per successful verification plus the SMS price. Phone Number Validate: $0.006/request. Add-ons: SMS Protect Monitor/Filter $0.01/message; Notify managed feature $0.030-$0.045/message volume-tiered plus channel fee; phone number leasing $1-$995/mo; short codes US setup $650 + $995/mo. The legacy '100 free SMS/month in US regions' SNS free tier is no longer published anywhere on the current End User Messaging/SNS pricing pages — likely absorbed into the $200 Free Account credit model.
  - *Eligibility:* Any End User Messaging customer. No free tier to claim; new accounts' up-to-$200 Free Account credits can offset early SMS spend. No card needed during the 6-month Free plan.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim for SMS specifically — usage is metered; if you need zero-cost SMS, rely on the up-to-$200 new-account credits or an AWS Activate credit grant.
  - *Expiry:* No free tier, so no expiry question; pricing is standing. Legacy 100-msg US free tier: no longer listed as of 2026-10-22.
  - *Gotchas:* High gotcha surface: carrier fees are passed through on top of AWS fees (so 'real' per-SMS cost is higher than the AWS number); promotional SMS in many markets is heavily regulated; OTP and Phone Number Validate are separate paid features; scaling past 'Notify' requires dedicated phone numbers + brand verification (US 10DLC etc.), which cost money.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/end-user-messaging/pricing/
- **Chat APIs (WhatsApp & RCS via End User Messaging): no free tier — metered with Meta/carrier surcharges** (high) — WhatsApp: AWS fee $0.005 per outbound message globally (except India Authentication/Utility at $0.002), $0.001 per inbound globally; WhatsApp voice calling $0.005/min outbound (India $0.003), $0.001/min inbound; Meta's separate message fees are added on top and vary by country/message type. RCS (US): Rich RCS outbound $0.007/segment AWS + $0.00494/segment carrier; Rich Media RCS $0.020/message AWS + $0.00949 carrier. 'Businesses are only charged when a message is successfully delivered.' No free messages for WhatsApp or RCS.
  - *Eligibility:* Any End User Messaging customer; no free tier to claim. No card requirement during the new-account Free plan window.
  - *How to claim:* Use EUM WhatsApp/RCS APIs; expect cost from message #1 unless you hold AWS credits (Free Account $200 or Activate grants).
  - *Expiry:* Standing metered pricing; no trial stated.
  - *Gotchas:* Double billing: AWS fee + Meta fee (WhatsApp) or AWS fee + carrier fee (RCS); 'only charged on successful delivery' cuts both ways for delivery-failure accounting.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/end-user-messaging/pricing/
- **AWS Activate Founders tier: $1,000 in AWS credits (up to $5,000 over time) for early-stage, self-funded startups** (high) — Founders tier (current as of the June 2026 applying guide): $1,000 in AWS Activate Credits; 'select startups may qualify for up to $5,000 over time'. Eligibility: new to AWS Activate Credits, self-funded, fully functioning company website, founded in the past 10 years. Credits expire within 1-2 years depending on package and are applied monthly to new eligible charges only (never retroactive). Processing 7-10 business days. Credits are redeemable across 200+ AWS services, including third-party foundation models on Amazon Bedrock (OpenAI, Mistral, Anthropic, Meta, etc.). No credit-card requirement mentioned for the program itself. Re-applying is allowed for a larger tier; you receive the difference up to a lifetime cap; submitted applications can't be edited (cancel + unlink + resubmit).
  - *Eligibility:* Startup founders (individuals applying on behalf of a company): self-funded (no institutional funding), company < 10 years old, working company website required, first-time Activate recipient, business email matching the startup domain. Applies to startups of any funding stage at or before Pre-Series B.
  - *How to claim:* Create/sign in to an AWS Builder ID (personal email) → complete one-time Activate profile (business email) → choose Founders tier → enter company + funding details → link your primary AWS account (root/admin) → verify in console → submit. Credits land on the linked AWS account and automatically offset SES/SMS/push spend.
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire 1-2 years from grant depending on package; no stated renewal — you can re-apply when you qualify for a bigger tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Self-funded only — a small funding round or accelerator affiliation pushes you to the Portfolio tier instead. Credits cover new usage only; pre-existing debt on the account is not credited. $5K 'over time' is discretionary/select.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/aws-startups/learn/applying-for-aws-activate-credits-a-step-by-step-guide/(plushttps://aws.amazon.com/startups/)
- **AWS Activate Portfolio tier: up to $200,000 in AWS credits for provider-backed startups (pre-Series B)** (high) — Portfolio tier: 'up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits'. Eligibility: associated with an AWS Activate Provider (accelerator/VC/ecosystem org, 'thousands worldwide') and hold their Organizational ID; Pre-Series B (most recent funding round within last 12 months, if funded); founded in the past 10 years; working company website; no prior AWS Activate Credits of equal or greater value. Beyond $200K, 'AI startups ready to grow may be eligible for additional credits' via an Account Manager. Credits redeemable on 200+ services incl. Bedrock third-party models; expire within 1-2 years; applied monthly to new eligible charges. Program stats: 350,000+ startups, $8B+ in promotional credits since 2013. Note: an older marketing page (startups.aws.com LP) still advertises 'up to $100,000' — the June 2026 guide and main page confirm $200,000 as the current ceiling; also a new 'Kiro credits' offer for startups exists (kiro.dev/startups).
  - *Eligibility:* Startup companies affiliated with an Activate Provider (Org ID), pre-Series B funding cap (most recent round <= 12 months old), < 10 years old, working website. Not for individuals or non-startup businesses; no .edu involved (student startups go through their university's provider agreement — see Educate finding).
  - *How to claim:* Obtain your provider's Org ID → AWS Builder ID + Activate profile (business email) → choose Portfolio tier → submit funding/company details → link primary AWS account → wait 7-10 business days; credits apply monthly to new eligible usage.
  - *Expiry:* Grant-to-expiry 1-2 years per package; re-application gives the difference vs previously held credits up to the lifetime cap.
  - *Gotchas:* The headline number moved from $100K to $200K (some pages stale); 'most recent round within last 12 months' is a hard timing rule; credits are new-charge-only; wrong/funding details = denial (inaccurate info results in denial).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/aws-startups/learn/applying-for-aws-activate-credits-a-step-by-step-guide/
- **AWS Educate: student program at partner institutions (credits ~$100/yr; some universities offer up to $5,000 for student startups)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — AWS Educate (successor to AWS Academy's classroom content) is offered through enrolled institutions: students get free self-paced courses, labs, and a promo credit (commonly ~$100/yr, redeemed by code in Billing & Cost Management -> Credits). Some university startup programs (e.g., AWS Activate partnerships at universities like Syracuse's Blackstone LaunchPad) extend student-startup eligibility to up to $5,000 in credits valid ~2 years plus 1 year of AWS Business Support. Note: the official Educate landing page could not be fetched today (404/DNS), so dollar amounts are from university-published pages updated in 2025; treat as approximate.
  - *Eligibility:* Enrolled students/faculty at a partner institution; the ~$5,000 startup path requires the university's Org ID and meeting Activate Portfolio-style requirements (website, funding stage). Verification via the institution's enrollment, not a .edu email alone.
  - *How to claim:* Register through your school's AWS Educate portal, complete the AWS Educate application, then redeem the emailed credit code in AWS Billing & Cost Management; for student startups, apply through the university's Activate provider page (Org ID) for the larger grant.
  - *Expiry:* Educate credits typically annual per-enrollment; university startup grants ~2 years. Verify current numbers with the school's page.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit codes are account-bound (redeem on the account you'll use, they don't transfer); some schools cap or restrict production use (learning-only policies, e.g., UC Berkeley's disclaimer that these are sandbox resources, not for launching a business).
  - *Source:* https://support.sou.edu/kb/articles/amazon-web-services-educate-program(institutionalreference;officialpagehttps://awsacademy.com/amazon-educate/wasunreachabletoday)
- **AWS Builder Center student rewards: free Skill Builder year + badge-earned credits, no credit card required** (high) — Verified students at accredited higher-education institutions (verified via SheerID with name, college name, and school email — .edu domain NOT strictly required) get: 1 year of AWS Skill Builder (900+ courses, labs, cert exam prep, game-based learning) just for completing a profile; badge-based rewards — 7 badges = $10 AWS credits, 14 badges = $20 AWS credits, 21 badges = $100 AWS Certification voucher; plus free workshops/courses/community, explicitly 'No credit card'. Student Builder Groups (student-led campus communities) also exist.
  - *Eligibility:* Students (or staff) of an accredited higher-education institution, verified by SheerID (most checks minutes; up to 24-48h with extra docs). No startup/funding rules.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in with an AWS Builder ID at builder.aws.com → complete SheerID student verification → complete profile (unlocks Skill Builder) → earn badges (articles, comments, streaks) to unlock the $10/$20 credits and $100 voucher. Credits apply to AWS usage (incl. messaging services) if you have/created an AWS account.
  - *Expiry:* Skill Builder benefit is 1 year; badge credits/voucher have no expiry stated on the page; program is ongoing.
  - *Gotchas:* Rewards are small ($10-$20 credits, $100 voucher) — meaningful but not a production budget; the cert voucher is a voucher, not exam free passage; no card needed but actual AWS production usage still requires a billing account.
  - *Source:* https://builder.aws.com/student-rewards
- **AWS Free Tier — Free Plan: up to $200 credits, 6 months, no charges** (high) — As of the July 15, 2025 revamp (current structure, verified today): new customers get $100 in credits immediately on sign-up and can earn up to $100 more (5 console activities worth $20 each: launch an EC2 instance, create an RDS database, build a Lambda web app, try a Bedrock foundation model, set an AWS Budgets cost budget). Free plan = access to 90+ select services for up to 6 months with no charges and no surprise overages; you cannot exceed limits — remaining credits auto-apply. Upgrade to Paid plan any time; on the old sign-up experience unused credits stay usable up to 12 months from sign-up, but under the new sign-up experience (launched Oct 2026) the FAQ states Free Tier credits expire 6 months from account creation. Free plan accounts auto-enroll at account creation.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS customers only (an existing or previously-had AWS account makes you ineligible). No .edu email or student status required — open to anyone worldwide. Credit card NOT required for most new customers (FAQ Q4: 'A payment method isn't required to sign up for most new customers. In some cases, we may request additional information, such as a payment method, to verify your identity'). NOT available in China (ZHY/Ningxia, BJS) regions or AWS GovCloud (US) regions (certain services such as Lambda excepted). Free plan + credits are NOT available through AWS Organizations: enrolling a Free plan account in Organizations upgrades it to Paid, and an Organization can benefit from offers via only one account (usage aggregated).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an AWS account, select Free plan (default). 2) $100 credit appears on the Credits page (up to 10 minutes to show). 3) Complete the five 'Explore AWS' activities in the AWS Management Console Home widget to earn up to $100 more. 4) Monitor balance/days remaining via the Cost & Usage widget or Settings Portal (new sign-up experience) / Credits page in Billing console (pre-Oct-2026 sign-ups). 5) Upgrade to Paid plan to continue after expiry.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan expires at the earlier of 6 months from account creation or credit exhaustion (cannot be extended). After expiry AWS suspends the account and resources; data is retained 90 days, then the account and all content are permanently erased unless upgraded within 90 days. Credits expire 6 months from account creation on the new sign-up experience (FAQ); 12 months per the July 2025 Free Tier Terms for earlier sign-ups.
  - *Gotchas:* Suspended after expiry — move data/projects before the 90-day window or lose them. Free plan is 'evaluation only' per terms (sensitive data processing disallowed). Credits can't be transferred to other accounts. AWS may reclaim resources after 3 months of inactivity with 30 days' notice. Unused monthly free amounts don't roll over. Activating paid-only services upgrades you to Paid plan (bills on pay-as-you-go beyond credits). EC2/RDS/S3/API Gateway on new accounts are no longer fixed monthly free allowances — they consume the $200 credit pool; new free-plan-eligible EC2 types: t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large (t2.micro is legacy only).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/free-tier-faqs/
- **AWS Free Tier — 30+ Always Free services (all customers, indefinite)** (high) — All AWS customers (new and existing, Free and Paid plans) get over 30 'Always Free' services with monthly usage caps; usage is aggregated across all regions; excess usage automatically applies Free Tier credits (Free plan) or bills at standard on-demand rates (Paid plan). Representative always-free caps (per service docs and community verification, not all published on the /free/ page itself): Lambda 1M requests + 400,000 GB-seconds; DynamoDB 25 GB + 25 provisioned RCU / 5 WCU; SQS 1M requests; SNS 1M publishes; CloudWatch 10 custom metrics + 10 alarms + 1M API requests; CloudFront 1 TB data transfer out.
  - *Eligibility:* Any AWS customer — no .edu, no student status, no credit card required for the account itself. Available in regions where AWS operates (Always Free offers are region-broader than the Free Plan credits, which exclude China and GovCloud).
  - *How to claim:* Automatic once you have an AWS account; no application.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry while you hold an AWS account; monthly limits reset each month with no rollover. AWS may terminate any offer at any time.
  - *Gotchas:* Data transfer out (egress) beyond CloudFront's 1 TB and inter-region traffic are billable; paid add-on features auto-bill on Paid plans; individual service caps vary — check each service's pricing page before heavy use. The per-service figures above are medium-confidence (sourced from the EC2 docs and builder-community summaries, since the /free/ page lists service tiles without caps).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Free Tier — short-term trials on selected Paid-plan services** (medium) — Selected Paid-plan services can be 'tried' with limited free trials; the trial begins when you activate the service, and any eligible Free Tier credits automatically apply to usage beyond trial limits. Some offers are limited in duration or free-usage amount; on a Free plan, activating a paid-only service upgrades the account to Paid plan.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS customers (per-service trials listed on aws.amazon.com/free/); no .edu required; same region caveats (Free Plan credits unavailable in China/GovCloud).
  - *How to claim:* Activate the trial service from the console; track the trial end date yourself — AWS does not always notify when a trial ends.
  - *Expiry:* Per-service trial windows; AWS may end any offer at any time.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs Always Free confusion: trial periods end and pay-as-you-go billing starts without a notification in some cases — delete trial resources before expiry. Egress/data-transfer and public IPv4 charges ($0.005/hr) commonly surprise trial users.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Educate — free self-paced curriculum, hands-on labs, badges, job board** (high) — Free, self-paced online cloud training 'designed for beginners': hundreds of hours of content incl. Getting Started with Storage (S3), Compute (EC2), Networking (VPC), Databases (RDS), and Cloud Operations + cost-estimation labs, a Cloud 101 pathway, and a Cloud Literacy interactive badge. Includes free hands-on labs in a sandboxed AWS Console environment (time-boxed, unlimited retries), shareable Credly digital badges, an invitation to the AWS Emerging Talent Community, and an AWS Educate Job Board (18+) with in-demand jobs and internships. No dollar-denominated AWS cloud credits — it is a learning program, not a credit grant.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to any individual regardless of education, experience, or career journey — NO .edu email or university affiliation required. Register with just an email address; no credit card needed. Minimum age: 'as young as 13' per the current page; the Educate FAQ says 14+ generally, with EU 16+; Algeria, Lebanon, Portugal 18+; not offered in China or Switzerland.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Register at the AWS Educate sign-up page (promo code 'EducateLP' referenced on the page) or sign in to AWS Educate. 2) Choose a learning path (Cloud 101 recommended for new-to-cloud). 3) Complete labs/courses. 4) Claim digital badges on Credly. 5) Job board access at 18+.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; content and lab time limits update over time; lab attempts are time-boxed but retries are unlimited.
  - *Gotchas:* Labs run on shared sandboxes, not your own AWS account (does not stack with your Free Tier account). No monetary credits. Employer direct-messaging on the job board is US-only per the FAQ. Age minimum varies by country (higher in EU and some regions than the '13+' marketing). No certification exam included — AWS certifications remain paid (see AWS Academy vouchers).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/education/awseducate/
- **AWS Skillbuilder — 500-600+ free self-paced digital training courses** (high) — AWS's Training FAQ: 'Self-paced digital training on AWS Skill Builder is free.' Free digital training includes 500+ on-demand courses in 17 languages (the /training/ page markets '600+ free digital courses'); free digital badges (knowledge, Cloud Quest) earned at >=80% on the associated assessment, claimable on Credly. Paid Individual/Team subscriptions add more content and labs; free webinars/Twitch training events also exist, while classroom training and certification exams cost money.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone — no .edu or student status; sign in with a free AWS Builder ID (or APN credentials / company Team subscription if an org member). No credit card.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Open AWS Skill Builder and sign in or create a free AWS Builder ID. 2) Enroll in any free digital course. 3) Score >=80% on the assessment to earn a badge. 4) Claim the badge via Credly.
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiry for free access; subscription content changes with plans.
  - *Gotchas:* Only part of Skillbuilder is free — advanced lab-heavy content is behind paid Individual/Team subscriptions; certification exams are paid (vouchers only via AWS Academy pathways); don't confuse free digital badges with paid AWS certification badges.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/training/faqs/
- **AWS Activate — Founders package: $1,000 credits (select participants up to $5,000)** (high) — For self-funded, early-stage startups new to AWS Activate Credits: $1,000 USD in AWS credits (per AWS's gaming-startups page: 'Start with $1,000 USD in Activate Credits. Select participants may qualify for additional credits up to $5,000'). Credits redeem on 200+ eligible AWS services incl. third-party foundation models on Amazon Bedrock. Also includes technical support, business mentorship, co-build/co-market/go-to-market resources, free pre-built cloud templates, AWS Startup Advisor, AWS Support Plans eligible for credits, and access to 80+ third-party offers. New customers also get the AWS Free Tier when creating a new account.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup program — NOT an education/.edu program (student-founded startups can apply as startups). Criteria: self-funded (or pre-Series B per AWS's June 2026 guide), founded in the past 10 years, a fully functioning company website or public company profile, new to Activate Credits (or requesting more than previously received), and a business email matching the company domain. No student-specific grant exists.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a personal AWS Builder ID with a professional email. 2) Complete the AWS Activate profile. 3) Choose the Founders package. 4) Submit company/product/funding details (accurate info required; inaccuracies can be denied). 5) Link an AWS account and submit; decision in 7-10 business days by email, trackable in the console.
  - *Expiry:* Credits are subject to the AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions (fixed validity from grant, not re-stated on these pages); not renewable at the same value without a larger request.
  - *Gotchas:* Without a functioning startup/website you won't qualify. AWS has final say; website professionalism is weighted heavily. Credits don't cover Marketplace third-party services, Route 53 domain registration/transfer, most support tiers, or Professional Services (except Bedrock third-party model charges). AWS offers generally not available in China regions.
  - *Source:* https://startups.aws.com/lp/aws-activate-credits
- **AWS Activate — Portfolio package: up to $200,000 credits (provider-backed)** (medium) — For startups affiliated with an AWS Activate Provider (accelerator, VC, incubator, or partner org — e.g., Y Combinator, a16z, nVIDIA, Carta): up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits per the main /startups/ page and AWS's June 4, 2026 step-by-step guide ('The Activate Portfolio tier offers up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits for provider-backed startups'); AI startups ready to scale can get additional credits beyond $200,000 via their Account Manager. Also: 80+ exclusive third-party offers/memberships valued up to $800,000, dedicated account managers, and solutions architects. Note: the /free/offers/ comparison page still lists the range as '$1,000 to $100,000' with eligibility 'funded up to and including Series A' — the $200k / pre-Series-B figures come from AWS's newer 2026 pages, indicating the cap and funding stage were raised in 2025-2026.
  - *Eligibility:* Must hold an Organizational ID from an AWS Activate Provider; pre-Series B (most recent funding round within the last 12 months, if applicable); founded in the past 10 years; a fully functioning company website; business email; must be new to Activate Credits or requesting more than previously received. Not open to direct application — provider affiliation is mandatory. No .edu/student path.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Enroll in a program with an AWS Activate Provider and get their Org ID/invitation. 2) Create an AWS Builder ID + complete the AWS Activate profile. 3) Choose the Portfolio package with your Org ID. 4) Submit details; link an AWS account; decision in 7-10 business days.
  - *Expiry:* Per the AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions (fixed validity from grant).
  - *Gotchas:* Provider gatekeeping (AWS has final say and gets pickier above $5,000). Official AWS pages conflict today on the cap: $100k on /free/offers/ vs $200k on /startups/ and the June 2026 guide — treat 'up to $200,000' as the current advertised max and verify at application. Legacy sub-tiers ($5k/$10k/$25k/$100k; YC's up to $250k) are no longer publicly listed.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/
- **AWS Academy — free participation for accredited higher-education institutions (+ exam vouchers)** (high) — AWS Academy 'provides higher education institutions with a free, ready-to-teach cloud computing curriculum' (aligned to AWS certifications) plus Learner Labs. FAQ: 'There is no cost to join.' Educators may request a 100% discount voucher on AWS certification exam cost; member institutions may request a 50% discount exam voucher that students redeem at 100% of the voucher's value; students in badge-eligible courses earn Emerging Talent Community points redeemable for rewards such as discounted AWS certification exam vouchers. This is AWS's closest university-scoped free offer.
  - *Eligibility:* Institutional, not individual: accredited higher-education institution offering degrees, diplomas, certificates, or continuing-ed programs, with an authorized Program Administrator or Central Point of Contact (CPOC). No .edu-email-only rule, but institutional membership is required; individual students/educators can't self-enroll.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Institution applies to join AWS Academy (or is added to an existing membership). 2) CPOC nominates educators; educators complete the mandatory Getting Started course. 3) Educators teach courses; students use Learner Labs. 4) Educators/students claim exam discount vouchers per program rules.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry while the institution remains a member; voucher/reward programs are 'subject to change' per the FAQ.
  - *Gotchas:* Vouchers discount exam fees only — they are not AWS usage credits. Institutional onboarding can be slow; the free value to students is mainly the curriculum, labs, and exam discounts — hands-on AWS usage still comes from the regular Free Tier (which needs no Academy affiliation).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/training/awsacademy/faq/
- **2025–26 change:** SUNSET / MATERIAL CHANGES 2024-2026: (1) The classic AWS 12-month free tier (EC2 750 hrs/mo, S3 5 GB, 1M input + 1M output free Bedrock model-invocation tokens per month for 12 months across ~75 models, RDS 750 hrs, etc.) has been SUPERSEDED by the new Free plan / Paid plan model: $100 signup credits + up to $100 earned, 6-month window, 30+ 'always free' services, and notably no payment method required for most new customers. The Bedrock-specific free-token docs page (docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/free-trial.html) now renders empty/removed, and the new Free Tier FAQ contains zero mention of Bedrock free tokens, 750 EC2 hours, or 5 GB S3 - legacy per-service allowances no longer exist. So the 'free Bedrock tokens for students' front is effectively dead; Bedrock access now comes via (a) Free plan $100-200 credits, (b) AWS Activate credits, or (c) Kiro credits for agentic coding. (2) June 2024: AWS doubled the portfolio/accelerate credit ceiling from $100,000 to $200,000 (announced July 1, 2024, for startups that raised Series A in the past 12 months; seed stage stayed at $100,000 at the time; credits' expiry moved from 1 year to 3 years - per CNBC 2024-06-28). The current credits page (2026) shows one Portfolio tier at up to $200,000. (3) 2026: the credits page was redesigned (awslabs/startups PR #75, June 2026) renaming tiers to Activate Founders ($1,000 start, up to $5,000 - NOT the ~$100 sometimes misreported; the $100 figure matches the new Free plan signup credit, not Activate), Activate Portfolio (up to $200,000), and adding the invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier ($200,000+) plus the new Kiro Startup Credits program. (4) 'Known so far' figures from the task (~$100K Portfolio, ~$100 Founders) are outdated: Portfolio is now up to $200K and Founders is $1,000-to-start/up-to-$5,000. No standalone student Bedrock/LLM free offer from AWS exists today - the closest student fronts are the no-card Kiro Student Offer (1,000 credits/month x 12 months) and AWS Academy (training only, no credits). Verification caveat: today's live fetches of aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/ and free sub-pages intermittently returned 403 via proxy, but credits-page content was captured today via the official page mirror in the awslabs/startups repo and search snippets of the live page; free-plan figures came directly from aws.amazon.com/free/ and /free/free-tier-faqs/ read today.</sunset_notes>
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes (all verified on official AWS pages fetched today, 2026-10-22; WebSearch budget was exhausted so only official-page fetches were used): (1) The legacy usage-based 12-month AWS Free Tier (750 RDS hours, 5 GB S3, etc.) was replaced by a credit-based model for accounts created on/after July 15, 2025 — $100 at sign-up + up to $100 earned, Free plan (6-month auto-closing account) vs Paid plan (12-month credit validity); a refreshed sign-up experience launched October 2026, and pre-October accounts still see the old credits console. The RDS pricing page still documents the legacy 750-hour/20 GB free database for pre-July-15-2025 accounts only. (2) Per-service 'Always Free' and legacy free-trial figures (e.g., Athena GB-per-month, Redshift RA3/RA2 1-node 12-month developer tier and 30-day standard tier, Glue 1M source-records ETL free allowance, Bedrock character-based free tiers) no longer appear on today's service pricing pages — the credit model absorbed them, so do not quote legacy numbers for new accounts. (3) Activate program structure is now three tiers: Founders ($1,000, up to $5,000), Portfolio (up to $200,000 via provider Org ID), and an invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier adding $200,000+ for AI startups scaling past the Portfolio ceiling. Credits now explicitly cover Amazon Bedrock third-party models (OpenAI, Mistral, Anthropic, Meta). Note a discrepancy: /free/offers/ still lists Activate as '$1,000 to $100,000' — likely stale vs the $200,000 ceiling on the credits page/FAQ. (4) New Kiro startup credits program (up to a year of Kiro Pro+, 2/10/30-user tiers, 'limited time only', excludes France/Germany/Italy/Spain/Poland/UAE and ~13 other countries). (5) AWS Academy / Cloud Career Academies: no working page found on today's main AWS site (URLs 404; cloudcareeracademies.org DNS-failed) — status unconfirmed; AWS Educate (free, 13+, no credits/vouchers) is explicitly still active with a 2026 copyright. Also of note: AWS Educate bundles no cloud credits and no free exam vouchers on any page I could read today.
- **2025–26 change:** (1) AWS Free Tier revamp: the classic structure - 12 months of Free Trial on ~75 services plus a separate 'Always Free' tier - is no longer on the official page; it has been replaced (2025-2026) by the Free plan / Paid plan model with up to $200 in credits, 90+ services for up to 6 months, and '30+ services always free within monthly usage limits'. The exact launch date of the revamp could not be confirmed (web-search budget exhausted mid-session); the page read today (2026-10-22) shows only the new structure. (2) AWS Activate: the legacy 'standard program up to $1,000 in credits' copy no longer appears on the live portal; the join page now says 'Apply for up to $200,000 in Activate Credits' while the application widget caps at 'up to $100,000' - consistent with a headline increase beyond the old $100K Accelerate ceiling, unconfirmed via a dated press release. (3) AWS Impact Accelerator, the US-only predecessor (part of AWS's 2022 $30M commitment to Black, Women, Latino and LGBTQIA+ founders), was superseded by the global AWS Impact Bootcamps announced Jul 30, 2025; the 2022-2023 US cohorts are closed. (4) All AWS Impact Bootcamps application windows through the 2026 Tokyo and Berlin cohorts are closed (deadlines Feb 27 and Mar 13, 2026), so as of 2026-10-22 the $5,000 credits, $50K grant and $15K credits are only obtainable in future, not-yet-announced cohorts. (5) Research caveat: https://startups.aws.com/lp/aws-impact-bootcamps currently returns HTTP 500 at the server level (its Next.js content payload is intact and was extracted from the raw HTML); the shared WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted, so 'was this changed in 2025-2026' checks rely on today's live pages rather than dated announcements.
- **2025–26 change:** Key 2025-2026 changes: (1) 2025-07-15 cutover - AWS replaced the legacy 12-month service-level Free Tier with a credit-based model: new accounts get a 6-month 'Free plan' ($100 signup credits + up to $100 service-exploration credits = up to $200) or a Paid plan; accounts created before 2025-07-15 are grandfathered on the old 12-month per-service free tier (e.g. RDS: 750 hours/month single-AZ, 20 GB gp2 storage, 20 GB backup). (2) Free-plan accounts auto-close at 6 months or when credits run out; data retained 90 days, then permanently erased. (3) Startup program restructuring: the /startups/credits/ page now lists 'Activate Founders' (up to $5,000; starts at $1,000) and 'Activate Portfolio' (up to $200,000, was the $150,000 Founders' Program era ceiling; the previous 'Accelerate' up to $100,000 tier name no longer appears) plus two newer tracks - 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' ($200,000+, invite-only post-Portfolio) and 'Kiro Startup Credits'. The exact 2025-2026 rename/retirement dates could not be confirmed: WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted, so only the current official pages could be verified. (4) No .edu-gated student dollar-credit license was found: the education front is AWS Educate (free training/badges, no credits) and institutional 'special pricing' programs; AWS Academy pages returned 404 on fetched sub-paths, so its current status/offerings are unverified rather than confirmed active or sunsetting. (5) Free-plan credits are unavailable in China regions or AWS GovCloud (US); the Free plan also excludes all promotional credits (Activate), so startup credits effectively require a Paid-plan account.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) July 15, 2025 - the classic 12-month per-service Free Tier was replaced for NEW accounts with a credit-based model: $100 sign-up credit + up to $100 earned via 5 console activities, a 6-month Free plan window (account closes at 6 months or when credits run out), 90+ services, and 30+ always-free services; accounts created before July 15, 2025 retain the legacy 12-month tier. Free-tier-eligible EC2 instances changed too (t2.micro dropped; t3.small, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large added). (2) Short-term free trials are now paid-plan-only; the Free plan only gets 'Always Free' offers. (3) Feb 21, 2023 - AWS Educate stopped its old monetary grants ($35/$100 student grants, $75/$200 educator grants); educator tooling moved to AWS Academy. (4) Feb 16, 2026 - Free plan (Free Tier) accounts became ineligible for promotional credits, which affects AWS Cloud Credit for Research and similar programs (a Paid plan is required to hold awards). (5) Aug 20, 2026 - AWS launched Student Rewards on AWS Builder Center as part of a $500M+ student commitment; the old 'AWS Activate for Students' style offer no longer appears on aws.amazon.com - student benefits are now Builder Center Student Rewards + AWS Educate + AWS Academy. (6) October 2026 - new AWS sign-up experience launched; credit-expiry display moved to the Settings Portal (accounts that signed up before Oct 2026 still use the Credits page in the Billing console). No free offer listed above was fully sunset as of 2026-10-22.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) July 15, 2025 — AWS replaced the long-standing 12-month Free Tier (750 hrs/mo t2.micro/t3.micro + 30 GB EBS + 100 GB egress, 30+ always-free services) with a credit-based model for NEW accounts: $100 sign-up credits + up to $100 earned = up to $200, with a 6-month no-cost Free plan that auto-closes. Legacy 12-month benefits remain ONLY for accounts created before July 15, 2025. Also, since the revamp, a credit card/payment method is no longer required to sign up for most new customers (previously mandatory). A new sign-up experience (Settings Portal credit tracking) launched October 2026. (2) June 2026 — AWS Activate Portfolio credit ceiling doubled from $100,000 to $200,000; the Founders tier was standardized at $1,000 with an 'up to $5,000' extension for select participants; a new invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier (>$200K) was introduced (confirmed by the redesigned aws.amazon.com/startups/credits page © 2026 and the awslabs/startups repo content update June 16, 2026). Note: CNBC's June 28, 2024 report that Series A startups got $200K from July 1, 2024 was NOT reflected in 2025 official materials (which still said 'up to $100,000'); the cap only officially moved to $200K in June 2026. (3) April 2025 — AWS launched the $30M Impact Accelerator (new program, not a sunset). (4) No VPS/bare-metal/dedicated specific free offer was sunset, because none existed as a standalone front: bare-metal and dedicated hosts have never had free tiers and remain credit-eligible under Activate/Free Tier credits only. (5) GAIA 2025 cohort status and Impact Accelerator 2026 track openings could not be re-verified this session (shared web-search budget exhausted); confidence ratings above reflect that.
- **2025–26 change:** Sunset/changed in 2025-2026: (1) 15 Jul 2025 (announced AWS Summit NY, 15-16 Jul 2025): the 12-month Free Tier was retired for NEW accounts and replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 signup credit + up to $100 explore credits, 6-month auto-closing plan, 90-day post-expiry data retention); pre-cutoff accounts keep their legacy 12-month offers until each one's own expiry. (2) EC2 free-VPS offer sunset: t2.micro (and hours-based t3.micro 750 h/mo) free tiers no longer apply to new accounts; new free-tier-eligible EC2 types are t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large, consumed via credits. (3) AWS Activate was folded into AWS for Startups: /aws.amazon.com/activate/ now serves the startups program (startups.aws.com); headline credit figure is 'up to $200,000' with published tiers $1,000-$100,000, 80+ partner offers valued up to $800,000, plus undisclosed extra AI-startup credits and Kiro credits — replacing older multi-year $1M total caps and old student/faculty Activate tiers (up to $100k/$150k) which no longer appear on official pages. (4) The Free Plan explicitly cannot combine with any other promotional/incentive offer, and joining AWS Organizations/Control Tower auto-upgrades to Paid and voids remaining explore credits. (5) AWS signals a new sign-up experience in October 2026 (FAQ note); Free Tier Terms last updated 9 Jul 2025. Note: session web-search budget was exhausted, so 2025-2026 change verification relied on WebFetch of official AWS pages (free/, free/offers/, free/free-tier-faqs/, free/terms/, startups/, education/, EC2 docs, Lambda pricing, RDS free page, what's-new Jul 2025 entry, AWS blog 15 Jul 2025) rather than broader search.
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025-2026 changes (all verified against AWS pages today, 2026-10-22): (1) Jul 15, 2025 — the legacy "12 months free" tier was replaced for NEW accounts by a credit-based Free Tier: $100 sign-up credit + up to $100 earned, 6-month Free plan; every legacy 12-month offer on pre-Jul-15-2025 accounts (including legacy CloudWatch 12-month items) has now fully lapsed. (2) Oct 2026 — AWS launched a new sign-up experience: social sign-in (Google/GitHub/Apple/Amazon), no credit card needed for most customers, and credit tracking moved from the Billing console to a Settings Portal for accounts signed up after the rollout; the Free Tier FAQ now states base credits expire 6 months (vs. 12 months in the Jul 9, 2025 Terms) — a live contradiction AWS hasn't harmonized, so treat 6 months as the safe planning horizon. (3) Dec 3, 2025 — Kiro Startup Credits v1 launched (up to 100 users, pre-seed to Series B, apply by Dec 31, 2025) and closed; relaunched Apr 7, 2026 with 2/10/30-user tiers, early-stage to Series A (VC-backed per the current promotion), apply by Dec 31, 2026; startups with active AWS Activate credits are excluded from the Kiro promo. (4) Jun 2024 (context) — AWS doubled Portfolio credits $100K to $200K and extended expiry 1 year to 3 years; still the structure in effect. (5) Feb 2023 (context) — AWS Educate stopped issuing universal/institutional credit grants; it is now free learning only, and student credits now flow through Builder Center Student Rewards (up to $579 incl. $30 credits + $100 exam voucher, launched Aug 2026). (6) CloudFront's classic "100 GB/month always-free" line is now presented as a standing $0 "Free" plan tier with 100 GB + 1M requests + 5 GB S3 storage credits and no overage charges — not sunset, but restructured. No CloudWatch/observability offer was sunset; the known limits (10 metrics, 1M API requests, 5 GB logs, 100 canaries, 3 dashboards, 10 alarms, 100K X-Ray traces) all still apply as ALWAYS-FREE allowances. There is no CloudWatch-specific startup or student grant beyond the general AWS Free Tier credits and Activate credits, which can offset CloudWatch usage on a Paid plan.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes verified on official pages today (2026-10-22): (1) The legacy 12-month Free Tier is gone — the old 'free for 12 months' language no longer appears on aws.amazon.com/free; it has been replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 signup + up to $100 explore = up to $200 over 6 months), after which the account auto-closes (data kept 90 days, then erased). (2) The classic 'Always Free' list was narrowed in messaging to '30+ services' with monthly limits (legacy tier had 60+ services); per-service quota numbers are no longer published on the hub. (3) SageMaker notebook free allowance shrunk to 250 hours of sc.t3.medium for the first 2 months (vs legacy 750 hours/month ml.t3.medium for 12 months). (4) AWS Activate tiers renamed/revalued: 'Activate Community' no longer appears on the current credits page — it is now 'Activate Founders' (up to $5,000, start $1,000) alongside 'Activate Portfolio' (now up to $200,000, previously up to $100,000) plus a new invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' ($200,000+); a new Kiro Startup Credits track (up to 1 year of Kiro Pro+, 1/10/30 users) was added. The stale /free/offers page still shows old $1,000-$100,000 figures and 7-10 day processing, inconsistent with the current credits page ($5,000/$200,000, 5-10 days) — treat the credits page as authoritative. (5) WebSearch was unavailable during this run, so no third-party press confirmation of announcement dates was possible; all claims above are from the official AWS pages fetched today.
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Amazon Pinpoint sunset (HARD DATE): AWS ends support for Amazon Pinpoint on October 30, 2026 — console access and all Pinpoint resources (endpoints, segments, campaigns, journeys, analytics) terminate that day; only the messaging-channel APIs (SMS, voice, mobile push, OTP, phone number validate) continue under the rebranded "AWS End User Messaging" service. Anyone relying on Pinpoint journeys/campaigns must migrate before 2026-10-30. 2) Legacy per-service Free Tier replaced: the old model — e.g., "SES: 3,000 emails/month free for the first 12 months, then $0.10/1K" and "SNS: 100 SMS/month free, US regions" — is no longer published; as of the October 2026 sign-up experience, new accounts instead get up to $200 in Free Tier credits over 6 months (Free plan auto-closes at 6 months or when credits run out). The "then $0.10/1K" figure survives, but as the à-la-carte SES price, while NEW SES accounts default to the more expensive Essentials plan ($0.16/1K) starting July 21, 2026. 3) The formerly permanent "3,000 emails/month free (US regions, no purchase of sending capacity)" SES allowance is not listed on today's official pricing page — treat it as superseded by the credit model for new accounts; legacy account behavior not verifiable (docs pages didn't render). 4) The legacy 100-free-SMS/month US-regions tier also no longer appears on End User Messaging pricing; SMS is now fully pay-per-message. 5) AWS Activate credit ceiling was raised to $200,000 (some pages still show the old $100,000 figure — a 2025-2026 program change, not a sunset).
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes (verified against official AWS pages on 2026-10-22): (1) Classic 12-month Free Tier sunset for NEW accounts on July 15, 2025 — replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 + up to $100 earned = up to $200 over 6 months, auto-enrolled, no charges); accounts created before July 15, 2025 remain on legacy 12-month terms (750 hrs/mo t2/t3.micro etc.); legacy terms still published. (2) October 2026: AWS launched a new sign-up experience; the current FAQ now states Free Tier credits expire 6 months from account creation (vs 12 months under the July 2025 Free Tier Terms) and moved credit tracking to the Settings Portal — AWS's own FAQ is internally inconsistent between sections (6 vs 12 months), so treat credit expiry on the new experience as 6 months and verify per-account in the Settings Portal. (3) AWS Activate restructured in 2025-2026: old tiers (Starter $5k / Portfolio up to $100k / YC up to $250k / $5k-$100k sub-tiers) are no longer publicly listed; current structure is Founders ($1,000, up to $5,000 for select participants) + Portfolio (up to $200,000, cap raised from $100,000), with eligibility shifted from 'funded up to Series A' to 'pre-Series B, most recent funding round within the last 12 months', and the application flow now goes through the AWS Builder ID. AWS pages are inconsistent today: /free/offers/ still advertises '$1,000 to $100,000' while /startups/ and the June 4, 2026 AWS blog advertise 'up to $200,000'. (4) No education-only (.edu/student-email) free credits exist at AWS: no free offer requires a .edu or student email — AWS Educate is open to anyone 13/14+ with just an email, the Free Tier is open to any new customer, and AWS Marketplace 'education pricing' is a paid discount, not free. The only university-scoped free offer is AWS Academy (free institution participation + exam vouchers). (5) Free Plan/credits unavailable in China (ZHY, BJS) and GovCloud (US) regions, and not usable via AWS Organizations (auto-upgrades to Paid plan). (6) AWS Educate availability excludes China and Switzerland and raises minimum age in the EU (16) and Algeria/Lebanon/Portugal (18).
- **Critic correction** (AWS Activate (Amazon Bedrock)): Figures are out of date. The Activate Founders self-serve package is $1,000 (select participants can reach $5,000), not about $100. The Portfolio package, for startups tied to an Activate Provider with an Org ID, is now up to $200,000; it was $100,000 before June 2026. Credits can be used on Bedrock third-party models. AI startups ready to scale can talk to an account manager about more.
- **Critic correction** (AWS Startups): Range is outdated. Current tiers are Activate Founders at $1,000 (up to $5,000 for select startups) and Activate Portfolio at up to $200,000 (raised from $100,000 in June 2026). The top of the range should read $200,000 for Portfolio, not $100,000+.
- **Critic correction** (Up to ~$100K in AWS credits (Portfolio/Partner tier; ~$100 Founders self-serve)): The current official maximum is up to $200,000 for the Portfolio tier (requires a provider Org ID; pre-Series B). The Founders (self-funded) tier starts at $1,000, with up to $5,000 for select participants, not about $100.

### Baseten

- **Starter credit for every new Baseten workspace (~$30, no application needed)** (medium) — Every new Baseten account gets a one-time starter credit of ~$30 applied automatically to the workspace's invoice before any payment method is charged; no redemption step. Credits cover Model API calls (per-token, e.g. DeepSeek/GLM/Kimi/gpt-oss open models) and dedicated GPU deployments/training (per-minute GPU billing). The default Basic/Startup plan is $0/month with pure pay-as-you-go: no platform fee, no minimums, no idle-time billing (scale-to-zero supported). Baseten's FAQ states verbatim "new Baseten accounts come with $30 of free credit so that you can start running models for free"; the current pricing page FAQ says only that new accounts "come with credits" without restating the figure. There is no perpetual free tier or free-model tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone who signs up — no .edu, no startup screening, no credit card required to sign up or receive the credit. One-time per workspace (spinning up a second workspace under the same account does not stack). No region limit stated. After the credit is exhausted, a payment method is required to keep deployments running (deployments are deactivated otherwise).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at app.baseten.co (email/Google/GitHub); 2) confirm the email; 3) create a workspace (name + region) — it is the billing boundary; 4) the starter credit is credited automatically, nothing to redeem; 5) check the balance in the billing dashboard; 6) add a payment method only if you want to continue past the ~$30.
  - *Expiry:* One-time grant, not renewable, no recurring monthly allowance; no published expiry date for the credit itself. Standing offer as of 2026 (last re-verified by third parties in Sep-Oct 2026).
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-then-paid, not a free tier: once ~$30 is burned, pay-as-you-go with a card is required. Basic-tier default rate limits are ~15 req/min and ~100K tokens/min (not a guaranteed no-card free allowance). The $30 figure comes from Baseten's FAQ/changelog, not the current pricing page headline, so treat as ~$30 and confirm against your own workspace's billing view. If you are an eligible startup, using this credit on your first workspace can disqualify you from the AI Startup Program (net-new-customers rule — see next finding).
  - *Source:* https://www.baseten.co/pricing/
- **Baseten AI Startup Program — up to $25,000 (Dedicated Inference/Training) + up to $2,500 (Model APIs)** (high) — Two separate credit pools: up to $25,000 in credits for Dedicated Inference or Training, and up to $2,500 in credits for Model APIs (combined ceiling $27,500, discretionary — amounts granted vary by startup, not guaranteed). Program perks beyond credits: response-time support escalating to shared Slack/Zoom channels, exclusive networking events, cross-cloud autoscaling access, and GTM support (launch/funding amplification, consultations, webinars). The same offer is listed as a non-starred entry on Google Cloud's Startup Perks page: "Up to $25K for Dedicated Deployments or Training and up to $2.5K for Model APIs — Get inference and training credits to get started on Baseten platform"; the Google Cloud Startup Perks page confirms the entry is unchanged in this form in 2025-2026.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage AI-first, VC-funded startups: funding stage Seed to Series A, company started less than 5 years ago, AI core to primary products. Net-new Baseten customers only (never received any Baseten credits before). No .edu email, no funding cap, and no regional limit stated; education institutions, governments, nonprofits, IPO'd/acquired companies, dev shops/consultancies/agencies are excluded per Baseten program acceptance terms. Not a student offer — there is no Baseten student/education program (no .edu path exists; third-party 'education discount' listings for Baseten are unverified boilerplate).
  - *How to claim:* Direct route: submit the application at https://www.baseten.co/talk-to-us/startup-program (form; Baseten follows up; manual, selective review — not guaranteed). Google route: join the Google for Startups Cloud Program (Start tier: unfunded digital-native startup, founded within 24 months, up to $2,000 GCP credits; Scale tier: VC-funded, up to $200K GCP credits, up to $350K for AI startups), then redeem the Baseten perk via the Perks page (cloud.google.com/startup/perks). Have funding-stage proof ready; the Baseten application gates you even when arriving via Google.
  - *Expiry:* No credit expiry date or renewal mechanism is published on the program page as of today.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Up to' figures are ceilings, not grants. Net-new-customer rule: if you already burned the ~$30 starter credit on a workspace you plan to use, you likely disqualify yourself — route your first serious workload through the application instead. Amount awarded is at Baseten's sole discretion; applications are selective. Whether a credit card is required to enroll, and any regional limits, are not stated on the official page. The Google channel adds a prerequisite (Google program membership) but the Baseten criteria still apply on top.
  - *Source:* https://www.baseten.co/startup-program/
- **Google Cloud Startup Perks — Baseten entry (channel: redeem via Google for Startups Cloud Program)** (medium) — On Google Cloud's Startup Perks page (AI infrastructure and tools section), Baseten's perk is one of 30+ partner offers and, unlike starred entries (Datadog, ElevenLabs 33M credits, Cartesia 12 months, OpenRouter $1K), it carries no star — meaning it is open to all Google for Startups Cloud Program members, not just Scale-tier (VC-funded) members. Redemption happens through the Perks page after joining the program. Google's own program credits (Start tier up to $2,000 valid 1 year; Scale tier up to $200K, up to $350K for AI-first startups, year 1 100% up to $100K/$250K plus 20% up to $100K more in year 2) are separate from the Baseten credits. The Baseten entry text on the Perks page matches Baseten's own program page figures exactly.
  - *Eligibility:* Members of the Google for Startups Cloud Program. Start tier: technology startup planning to seek VC funding soon, founded within the last 24 months, not yet received Google Cloud credits beyond free trial. Scale tier: VC-funded, Pre-Seed/Seed within last 5 years and Series A within last 12 months, not yet received more than $5,000 in Google Cloud credits. The Baseten perk itself additionally requires Baseten's own startup criteria (Seed-Series A, <5 years, AI-first, net-new Baseten customer). No .edu path; no student tier.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply to the Google for Startups Cloud Program (cloud.google.com/startup) and get approved; 2) open the Startup Perks page (cloud.google.com/startup/perks); 3) select the Baseten entry and follow its claim/application flow (which lands on Baseten's application form); 4) complete the Baseten application with funding-stage evidence.
  - *Expiry:* Google program: GCP credits valid 1 year; Baseten credits: no expiry published. Perks catalog is ongoing; Baseten's figures unchanged in 2025-2026. Some Google-wide perks on the same page carry their own terms (e.g., Google Workspace Business Plus free 12 months requires the company domain not have been on paid Workspace within 31 days).
  - *Gotchas:* The Perks page is JS-rendered and could not be fetched directly today — its Baseten entry was verified via the search-provider's full text of cloud.google.com/startup/perks, which matches Baseten's own program page; re-check before acting. Non-starred does not mean automatic: the Baseten application is still selective. Stacked credits (GCP program + Baseten) don't change Baseten's 'net new customer' rule.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/perks
- **2025–26 change:** Fall Into Inference (Sept-Dec 2025, sunset 2025-12-20): Baseten ran a promo, 'Until December 20, 2025... up to $10,000 of free inference credits' for new Baseten customers (official page baseten.co/talk-to-us/fall-into-inference, acceptance terms last updated 2025-09-02). It has ended and is no longer listed on the current Google Perks page or Baseten's site; as of 2026 the standing offers are only the ~$30 starter credit and the AI Startup Program ($25K/$2.5K). No 2025-2026 change to the Startup Program amounts: both $25K and $2.5K re-confirmed on Baseten's official program page and on cloud.google.com/startup/perks today. No student/education free offer exists for Baseten — no .edu email or education license anywhere; Baseten's program acceptance terms explicitly exclude educational institutions, government entities and nonprofits. Baseten's pricing-page FAQ no longer restates the $30 figure (says only 'new accounts come with credits'), so the exact starter-credit amount rests on Baseten's FAQ/changelog (~$30) and third-party re-verification; minor model deprecations in Sep 2026 do not affect credit programs.

### Cerebras Inference Free Trial

- **Free Trial: one-time $5 credit for new accounts** (high) — New accounts receive a one-time $5 promotional credit, usable across all public models (currently gpt-oss-120b and qwen-3.8-27b). Free Trial rate limits per model: 5 RPM, 30K uncached TPM, 90K total TPM, 1M tokens/hour, 1M tokens/day; qwen-3.8-27b vision capped at 2 images per request / 10 MiB payload. Free context is truncated vs paid: gpt-oss-120b 65k vs 131k, qwen-3.8-27b 64k vs 128k. Official changelog entry 2026-07-16 ('Free Trial credits for new accounts') introduced it; it replaces the prior always-free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Any new account — no .edu email, no startup status, no funding cap. A verified payment method (credit/debit card) IS required before Playground/API activate; you are not charged unless you buy PayGo credits. No region limits stated on official pages (one third-party tracker notes access is intended outside mainland China, unverified).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up for Cerebras Inference → add a valid payment method (activates Playground + API and grants the $5 credit) → build within Free Trial limits; a first PayGo credit purchase upgrades the account to Developer tier (much higher limits, e.g. gpt-oss-120b 1K RPM / 1M uncached TPM; no hourly/daily caps).
  - *Expiry:* Credit expires 30 days after it is granted; no renewal. When the credit is exhausted or expires, API and Playground access pause (keys/projects/settings stay intact); you must purchase PayGo credits to resume.
  - *Gotchas:* Card required despite being a 'free' trial (unlike most free tiers); credits are non-transferable, no cash value, and may be withheld/revoked for fraud, abuse, or duplicate accounts; 5 RPM breaks any agent that fires parallel tool calls (instant 429s); limits are per organization and per model; 'free' context windows are shorter than paid; model menu keeps shrinking (GLM-4.7 and Gemma 4 31B no longer on public endpoints as of Aug/Sep 2026); many guides still quote the old '1M free tokens/day' tier which no longer exists.
  - *Source:* https://www.cerebras.ai/pricing
- **YC Startup Deal: up to $22,500 in Cerebras Cloud inference credits** (high) — YC startups from any batch receive up to $22.5K worth of inference on Cerebras Cloud, plus priority support and co-marketing opportunities. Official page states 'No setup fees, no strings.' No published credit expiration, deadline, or per-startup amount caps.
  - *Eligibility:* YC (Y Combinator) startups from any batch — the current offer is YC-only on the official page. No funding/age caps stated. Non-YC startups are instead routed through Cerebras channel partners.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the 'Apply Now' button on https://www.cerebras.ai/yc-startup-deal (links to a HubSpot application form); an RSVP button on the same page points to the same form.
  - *Expiry:* No end date or credit-expiry stated on the official page; 'up to $22.5K' is discretionary and no separate terms page is on record.
  - *Gotchas:* Eligibility is YC-batch-only (the earlier open 'Cerebras for Startups' program is marked no longer available on third-party trackers); the exact credit amount you receive is not published — 'up to $22,500'; co-marketing commitment is mentioned but undefined.
  - *Source:* https://www.cerebras.ai/yc-startup-deal
- **No student/education program exists (negative finding)** (medium) — As of 2026-10-22 there is no .edu/student or academic licensing offer from Cerebras. Neither the pricing page, the inference docs, nor searches of 2025-2026 coverage surface a student discount, education license, or academic credit program. The only education-adjacent item is a free DeepLearning.AI short course 'Fast LLM Inference with Cerebras' (built with Cerebras, launched ~July 2026) — instructional content, no API credits.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — nothing to claim for students specifically; students use the standard $5 trial (card required) or Cerebras models hosted on partner platforms (OpenRouter, HuggingFace, AWS Marketplace), where the partner's own free allowances may apply.
  - *How to claim:* None on Cerebras itself; students may take the free DeepLearning.AI course (enroll at deeplearning.ai, free) for hands-on Cerebras inference learning.
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* No .edu verification anywhere in Cerebras's onboarding; if a tool offers 'Cerebras student credits', it is not an official Cerebras program. Partner front-ends (OpenRouter free models, HuggingFace free tier) are how cardless users actually reach Cerebras-hosted models for free.
  - *Source:* https://community.deeplearning.ai/t/new-course-enroll-in-fast-llm-inference-with-cerebras/893825/1
- **Referral token-bonus program (third-party reported, unverified)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — A third-party tracker (Model Speed Arena) describes a Cerebras referral program: +200K Qwen3-Coder tokens/day for both referrer and referee, capped at +1M extra tokens/day, with personal referral links generated from an in-product signup flow. This does not appear on any Cerebras official page I could find.
  - *Eligibility:* Unknown — described as an in-product signup/referral flow; likely open to existing users.
  - *How to claim:* Per the third-party description: generate a personal referral link from the Cerebras console's signup flow and share it; both parties gain daily token bonuses.
  - *Expiry:* Unknown; no stated deadline in the third-party source.
  - *Gotchas:* Treat as unverified/stale: the model it references (Qwen3-Coder) is not in the current public model catalog (only gpt-oss-120b and qwen-3.8-27b as of Oct 2026), and Cerebras official pages make no mention of a referral program. Confirm in-console before relying on it.
  - *Source:* https://oldmsa.millaguie.net/providers/cerebras.html
- **2025–26 change:** 1) The old always-free tier ("1M free tokens/day", no card required — announced June 2025 when Cerebras opened the API without a waitlist) ended in July 2026: the official changelog shows the $5 Free Trial credit launching 2026-07-16 and a third-party tracker (pricepertoken.com) dates the old free tier's end to 2026-07-21. Cerebras now states plainly there is no permanently free or auto-renewing tier; a verified payment method is required for any access. 2) The earlier general "Cerebras for Startups" program (up to $22,500 GPU/inference credits, direct application, no partner required) is marked "No Longer Available" on third-party trackers; the surviving startup offer is the YC Startup Deal (YC startups any batch, up to $22.5K), recorded on the official site as of early Aug 2026. 3) Public shared-inference model menu shrunk through 2025-2026: Llama 3.3 70B and Qwen 3 32B deprecated (Feb 2026), zai-glm-4.7 deprecated 2026-08-17, gemma-4-31b removed from public endpoints 2026-09-03 (dedicated only), qwen-3.8-27b added 2026-09-03; as of Oct 2026 the public catalog is just gpt-oss-120b and qwen-3.8-27b. 4) Cerebras Code Pro ($50/mo, ~24M tokens/day) and Code Max ($200/mo, ~120M tokens/day) flat-rate coding plans were sold out / on waitlist at time of reporting — neither is free. 5) No region restrictions are stated on official pages; third-party trackers suggest access outside mainland China.
- **Critic correction** (Cerebras Inference Free Trial): Renamed and restricted. The Free Trial was merged into one Developer tier in Sept-Oct 2026. The pricing page now describes that tier as for development, evaluation, and experimentation and not intended for production use, so production needs an Enterprise contract. The one-time $5 credit (needs a valid payment method, expires 30 days after activation) still matches.

### Cloudflare

- **Workers AI free tier: 10,000 free Neurons/day (no signup offer needed)** (high) — Cloudflare's Workers Free AND Workers Paid plans each include 10,000 Neurons per day at no charge; the cap resets daily at 00:00 UTC; exceeding it makes further Workers AI requests fail. 72 models currently listed (39 LLM, 6 embeddings, 6 image, 10 audio, 11 other). Pricing page was restructured: "updated to be more granular, with per-model unit-based pricing presented" (per-model $/token, $/tile, $/minute alongside Neuron equivalents); backend billing still uses Neurons. On Workers Paid, usage above the daily allowance costs $0.011 per 1,000 Neurons; prepaid AI Gateway credits can also cover frontier-model usage and raise rate limits for those models.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a Cloudflare account on the Workers Free plan (no credit card, no application, no .edu/funding requirements); the same allowance also ships with Workers Paid. US region: no student-only or startup-only gate.
  - *How to claim:* Create a free Cloudflare account (no payment method required for the Free plan) and call Workers AI (HTTP API or Worker binding). 10,000 Neurons/day auto-applies; nothing to redeem.
  - *Expiry:* Permanent free tier; daily 10k-Neuron window resets at 00:00 UTC. No fixed end date.
  - *Gotchas:* 8 frontier models (kimi-k2.6, kimi-k2.7-code, glm-5.2, glm-5.3, glm-5.3-flash, deepseek-v4-flash-0731, deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017, deepseek-v4-pro-0813) require a paid billing method — unavailable on Free plan alone, even within the free Neuron allowance. Neurons-per-request varies by model and input/output direction, so the effective free daily quota in tokens differs widely per model; per-model caps also exist. On Paid, overage is $0.011/1k Neurons. Egress/data costs: Workers AI calls from a Worker are same-edge, but if your app is outside Workers, standard API call costs apply.
  - *Source:* https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/platform/pricing/(lastupdatedOct20,2026—readtoday)
- **Cloudflare for Startups: up to $350,000 in credits (3 tiers: $10k / $100k / $350k)** (high) — Current structure (read on cloudflare.com/startups today): Tier 3 = $10k credits (bootstrapped/self-funded or under $1M raised, no partner needed); Tier 2 = $100k (under $5M raised, funded by an affiliated partner, account manager + expert sessions); Tier 1 = $350k ($5M+ raised, funded by affiliated partner, account manager, priority support, bi-weekly Solutions Architect office hours). Credits are valid 1 year or until fully consumed, whichever comes first; no extensions. Credits cover usage-based services; Workers AI is subject to per-tier caps: $2,500 (Tier 3), $10,000 (Tier 2), $50,000 (Tier 1), at standard partner-model pricing; R2 capped at $10,000; AI Gateway temporarily NOT covered by credits. Free at every tier: 3 enterprise domains, unlimited Pro/Business plans, DDoS/bot protection, WAF with 1,000 custom rules, CDN/DNS, LB (2 origins), 100% SLA. Business email matching company domain required; a valid payment method on file is required.
  - *Eligibility:* For-profit tech company incorporated within the last 10 years; funding up to Series B, funded within the last 12 months; live public website; active LinkedIn/X/GitHub; first-time program applicants only; business email matching your company domain (no personal email). Tier 2/1 require funding by an affiliated (approved VC/accelerator) partner.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a standard Cloudflare account and add a billing/payment method. 2) Fill the application at /lp/startups (company domain email, website, social link, Cloudflare account ID). 3) Manual review ~48 hours; on approval credits and plan upgrades are applied to the account. Rejections include a reason and you may request re-evaluation.
  - *Expiry:* Credits: 12 months from approval or until consumed; no renewal/extension offered (upgrading tier does not reset the 12-month window). Program expansion (Sept 2025) also covers non-profits/civil-society orgs (up to $250k, per Birthday Week 2025 announcement).
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card/payment method IS required despite being a 'free credits' program. Overage beyond the credit balance is billed automatically. The old 4-tier structure ($5k/$25k/$100k/$250k, announced April 2025) was replaced — Tier 3 raised $5k→$10k, top tier $250k→$350k. Workers AI sub-cap ($2.5k–$50k depending on tier) means most of the $350k is NOT spendable on Workers AI. AI Gateway excluded for now. Non-US regional pages (e.g. en-in) still show the outdated $250k/4-tier structure — the US .com page is authoritative.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/startups/(readtoday)
- **Cloudflare for Students: 12 months of Workers Paid at no cost (US .edu students)** (high) — US students 18+ with a verified .edu billing email get the Workers Paid plan's $5/month base fee waived for 12 months, with increased usage allotments vs. the free plan: up to 500 Worker scripts, 10M requests/month (+$0.30/M after), 20M included logs/month (+$0.60/M after), up to 30s CPU per request, 6,000 build minutes/month, 6 concurrent build slots, extra Workers KV edge storage. The announcement post (Sept 22, 2025) additionally listed Hyperdrive unlimited DB queries/day, Durable Objects 1M req/day (+$0.15/M after), Logpush 10M events/month, and Queues 1M ops/month — usage beyond the paid-tier allotments still incurs standard rates, and Workers AI remains metered at $0.011/1k Neurons (10k Neurons/day included on any plan). Includes a dedicated student Discord. Announced at Birthday Week, Sept 22, 2025.
  - *Eligibility:* United States only; 18+; currently enrolled in an eligible educational program; verified .edu email address that must be the BILLING email (not just signup email). Expansion to other countries is requested via the program's school-domain registration form.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up (or log in) with your verified .edu email. 2) On the Billing page, add a valid credit card and confirm your billing email is the .edu address. 3) Add the Workers Paid plan at the Workers plans page — the $5/mo fee auto-waives for 12 months. 4) Confirmation email arrives in your inbox.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months; if you don't renew to standard Workers Paid, the account auto-reverts to Workers Free with no charge. No automatic renewal of the waiver.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required (verification + overage billing) even though the fee is waived; overage usage above student allotments is charged to that card. Not stackable with the Startup Program's business email requirement (personal .edu vs company domain). Workers AI 'free' access here is just the plan's standard 10k Neurons/day — the student offer does NOT add extra AI neurons; heavy LLM use costs money on the student account.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/students/(readtoday)+https://blog.cloudflare.com/workers-for-students/(Sept22,2025)
