The free/open/self-paced model and free-certification positioning are consistent with Cloudflare's learning/community pages but were not re-read on the live page.
  - *Source:* https://academy.cloudflare.com/
- **Cloudflare Ambassador & Community programs (student leaders)** (high) — The Cloudflare Community Ambassador program explicitly targets 'student leaders' (alongside community organizers and technical creators): run on-campus clubs, meetups, workshops, and content. Benefits include Cloudflare credits, marketing assets, swag, and access to Cloudflare experts; cohorts last 1 year with the option to reapply. A separate Community Engineer track provides discretionary grants for meaningful open-source work. Neither is a paid role.
  - *Eligibility:* Open application; non-technical enthusiasts welcome; student club leaders, community organizers, and open-source contributors.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through cloudflare.com/community under the Ambassador (or Community Engineer) track.
  - *Expiry:* Cohort-based: 1-year Ambassador cohorts, reapply to continue; Community Engineer has no fixed term.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits/grants are discretionary (awards, not a flat entitlement) and cohort-based, not a guaranteed free tier like the Free plan.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/community/
- **Cloudflare Free (Network & CDN) plan — always-free** (high) — $0/month website/CDN plan: CDN, unmetered DDoS protection, Universal SSL, WAF with Free Managed Ruleset, DNS, SSO, Role-based Account Control; 70 WAF rules + 10K bulk redirects; bot mitigation limited to 'Simple bots'; community forums support only. Paid tiers for comparison: Pro $20/mo annual ($25 monthly), Business $200/mo annual ($250 monthly), Enterprise custom. Page targets 'personal or hobby projects'.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone worldwide; no .edu email, no student status, no credit card/payment method required for the Free plan (sign up at dash.cloudflare.com/sign-up, 'Start building for free' — payment method only needed later to enable paid products/services).
  - *How to claim:* Create a free Cloudflare account (no payment method), add your domain as a zone, set nameservers to Cloudflare. Instant, perpetual, renews monthly at $0. The plan page (network-cdn) does not state a hard zone/site cap or bandwidth cap; Free has historically been unlimited-bandwidth with all sites/zones included.
  - *Expiry:* Permanent; no expiry. Free-plan feature sets can shrink/change with plan revisions.
  - *Gotchas:* Only 'Simple bots' mitigation and 70 rules on Free; WAF max payload and bot analytics are paid-plan features. Paid add-ons cost extra (e.g. Log Explorer $1/GB after 10 GB).
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/plans/network-cdn/
- **Cloudflare Developer Free plan — Workers, R2, D1, KV, Pages, AI (perpetual, not a trial)** (high) — Perpetual free allowances (monthly unless noted): Workers 100,000 requests/day + 10 ms CPU/request (128 MB memory; 100 Workers; 5 cron triggers; 50 external subrequests/request); R2 10 GB-month Standard storage + 1M Class A + 10M Class B ops/month; D1 5 GB total storage + 5M rows read/day; Workers KV 1 GB storage + 100K reads/day; Pages 500 builds/month, 100 custom domains/project, 20K files, 20-min build timeout; Workers AI 10,000 neurons/day; Observability free: 0.5 GB/day ingestion, 7-day retention; Workflows 3,000 steps/day + 1 GB-month storage. No charge for egress/bandwidth on Workers and R2. Workers Paid plan is $5/mo minimum (includes 10M requests + 30M CPU ms).
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone; no .edu/student requirement, no funding/company status, and credit card NOT required to create a Free account — docs explicitly frame adding a payment method as a post-signup step to 'enable additional products and services'.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up free at dash.cloudflare.com/sign-up; free allowances are included automatically on any new account — no application, no key, no card. Exceeding Workers quotas returns error 1027 (fail-open/closed), not a bill, on the Free plan; overages on R2/D1/KV etc. do bill, which requires a payment method on the account.
  - *Expiry:* Permanent free tier; daily limits reset at midnight UTC, monthly allowances reset each billing month. 2026 additions started charging for some add-ons (see sunset_notes).
  - *Gotchas:* R2 free tier covers Standard storage only (Infrequent Access is never free). New metered add-ons (R2 SQL, R2 Data Catalog billed 2026-08-03; AI Search billing from 2026-11-01 with 5M ingestion tokens, 10 GB-month, 1K semantic + 1K full-text queries/month included) can produce invoices on an otherwise-free account — monitor 'Billable Usage' and set spend caps. Workers AI Gateway is 'temporarily not covered' by startup credits.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/plans/
- **Cloudflare Startup Program — annual credits ($10K / $100K / $350K tiers)** (high) — Cloudflare's startup program (cloudflare.com/startup, apply at /lp/startups) offers credits valid 1 year or until consumed (extensions not granted). Tiers recently raised: Tier 3 $10,000/yr (was $5,000), 'no minimum funding required'; Tier 2 $100,000/yr + account manager; Tier 1 $350,000/yr (was $250,000) + Office Hours + priority support. Exclusions/caps: R2 capped at $10K credit; Workers AI capped at $2.5K/$10K/$50K by tier; AI Gateway temporarily not covered; domain registrar fees and premium network services (Magic Transit, Enterprise Spectrum, Dedicated IPs, China Network) excluded. Review typically ~48 hours, confirmation by email.
  - *Eligibility:* For-profit company only; incorporated no more than 10 years ago; funded up to Series B (funding within last 12 months, except Tier 3 needs no minimum funding); business email on the company domain — personal emails are rejected, so .edu/student emails are NOT eligible; must have a public website and public LinkedIn/X/GitHub presence; first-time applicant only; no region limit is stated on the page.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the form at https://www.cloudflare.com/lp/startups with company/business-domain email; if accepted you receive the tier's annual credit to offset Cloudflare spend. The startup program is for companies, so students/universities/sole .edu users effectively cannot claim it.
  - *Expiry:* Credits: 12 months from grant or until consumed, whichever first; no extensions. Active members near old caps may email startups@cloudflare.com for case-by-case tier upgrade (does not reset the 12-month window).
  - *Gotchas:* Business-entity + company-domain email rules make it unusable for individual students, classes, or nonprofit/university projects; 'funding within last 12 months' + up-to-Series-B cap means late-stage and >10-year-old companies are out; entry tier is now $10K not the $5K referenced in older sources.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/startup/
- **Education / .edu programs — no dedicated student or university program found** (medium) — No .edu-only or student-exclusive Cloudflare offer was found today: no Cloudflare education license, no student credits, no university pricing page (cloudflare.com/academy and /lp/startups-education equivalents 404; academy.cloudflare.com did not resolve in this session; Cloudflare docs contain no 'academy/education' content). Cloudflare Academy (free courses/certifications for IT/security training, open to everyone — not .edu-gated) has existed in recent years, but its current status is unconfirmed from today's fetches. The only 'free' education-adjacent value is the general free developer plan plus free Radar data dashboards.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — negative finding: no .edu/student-email program exists to claim; free platform is available to students exactly as to anyone else (no card required).
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim. Students get the standard Free developer plan (Workers 100K req/day, R2 10 GB, D1 5 GB, KV 1 GB, Pages 500 builds/month) with no special student terms; universities would have to use the startup program only if they fit its for-profit company rules, which they typically do not.
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* If Cloudflare Academy has moved hosts (dead subdomain in this environment), re-verify before citing its free courses/certifications; treat any .edu-only claim as unsubstantiated.
  - *Source:* https://academy.cloudflare.com/(DNSdidnotresolve,2026-10-22)
- **2025–26 change:** Changes in 2025–2026: (1) The Cloudflare for Startups program was restructured from the April 2025 four-tier layout ($5k/$25k/$100k/$250k, per blog post 'Startup Program update') to the current three-tier layout ($10k/$100k/$350k) — Tier 3 raised $5k→$10k and top tier $250k→$350k; regional pages (e.g. cloudflare.com/en-in/forstartups) still display the outdated $250k/4-tier structure, so use the US .com page as authoritative. (2) Sept 22, 2025 (Birthday Week 2025): Students program launched (12 months free Workers Paid for US .edu 18+) and the program was extended to non-profits/civil-society orgs (up to $250k credits). (3) Sept 2025: 'Come build with us' hubs — program participants get office access in SF/Austin/London/Lisbon from Jan 2026. (4) Sept 28, 2026: 'The Cold Start' competition announced — 5 early-stage companies pitch at Cloudflare Connect; grand prize $500,000 in credits (competition, not a standing program). (5) Workers AI pricing page (last updated Oct 20, 2026) moved to per-model granular unit-based pricing display while backend billing remains Neuron-based; 8 frontier models are now gated behind a paid billing method. Sunset of 2022–2024 programs: the original $1.25B/$2B-pledge Workers Launchpad funding program (2022) evolved into the current cohort Launchpad; no free-offer sunsets this cycle — all five offers above remain active as of 2026-10-22. Note: one follow-up web search (non-profit program page URL) could not be executed because the session's WebSearch budget was exhausted; non-profit and Launchpad findings lean on blog/press summaries and cached page content, hence medium confidence.
- **2025–26 change:** 2025–2026 program changes: (1) The Startup Program was restructured in 2025 from the April 2025 4-tier structure ($5,000 / $25,000 / $100,000 / $250,000, 'founded within last 5 years') to the current 3 tiers ($10k / $100k / $350k, founded within last 10 years) — entry tier doubled, top tier raised $250k->$350k, and the $25k tier was dropped. Members on the old caps can request a case-by-case upgrade that does not reset their 12-month expiry. (2) Sept 22, 2025: program expanded to non-profit, civil-society, and public-interest organizations (up to $250k credits; first application window ran until Dec 1, 2025). (3) Sept 22, 2025: Cloudflare for Students launched (12-month free paid developer features, US .edu only) — an education license/credit that did not exist before 2025. (4) 'The Cold Start' ($500k credit prize) closed with its Oct 19, 2026 Connect event; applications ended Oct 2, 2026. (5) Stale pages: some localized pages (e.g., cloudflare.com/en-in/forstartups/) still show the old '5 years / $5k-$250k' criteria — treat as outdated; the global /startups/ page is authoritative. (6) Community note: a global Cloudflare outage on Nov 18, 2025 (Workers KV, Access, Turnstile, dashboard, core CDN) is a recurring single-vendor risk with free tiers. The pre-launch claim 'WAF (1,000 rules) on all [startup] tiers' is confirmed — but the regular Free plan only gets 5 WAF custom rules; 1,000 is the Enterprise-plan limit that the startup program includes at no cost.
- **2025–26 change:** As of 2026-10-22, Cloudflare has NO distinct free offers targeted at students or startups: (1) No startup program — the official partners page (cloudflare.com/partners, fetched 2026-10-22) lists only PowerUP channel program, technology alliances, global SIs, service providers, and the Cloudflare@OCI alliance; no startup track, no free credits, no age/funding-capped program. (2) No trial credits — paid Workers plans simply start at $5/mo with usage-based overage; no free-trial-of-paid credits are offered. (3) No education license/.edu discount for infrastructure — cloudflare.com/plans (fetched 2026-10-22) shows usage-based Free/Paid tiers only and mentions no education, student, or nonprofit discount; no .edu email is required for any tier; the free tiers are universally available to any account. Cloudflare's only education-adjacent offering is free learning content/certification programs (Learning Hub / CF-ICD), which is training, not hosting/PaaS — and its landing page (developers.cloudflare.com/learning-hub) was 404, so status is unverified. 2025–2026 material changes affecting the free tiers: Pages is not sunset but is being pushed into a migrate-to-Workers posture (a 'Migrate From Pages' guide and a Pages index note steering users to Workers static assets; Pages limits unchanged: 500 builds/mo, 100 projects, 20,000 files); Workers Logs free tier migrates to Cloudflare Observability pricing on 2026-12-01; Durable Objects SQLite storage billing begins January 2026; legacy KV API routes deprecated with 2026-10-15 migration deadline; R2 free tier (10 GB) unchanged, with new free sub-tiers added in 2026 for R2 SQL (10 GB scanned/mo) and R2 Data Catalog (1M ops + 10 GB compaction/mo), both billing-enabled 2026-08-03. Note: the WebSearch budget was exhausted for this session, so 2025-2026 change verification relied on official docs/changelog pages (fetched today) rather than press/news coverage; 'last updated' dates on the source pages are 2026, confirming the limits above are current.
- **2025–26 change:** 2024-2026 changes: (1) Startup program was revamped Sep 2024 from a one-size $500k-era offer to a credits system capped at $250,000 (3 tiers). April 11, 2025: expanded to four tiers — $5,000 (bootstrapped/stealth, founded last 5 yrs), $25,000 (funded to $1M), $100,000 ($1M-$5M + approved VC/accelerator), $250,000 (Tier-1 VCs / AI / Workers Launchpad) — with a 5-year founding window and a 'BOOTSTRAPPED' promo-code path. The current 2026 page replaced this with three tiers ($10k/$100k/$350k), a 10-year founding window, 'funded up to Series B within the last 12 months', a hard payment-method-on-file requirement, and per-tier Workers AI caps. Old $250k/$5k-$250k four-tier copy still lingers on stale localized sub-pages (en-gb, en-ca) — treat cloudflare.com/startups as current. (2) Pages platform shift 2025-2026: Pages was consolidated into the Workers platform; docs now steer new projects to Workers ('Start new projects with Workers'; Pages Functions billed exactly as Workers), but Pages is not sunset — Free plan numbers stand as verified Sep 5, 2026 doc: $0, unlimited static requests/bandwidth, 500 builds/mo, 100 projects, 100 custom domains/project, 20,000 files (raised from the prior 10,000 cap; paid tiers unlock 100,000 via PAGES_WRANGLER_MAJOR_VERSION=4), 25 MiB max asset. (3) No student/education program was created or sunset in this window; 'Project Cybersafe Schools' (free K-12 email security + DNS filtering, US districts) remains the only education-facing free offer. No trial-credit program (e.g., a $500 API-credit trial) is currently advertised on the official pages I read.
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025–2026 changes: (1) Startup Program tier structure was revamped repeatedly: Sept 2024 revamp set the ceiling at $250k; April 2025 expanded to 4 tiers ($5k/$25k/$100k/$250k); by mid-2026 it was restructured into 3 tiers ($10k/$100k/$350k, per the current cloudflare.com/startups page and a May 2026 announcement). (2) Sep 2025: non-profit, civil-society, and public-interest organizations became eligible for the Startup program (up to $250k) — application window opened with a Dec 1 close for that cohort. (3) Eligibility window widened from 'founded within last 5 years' (2019–2024) to 'founded within last 10 years.' (4) Workers free-plan subrequest limit changed Feb 2026: now 50 external + 1,000 Cloudflare-service subrequests per invocation (previously a flat 1,000 total). (5) Workers Logs: effective Dec 1, 2026 it moves to Cloudflare Observability pricing (a near-term change affecting the free observability front). (6) Sep 2026: Free-account creation expanded so users can create up to 5 standalone Free accounts via dashboard or API. No traditional 'education license discount' or separate individual developer-credit grant exists at Cloudflare; the free value for students is the perpetual Free plan + free Academy + discretionary Ambassador/Community grants, and for startups the credits program.
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025-2026 changes: (1) Startup program tiers were "recently updated" (undated on page, current as of 2026-10-22): Tier 3 raised $5,000→$10,000/yr and Tier 1 $250,000→$350,000/yr — older references to a "$5K credit" are stale; entry tier is now $10K. (2) No general-public time-boxed trial or one-off trial-credit offer exists; the only dollar-credit offer is the application-based startup program (perpetual free tiers are not trials and never expire). (3) New billable dimensions landed on the free platform in 2026: R2 SQL and R2 Data Catalog billing enabled 2026-08-03 for non-Enterprise accounts (free tier 10 GB scanned / 1M catalog ops / 10 GB compaction per month), AI Search billing begins 2026-11-01 (free: 5M ingestion tokens, 10 GB-month, 1K semantic + 1K full-text queries/month), and 2026-02-11 raised Workers subrequest limits (Free stays at 50 external/1,000 internal per invocation; Paid default 10,000, configurable to 10M) — free accounts can still accrue charges on metered add-ons, so set spend limits. (4) 2025-12-05 WAF max-payload change: Free zones already protected to 1 MB; paid plans need support ticket to enable. (5) Unbound/Bundled Workers plans are deprecated and unavailable to new accounts. (6) Education: no .edu-only program found and Cloudflare Academy's host (academy.cloudflare.com) did not resolve today — treat its current availability as unverified; if it was sunset in 2025-2026, no student/education free offer remains at Cloudflare.

### Cohere Trial API key

- **Cohere Trial API key (free tier, self-serve)** (high) — Auto-issued Trial API key on account creation: all API calls free, 1,000 API calls/month across all endpoints combined (per official docs.cohere.com rate-limits page; not stated on the pricing FAQ itself), with per-endpoint rate limits: Chat 20 req/min per model (Command A+, A Reasoning, A Translate, A Vision, Command A, R+, R, R7B, North Mini Code), Embed 2,000 inputs/min (same as production), Embed (Images) 5 inputs/min, Rerank 10 req/min, Tokenize 100 req/min, Parse 500 req/min, Audio Transcriptions 5 req/min. No credit card required; non-commercial use only — trial keys are explicitly 'not permitted to be used for production or commercial purposes'. No expiry date published; monthly quota resets on calendar month.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone — any email, sign up at dashboard.cohere.com (email, Google, or GitHub). No .edu requirement, no student status, no region limit stated. No credit card or payment required.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at dashboard.cohere.com; the Trial key is generated automatically and is copyable from the dashboard / 'API Keys' section. No application step. Usage caps: 1,000 calls/month total; 429s when rate limits or monthly cap hit; 402 when account billing limit reached.
  - *Expiry:* No published end date; 1,000-call monthly quota resets each calendar month. Key remains available for the life of the account.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: this is a permanent free tier, not a time-boxed trial, but strictly non-commercial — commercial production use requires a paid Production key (Owner completes 'Go to Production' workflow; pay-as-you-go, invoiced monthly or at $250 outstanding balance). 2026 change: for newer Chat model variants (Command A+, Command A Reasoning/Translate/Vision) production keys are capped like trial keys (1,000 calls/month, 20 req/min) and commercial use of those models requires contacting sales@cohere.com — so the free tier is the only self-serve access for the newest models. Data: Cohere logs prompts/generations, deleted after 30 days; opt out via Settings -> Data Controls. Third-party 'student deal' listings (e.g. The Modelverse) that tout '.edu verified, 365-day grant' are just rebrands of this same universal trial key — no .edu verification or separate grant exists.
  - *Source:* https://cohere.com/pricing(FAQ:'HowdoIgetaTrialAPIkey?','What'sthedifferencebetweenaTrialAPIkeyandProductionAPIkey?')+https://docs.cohere.com/docs/rate-limits(officiallimitstables,readtoday)
- **Cohere Labs Catalyst Grants (research / education / public-benefit API credits)** (high) — Free Cohere API access (credits + compute) for academic partners, civic and public-impact organizations. Amounts per grant are not fixed or published; program page shows running totals (counters for grants awarded, $k in API credits, countries, university affiliations; third-party mid-2025 figure: 250+ grants, ~$350K API credits, 35+ countries). Credits tracked in the dashboard Billing tab; a Production API key is required to use them; if credits run out, contact brittawnya@cohere.com for more. AI-safety research has disclosure obligations: vulnerabilities must be disclosed unconditionally to labs@cohere.com at least 4 weeks before publication; no other pre-publication approval required.
  - *Eligibility:* Academic partners, civic and public-impact organizations (not strictly .edu-only — no formal .edu rule, but projects must be public-benefit or open-science research; non-commercial). Focus areas: education, healthcare, climate, sustainability, multilingual work, model safety, prosocial applications, values alignment. Credit card not required.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the form at cohere.com/research/grants/application (official page cohere.com/research/grants). Rolling consideration, no fixed deadline. Colleagues outside your institution can be invited to use the credits via the dashboard Team tab. Optional acknowledgment line: 'This work was supported by compute credits from a Cohere Labs Research Grant.'
  - *Expiry:* No sunset published; application status shown as 'Open / rolling basis' as of today.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are project/eligibility-scoped, not a universal student tier — individual students only get access via a faculty/institutional grant (or they apply as part of a qualifying organization). Grants are competitive and rolling, so timing is unpredictable. Grant access uses a Production key, meaning the non-commercial restriction of trial keys is replaced by the public-benefit/open-science restriction instead. The Sep 2025 education post is marketing for this same program, not a separate education license.
  - *Source:* https://cohere.com/research/grants(readtoday)+cohort/educationframing:https://cohere.com/blog/exploring-ai-in-education(Sep23,2025,'UsingCohereGrantstotransformstudentsintoAIbuilders')
- **Cohere Startup Program (2024 cohort) — 25% off for 12 months + partner-VC credits — CLOSED** (high) — Launched June 6, 2024: 25% discount on Cohere's proprietary models for one year, plus engineering support, logo/use-case showcase on cohere.com, joint webinars and co-marketing; zero equity; historically up to $100,000 API credits available to startups affiliated with partner VCs/accelerators. It was a discount program, not upfront free credits. Cohere's own post carried the note 'Applications are now closed for 2024' and no 2025/2026 cohort was announced.
  - *Eligibility:* At closure: early-stage AI startups from bootstrapped through Series B, building on Cohere tech, global (no published country restriction). The 25%-off benefit ran for 12 months from acceptance.
  - *How to claim:* No live claim path: cohere.com/startup-program and /startup-program-application now redirect to the cohere.com homepage (verified today, 2026-10-22, and re-verified by independent trackers on 2026-10-09 and 2026-10-10); cohere.com/startups returns 404. While it was open: submit the application, Cohere's team reviewed fit, then 25% discount applied at the billing layer for a year. Current substitute for prototyping: the free Trial key; for paid use: production keys or AWS Activate credits on Cohere models in Amazon Bedrock.
  - *Expiry:* Closed: 'Applications are now closed for 2024' per Cohere's post; program URLs dead as of 2026-10-09/10-22. No renewal announced.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not plan around this program: no closure notice was published, it simply lapsed — the launch post body is thin and the numbers above come from the post plus trackers. It is distinct from the Catalyst Grants (which cover research/public-benefit, not commercial startups). Third-party directories still list it as 'active' (e.g. SaaSTweaks, Startup Intros 'Cohere for Good') — ignore those; the official URLs are dead as of October 2026.
  - *Source:* https://cohere.com/blog/cohere-launches-startup-program(June6,2024launchpost)+closureverificationofhttps://cohere.com/startup-program(redirectstohomepage)andhttps://www.cloudroutehq.com/startup-credits/cohere(2026-10-09)
- **No dedicated student .edu license — education access rides on the universal trial key + Catalyst Grants** (medium) — As of 2026-10-22 there is no Cohere student/education license, no .edu-verified free tier, and no student credit card offer. Students get the same 1,000-calls/month trial key as everyone else (non-commercial, which covers coursework/personal projects but not a commercial product), plus eligibility for free API credits through a faculty/academic Catalyst Grant.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — this is the absence of an offer. Verified across cohere.com (pricing, startup, research pages) and the Sep 2025 education blog post, which is Catalyst-Grants content only.
  - *How to claim:* Students: self-serve trial key for non-commercial use; apply (with a university/institution) to cohere.com/research/grants/application for public-benefit/open-science API credits.
  - *Gotchas:* Third-party perk aggregators (The Modelverse 'Cohere Free Trial API Key Free Student Deal', resets.pro, freellmapihub) list Cohere under student/research programs, but the 'student deal' entries are the generic trial key with fabricated framing (e.g. '365-day grant', '.edu verification') — Cohere requires no student verification. The only genuinely student-relevant first-party offer is the researcher-facing Catalyst Grant.
  - *Source:* https://cohere.com/pricing+https://cohere.com/blog/exploring-ai-in-education+https://cohere.com/research/grants(allcheckedtoday)
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Cohere Startup Program (2024: 25% off models for 12 months, no equity, up to ~$100K partner-VC API credits): CLOSED — Cohere's launch post (June 2024) notes 'Applications are now closed for 2024'; cohere.com/startup-program, /startup-program-application and /startups now redirect to the homepage or 404 (verified 2026-10-09 and 2026-10-22). No successor program announced; ignore third-party directories still listing it as active. 2) 2026 material change to the free tier itself: production keys now behave like trial keys for the newest Chat model variants (Command A+, Command A Reasoning/Translate/Vision) — 1,000 calls/month and 20 req/min — and commercial use of those models requires contacting sales@cohere.com, so free trial-key access is the only self-serve route to the newest models (per docs.cohere.com rate-limits page, read today). 3) Fine-tuning for legacy text models (command, command-light, command-r, classify, rerank) was retired 2025-09-15 (third-party reference; not a free offer per se). 4) No student/education license has ever existed at Cohere — the Sep 2025 'AI in Education' blog is Catalyst Grants content, and student-perk listings on aggregators are rebranded trial keys. 5) Trial-key Embed limit: current official docs say 2,000 inputs/min; an older third-party summary (Sep 2025) claimed 100/min — the official docs figure stands.

### GitHub

- **GitHub Copilot Free (perpetual free tier, no credit card)** (high) — $0/month, no subscription or payment/credit card required. Includes up to 2,000 code completions/month, a (not numerically disclosed) allowance of GitHub AI Credits (1 AI credit = $0.01 USD), limited chat and agent usage via auto model selection only, Copilot CLI and the Copilot app. Under the legacy request-based system this was 50 premium requests/month; after the 2026-06-01 migration to AI-credit usage billing GitHub does not publish a fixed credit figure for the Free plan. Not available to users whose account is managed by an org/enterprise; personal use only. Never expires; limits reset monthly.
  - *Eligibility:* Any individual with a GitHub account not managed by an organization/enterprise and without an existing Copilot paid/Student/teacher/maintainer plan. No email domain, age, or region criteria stated. No credit card required. Region-limited only where Copilot is unavailable (standard Copilot availability restrictions apply).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up directly in VS Code/Visual Studio ('Sign up for Copilot Free'), via github.com/settings/copilot ('Start using Copilot Free'), or chat at github.com/copilot. No verification steps.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual; monthly caps reset. Free/Student/teacher plans are reevaluated only where verification applies (Free has no verification, so no expiry).
  - *Gotchas:* Auto model selection only — no manual model picker, entry-level models only. Hitting the cap prompts an upgrade to Pro ($10). If you later join an org that assigns Copilot, Free access is lost. No code review, cloud agent, app modernization, or third-party agent delegation included. 'Unlimited completions' applies only to paid plans; Free caps at 2,000.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/features/copilot/plans
- **GitHub Copilot Student (free education license for verified students)** (high) — Free for students verified through GitHub Education: unlimited code completions and next-edit suggestions (completions never consume credits), plus a monthly GitHub AI Credits allowance — community-reported at 200 credits/month (~$2.00 of agent/chat usage) since the 2026-06-01 credit-based billing; GitHub does not publish a single official dollar figure for students, so verify in your plan's usage display. Limited chat/agent usage, auto model selection only. Since 2026-03-12 students are on a dedicated 'Copilot Student plan' (UI shows it alongside GitHub Education benefits); premium models incl. GPT-5.4, Claude Opus and Sonnet are no longer self-selectable (GPT-5.3-Codex removed from the picker 2026-04-27); access to OpenAI/Anthropic/Google models continues via Auto routing. Eligibility is reevaluated monthly by GitHub.
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13+ enrolled in a degree- or diploma-granting course at an accredited institution. Verification is via school-issued email and/or official, dated proof of enrollment (no strict .edu requirement, but .edu helps). No credit card required. Global program (thousands of universities). Not available to managed org accounts.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at github.com/settings/education/benefits ('Start an application', complete verification — school email/proof). Once verified, under 'Free GitHub developer resources for students and teachers' click Learn more and activate Copilot Student (or github.com/github-copilot/free_signup). Activation can lag verification by a few days; do not complete a paid checkout while waiting.
  - *Expiry:* Persists while academic verification remains valid; GitHub reevaluates eligibility every month — access is withdrawn when no longer enrolled/verified. Model/limit adjustments are being rolled out in stages (GitHub said more model/limit changes would follow the 2026-03 transition).
  - *Gotchas:* Credit allowance (~200 credits / ~$2) is small: agent-mode sessions with newer models can exhaust it in one day (community reports of 4-20 agent requests using the full allocation post-June 2026). Auto model selection only, no manual picker. No third-party agents, code review, or cloud agent included. ~2M students use the program; GitHub is actively trimming it for sustainability.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/copilot/how-tos/manage-your-account/free-access-with-copilot-student
- **GitHub Copilot Pro free for verified teachers and open-source maintainers (education/OSS license)** (high) — Full Copilot Pro at $0: unlimited completions, model selection, chat/agent/CLI, code review and cloud agent, and a $15/month AI-credit allowance (Base 1,000 + Flex 500 = 1,500 AI credits, 1 credit = $0.01). Two qualifying paths: (1) verified teacher on GitHub Education, (2) maintainer of a popular open-source repository (eligibility is determined by GitHub; objective repo thresholds are not publicly published — GitHub directs you to Copilot settings to see if you qualify).
  - *Eligibility:* Teachers: faculty at an accredited educational institution, verified via GitHub Education. OSS maintainers: maintainers of 'popular' open-source repos as judged by GitHub's criteria. Eligibility reevaluated monthly by GitHub.
  - *How to claim:* Teachers: apply/verify at github.com/settings/education/benefits, then activate in Copilot settings ('Get access to GitHub Copilot'). Maintainers: open Copilot settings; if eligible, a 'GitHub Copilot Pro' eligibility page appears — click Get access and save use policies. No payment info required for the free grant.
  - *Expiry:* While eligibility holds (monthly reevaluation). Note: if you are granted this free access you cannot cancel the plan — only losing eligibility ends it.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Popular' OSS-maintainer criteria are opaque; if you don't see the offer you likely don't qualify. Free access is personal, not shareable to the team. If you later join an organization that assigns Copilot seats, the free grant may be displaced.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-on-github/set-up-copilot/enable-copilot/set-up-for-teachers-and-os-maintainers
- **GitHub Education / Student Developer Pack benefits bundled with Copilot Student** (high) — Verified students also get a free GitHub Pro account (unlimited private repos with advanced collaboration), 3,000 GitHub Actions minutes/month, up to 180 GitHub Codespaces core hours/month (plus ~20 GB Codespaces storage), 2 GB GitHub Packages storage, and the Student Developer Pack (dozens of third-party partner offers for professional dev tools). These are GitHub's education-license companions to Copilot Student; the Copilot benefit is claimed from the same GitHub Education portal.
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13+ enrolled in a degree- or diploma-granting program at an accredited institution; apply with school-issued email and/or official dated proof of enrollment. Check first whether your school is a GitHub Campus Program partner (in that case benefits may be pre-arranged). No credit card required.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via education.github.com / github.com/settings/education/benefits, complete verification, then claim offers under 'Free GitHub developer resources for students and teachers' in that portal.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to active student verification (reevaluated monthly). Codespaces hours/Actions minutes reset monthly; unclaimed Codespaces hours are lost, not banked.
  - *Gotchas:* Actions minutes and Codespaces hours are per-month allowances that reset — no rollover. Usage in class/teacher-owned repos bills the owning org instead. 180 core-hours figure is what the docs state ('up to 180 core hours'); exact storage figures come from the marketing page.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/education/students
- **Trial credits / startup program: none GitHub-side for Copilot** (medium) — No time-limited free trial of paid Copilot plans is offered on the current plans page (the $0 Free plan is the entry point, not a trial), and GitHub does not run a GitHub Copilot startup program, startup credit pool, or funded 'Copilot for startups' offer. The nearest adjacent program is Microsoft's Cloud Startups initiative (Azure credits for eligible startups; GitHub is a Microsoft company, but Copilot Free/Student/Pro are not part of it and no Copilot-specific startup benefit was found). Confirmed by absence across the official plans, docs, and education pages reviewed today; web-search budget was exhausted before independently verifying Microsoft Cloud Startups terms.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — no GitHub Copilot startup offer exists to claim.
  - *How to claim:* n/a.
  - *Expiry:* n/a.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not expect 'startup credit' or 'Copilot Pro trial' offers that third-party activation/activation-marketplace sites advertise (e.g., shared-account 'Copilot activation' services) — those are unofficial and violate ToS. The free path is strictly Free plan, verified-student plan, or teacher/OSS-maintainer Pro.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/features/copilot/plans
- **GitHub Pro (free for students)** (high) — Free GitHub Pro while verified as a student: 3,000 GitHub Actions min/month, 180 Codespaces core-hrs/month, 2GB GitHub Packages, 20GB Codespaces storage. Core of the pack; most-featured benefit.
  - *Eligibility:* GitHub account owned by a student verified through GitHub Education (school/.edu email or enrollment proof). Must be enrolled; benefits renew while student status is maintained.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a GitHub account owned by the student. 2) Verify as a student at education.github.com (school email or enrollment docs). 3) Pro benefits are applied automatically to the account once verified; manage via the pack page.
  - *Expiry:* Continuous while verified; re-verify as enrollment changes. Not a fixed trial.
  - *Gotchas:* Actions minutes beyond 3,000/mo are billable; Codespaces overage beyond 180 core-hrs incurs charges. Benefits lapse if student verification lapses.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Copilot (Student plan)** (high) — Copilot Student plan included free: unlimited code completions, an allowance of GitHub AI Credits for chat/agent/CLI/Spaces, and limited chat/agent usage restricted to auto model selection. More generous than the base Copilot Free tier (which is 2,000 completions/mo + a limited AI-credit allowance, 1 credit = $0.01, models incl. Haiku 4.5 / GPT-5 mini, no CC required).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via GitHub Education (the pack). Student plan is a dedicated tier; verified students link to the student offer from the copilot features page.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Verify student status in GitHub Education. 2) Activate the Copilot Student plan from the pack / Copilot page. 3) Use in editor, CLI, web, or Claude Code/agents.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to student/enrollment status; renews while verified.
  - *Gotchas:* Student plan chat/agent is capped and limited to auto model selection (vs. Pro/Pro+ choice). 2026: from ~Apr 24 GitHub may use Free/Pro/Pro+ interaction data for model training unless you opt out; from Jun 1, 2026 code-review workflows start consuming Actions minutes.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Codespaces (free Pro-level)** (high) — Free Pro-level access to Codespaces anywhere in the account, as part of GitHub Pro for students (supports the 180 core-hrs/mo + 20GB storage bundle).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Included automatically with GitHub Pro once student-verified; create and run Codespaces directly.
  - *Expiry:* Continuous while verified.
  - *Gotchas:* Compute hours beyond the 180 core-hrs/mo are billed; large machine sizes burn hours faster. Idle machines keep accruing hours.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Pages** (high) — One site per account and per organization, plus unlimited project sites, free.
  - *Eligibility:* Any GitHub user; in the pack context available to verified students.
  - *How to claim:* Available by default; deploy a site from a repo (gh-pages) or project site from a repo settings tab.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing; free tier with standard bandwidth limits.
  - *Gotchas:* Standard free-tier bandwidth limits apply; no AI/LLM features here.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Desktop** (high) — Open-source desktop Git client, free for everyone (not student-specific).
  - *Eligibility:* Any user; listed in the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Download and install from github.com/desktop; sign in with a GitHub account.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing, free open source.
  - *Gotchas:* Requires a working GitHub account; no trial/CC involved.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Microsoft Azure for Students (18+)** (high) — $100 Azure credit usable within 12 months, plus free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (new customers), plus 65+ always-free services. (Live page today shows a single flat student tier, not the older 'monthly $-credit' structure some sources still cite.)
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time university students 18+; sign up with a school email; renew yearly while enrolled; no credit card required; one account per person.
  - *How to claim:* 1) From the pack, open the Azure for Students claim link. 2) Sign up with school email. 3) Receive $100 credit + free monthly service amounts; renew yearly.
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit valid 12 months; free monthly amounts for 12 months; renew while a student.
  - *Gotchas:* Beyond free amounts and the $100 credit, metered/egress charges apply. Region availability varies. Always-free tiers have their own caps (e.g. 750 B1 hrs, 32GB storage).
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students/
- **Microsoft Azure for Students (ages 13-17)** (medium) — For students aged 13-17: free access to a subset of Azure services (App Services, Functions, Notification Hubs, MySQL in-app, Application Insights, Azure DevOps). No credit card required.
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13-17 via GitHub Education; separate tier from the 18+ offer.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's 13-17 Azure link; no CC.
  - *Expiry:* Continuous while enrolled in the 13-17 tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Service subset only; not the full 18+ catalog; no $ credit tier for this age band.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Visual Studio Dev Essentials (incl. $200 Azure credit)** (medium) — Visual Studio Community + Pluralsight training, and '1 free year of Azure services with $200 Azure credit for the 1st month' as listed in the pack.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Dev Essentials link; sign in with the student/educational account.
  - *Expiry:* ~1 year of Azure services; credit for the first month.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit and free-service terms are set by Microsoft/VS and can change; metered overage beyond the free year is billable.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Azure for Startups (startup offer, not in SDC pack)** (high) — For the 'Startup' front: $1,000 Azure credits instantly with no application, and up to $5,000 total credits after business verification. Initial credits valid 90 days; post-verification credits valid 180 days.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage startup (business), NOT the student program; separate from the GitHub Student Developer Pack. Business verification required for the larger tier.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at Azure for Startups; instant $1,000 tier, then complete business verification for up to $5,000.
  - *Expiry:* $1,000 credits 90 days; up-to-$5,000 credits 180 days post-verification.
  - *Gotchas:* Not available to the SDC student pack; it is a distinct startup program with its own criteria and verification.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students/
- **Heroku** (medium) — $13 USD/month for 24 months (Heroku platform subscription discount).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack; valid payment/claim method required by Heroku.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Heroku link; create Heroku account and apply the 24-month offer.
  - *Expiry:* 24 months. A 24-month committed/discounted subscription, not free-forever.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a 24-month discounted subscription that can auto-continue past 24 months unless cancelled - read renewal terms. Add-ons and extra resources are billed separately.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Appwrite (Education plan)** (medium) — Education plan: 2 projects at Pro-equivalent limits ($40/month value) for 6 months.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the 6-month education plan through the pack's Appwrite link.
  - *Expiry:* 6 months.
  - *Gotchas:* After 6 months, the plan reverts to free-tier limits or paid; egress/overage beyond included limits can cost.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **LocalStack** (medium) — Free license to LocalStack's AWS emulator in a ready-to-use cloud environment.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the free LocalStack license through the pack link.
  - *Expiry:* Per LocalStack's student terms (verify).
  - *Gotchas:* Check current license/region availability; cloud instance may have free-tier resource caps.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Stripe** (medium) — $25 in Stripe credits to offset fees on Stripe Payments, Billing and other products.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the $25 credit through the pack's Stripe link.
  - *Expiry:* Credit validity per Stripe terms (verify).
  - *Gotchas:* Credits offset fees only; Stripe also charges per-transaction fees on top of covered usage; verify credit expiry.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Clerk** (medium) — Free Clerk Pro plan while a student (auth/UI, more limits than free tier).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the free Pro plan through the pack's Clerk link.
  - *Expiry:* While student status is maintained.
  - *Gotchas:* Reverts to free/paid tier after student status ends; confirm no CC is required at signup.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Sentry** (medium) — Free tier: 50K errors, 100K transactions, 1GB attachments, 500 replays, Team features, 1-year limit (renewable).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Sentry link; renew annually while a student.
  - *Expiry:* 1 year, renewable while student.
  - *Gotchas:* Beyond the error/transaction/replay caps, ingestion is blocked or downgraded; renew or lose access.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **MongoDB Atlas** (medium) — $50 MongoDB Atlas credits, plus access to MongoDB Compass and MongoDB University incl. a free certification valued at $150.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the $50 credit + tools/cert through the pack's MongoDB link.
  - *Expiry:* $50 credit window per MongoDB terms (verify).
  - *Gotchas:* Atlas cluster costs beyond the credit/MB free tier and egress are billable; free shared clusters have storage/network caps.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
