  - *How to claim:* Claim from https://cloud.google.com/free ('OFFER' cards, 'Start a ... free trial' buttons) or the per-product docs 'free trial' links; create the trial instance in a GCP project under a billing account.
  - *Expiry:* Fixed durations: 30 days (Cloud SQL, AlloyDB, Looker, Gemini Enterprise Plus), 90 days (Spanner, GKE), 10 days (Bigtable). After expiry the instance converts to standard PayG pricing.
  - *Gotchas:* During the trial period usage beyond the trial spec (e.g., more storage, extra instances) bills; after expiry everything bills. GCP's managed-DB trial instances skew toward the premium/Enterprise Plus editions - budget accordingly. No free-tier replacement for Memorystore appears on the page. Egress/network costs are separate line items.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google Cloud for faculty / classroom - teaching credits (up to $100 per faculty + up to $50 per student)** (high) — Eligible faculty can apply for up to $100 in GCP credits per teaching staff member and up to $50 in GCP credits per student for hands-on classroom use. Credits attach to the applicant's email and are non-transferable; group projects may pool students' individual credits (documented in faculty resources). Country-restricted: the application shows a fixed country list (US, Australia, Brazil, Canada, ... not worldwide).
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty/teaching staff at accredited educational institutions; official school-issued email on a valid .edu (or equivalent national) domain required - if you lack one you can nominate another email, but credits attach to it permanently. Course start date must be within 1 year of application.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/faculty 'Apply for teaching credits'): enter course details (up to 3 courses), expected student count, school domains, and how credits will be used. Then redeem in GCP and manage billing per the 'Manage Google Cloud credits' guide.
  - *Expiry:* Credits are redeemable from approval up until 16 weeks after the course start date, and are good for up to 1 year after the course start date.
  - *Gotchas:* Not available in every country. Credits are per-student capped ($50) - large cohorts need many enrollments or instructor credits only. Explicitly do not have students use the $300 Free Trial (credit-card required) instead of these credits. Credits are non-transferable and locked to the school domains listed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty
- **Google Cloud for researchers - research credits (up to $5,000) + GPAR program** (high) — Faculty/researchers (incl. PhD students) can apply for up to $5,000 in GCP research credits via the Academic Research Grants form ('Google Cloud for Researchers'). Applications specify intended use (proof of concept, evaluation/comparison, workshop/training, open-source development) and external funding status. The Google Public Sector 'Program for Accelerated Research' (GPAR) layers on top: co-investment pricing for GPUs/TPUs, early access to new accelerators, foundational models/datasets (e.g., AlphaFold, Earth AI), agentic AI (Co-scientist, AlphaEvolve), generative-AI grant tooling, and campus research days.
  - *Eligibility:* Researchers/faculty at accredited academic institutions, including PhD students acting on behalf of the institution; .edu/institutional email; some country restrictions on the credits form; credits may only be used on behalf of the educational entity for the described project, not personal use, and not for commercial purposes.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/researchers 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'): project abstract, start date, use of credits, post-credit funding plan; create a GCP billing account to hold the coupon. GPAR/campus research day via the express-interest form (edu.google.com/programs/express-interest).
  - *Expiry:* Credit coupon is valid for 60 days from the stated project start date; the credit itself expires 365 days from the coupon redemption date or when the amount is fully used, whichever comes first. Credits can be issued in increments as you use them.
  - *Gotchas:* Academic, non-commercial use only - cannot be resold, bartered or transferred. If you miss the 60-day redemption window the coupon lapses. 'Up to $5,000' is a per-application maximum; approval amount is at Google's discretion. Students who are not at an institution do not qualify - use the student offer below.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/researchers
- **Google Cloud for students - free learning + 200 free Google Skills credits** (high) — 'Google Cloud for students': free self-paced learning, Google Cloud Certificates and skill badges, hands-on Google Skills labs (formerly Cloud Skills Boost/Qwiklabs) and the gamified Google Skills Arcade, plus Google Developer Student Club communities. Students can request 200 free Google Skills credits (redeemable toward lab/training use); exam previews are free on YouTube. No GCP dollar-credit pool and no managed-DB free tier is granted directly to students - their hands-on GCP usage comes via faculty teaching credits, the $300 trial (card required), or the always-free caps.
  - *Eligibility:* Students at participating/any accredited institution, verified via school email where the request flow asks for it; Google account required. No credit card needed for the learning program or Skills credits. No funding/stage criteria.
  - *How to claim:* Go to cloud.google.com/edu/students -> 'Get started' (Google Cloud for students) and 'Request credits' for the 200 Google Skills credits; join a Google Developer Student Club chapter via the same page.
  - *Expiry:* Google Skills credits expire one year after they are issued. Learning/arcade access is tied to active student status.
  - *Gotchas:* These are training/lab credits, NOT $200 of GCP service credit - don't budget them for Cloud SQL/BigQuery instances. If students need GCP compute, route through faculty credits ($100/$50) or the card-required $300 trial.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Cloud Free Trial: $300 credit, 90 days** (high) — New GCP customers get $300 in free 'Welcome credit' valid for 90 days from signup (NOT 60 days as previously believed; official docs and the Jan-30-2025 archived T&Cs both say 90 days/3 months). Credit covers 200+ products (incl. Cloud SQL, BigQuery, Spanner) including usage beyond free-tier limits; Free Tier usage does not draw down the $300. No billing unless you manually upgrade to a Paid account. On upgrade mid-trial, remaining credit still expires 90 days from signup. No SLA during trial; restrictions: no GPUs on VMs, no GCP Marketplace, no quota increases, no Windows Server VMs, no Cloud VMware Engine, no crypto mining; the $300 cannot pay Gemini API in AI Studio costs or managed partner models (MaaS). Trial account ends at $300 spent or 90 days, then a 30-day grace period — resources stop, then are permanently deleted if not upgraded.
  - *Eligibility:* Must have never been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and never signed up for the Free Trial (one per person/identity). A valid credit card or other payment method IS required at signup (identity verification; a $0.00–$1.00 authorization hold may appear, it is not a charge). Bank-account verification needed in some countries. US/Mexico government entities excluded. No .edu requirement; no startup-only gating.
  - *How to claim:* 1) cloud.google.com/free → 'Get started for free' / console signup; 2) create a Google account; 3) complete Free Trial signup: name, address, payment method; 4) Google verifies identity/payment, trial billing account 'My Billing Account' + 'My First Project' are created, $300 preloaded; 5) use products for 90 days; optionally click 'Activate' to upgrade to a paid account before the trial closes to keep resources.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from signup or when $300 is exhausted (earlier); then 30-day grace period, after which trial resources are deleted. Free Tier (below) continues indefinitely after.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card/payment method required even though you aren't billed; resources are permanently deleted after the 30-day post-trial grace period; GCE limited to 8 concurrent cores during trial; 'eligible customers' free-tier availability can vary by country (e.g., India trial needs an INR billing account; Maps Platform not available on INR accounts); free limits are 'subject to change'.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features(seealsohttps://cloud.google.com/freeandhttps://cloud.google.com/signup-faqs)
- **Cloud SQL Free Trial Instance (30 days, Enterprise Plus)** (high) — Official free Cloud SQL offer today: a 30-day free trial instance at no cost — Cloud SQL Enterprise Plus edition, N2 machine series, 8 vCPUs, 64 GB memory, 100 GB storage. Covers MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQL Server. In addition to the $300/90-day GCP free-trial credit (new customers). Upgrade to a paid instance anytime during the 30-day trial or the following 90-day grace period; if not upgraded the instance is stopped. Deleting the trial instance makes the trial non-restartable.
  - *Eligibility:* Both existing AND new GCP customers can use it, but the trial instance must be created in a NEW Google Cloud project, one trial instance per project. An enabled Cloud Billing account is required: existing users verify billing is enabled; new users must set up a billing account with a card/other payment method (which normally means going through the $300 trial signup). No .edu requirement, no startup requirement.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Console → Cloud SQL 'Get Started' page → 'Experience Cloud SQL at no cost for 30 days' section; 2) choose engine, instance ID, password, region; 3) 'Create free instance'; 4) monitor remaining days on the instance Overview page; 5) click 'Upgrade' before trial/grace ends to keep the instance (switches to normal pricing).
  - *Expiry:* 30-day trial period + 90-day grace period, then stops if not upgraded to paid.
  - *Gotchas:* Old 'Cloud SQL Always Free plan' (free shared-core instance in select regions, 750 h/month E2-micro/C2D + storage/backup/egress allowances) is NO LONGER listed on the Cloud SQL pricing page — the current official free offer is only this 30-day trial instance; egress and backups are charged once you upgrade; SQL Server trial carries 4-core license minimum rules; instance is 'evaluation' — production use not guaranteed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/sql/docs/mysql/free-trial-instance(offercard:https://cloud.google.com/cloud-sql/pricing)
- **Cloud Spanner Free Trial Instance (90 days, 10 GB)** (high) — Official free Spanner offer today: a 90-day free trial instance, Standard edition features + Spanner Graph, up to 10 GB (GiB) of stored data. In addition to the $300 GCP free-trial credit (new customers) — the trial instance itself costs nothing and does not need the $300. Regional configurations only (any region; NOT dual-region/multi-region). Not meant for production; no backups/restore, no CMEK, no Data Boost; ~500 QPS reads / ~100 QPS writes achievable. After 90 days the instance stops serving and enters a 30-day grace period, then instance + data are permanently deleted unless upgraded to a paid Enterprise instance (upgrade is downtime-free and can be done anytime during trial or grace).
  - *Eligibility:* Both existing and new GCP customers: you need an ACTIVE Cloud Billing account with Cloud Billing enabled on the project (new customers can create one via the $300 trial, which requires a card/payment method). One free trial instance per project lifecycle; maximum five free trial instances per Cloud Billing account. No .edu or startup requirements.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Console → Cloud Spanner → create a 'free trial instance' (console or CLI, per 'Create a free trial instance' docs); 2) pick a regional configuration and region; 3) optionally use the preloaded sample ecommerce database; 4) upgrade to paid Enterprise before the 90-day trial / 30-day grace ends to retain data.
  - *Expiry:* 90-day trial + 30-day grace period; data deleted after that unless upgraded. Re-eligibility: up to 5 trial instances per billing account, 1 per project lifecycle.
  - *Gotchas:* Data is deleted, not retained, after the grace period; read/write QPS caps make it unsuitable for real workloads; region choices are all regional (no multi-region DR); the previous '1 free 96 GB node (us-west1)' always-free allowance is not present on the current pricing page — the trial instance replaced it.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/free-trial-instance(offercard:https://cloud.google.com/spanner/pricing)
- **BigQuery Free Tier (always-free): 1 TiB queries + 10 GiB storage per month, plus free operations** (high) — Current official BigQuery free tier (per billing account, no expiry): the first 1 TiB of query data processed per month is free (on-demand pay-per-query; beyond that $6.25/TiB in US); the first 10 GiB of storage free each month (active and long-term logical/physical). Also free: batch loading from Cloud Storage/local files (uses free shared slot pool 'default-pipeline'), table reads/copies/metadata ops, and the first 2 TiB per month of gRPC Storage Write API (REST streaming inserts cost $0.01/200 MiB beyond). The old '100,000 slot-hours/month free' allowance is not listed on the current BigQuery pricing page. BigQuery free tier is available to all GCP accounts (free-trial or paid) as long as usage stays under the caps.
  - *Eligibility:* Any GCP billing account (free trial or paid) staying under the monthly caps; no card needed beyond trial signup; no .edu/startup requirement; applies per billing account (free query bytes ~1 TiB/month/account).
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim: free tier usage is automatic for any GCP account; use BigQuery normally. During a $300 free trial you also get the Free Tier at the same time. To keep using it after trial without paying: stay within 1 TiB queries + 10 GiB storage per month.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite monthly recurring allowance (no expiry; Google may change limits).
  - *Gotchas:* Free query allowance is per billing account and monthly; streaming inserts via REST are NOT free (only gRPC write API has the 2 TiB allowance); data egress/export to GCS is free but egress to other services/Internet costs; scheduled heavy queries can exceed 1 TiB in a month; the 100K free slot-hours claim found in older docs is no longer published — assume it no longer applies unless you buy editions/slots.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/pricing(freetierlimitsalsoshownonhttps://cloud.google.com/free)
- **Other product-specific free trials on the Google Cloud free program page (data platforms)** (high) — cloud.google.com/free lists additional time-boxed trials that stack on top of the $300 credit: GKE free trial (full product capabilities, 90 days, no cost); AlloyDB for PostgreSQL free trial (30 days; 8 vCPU basic primary instance, up to 1 TB storage capacity); Bigtable free trial (10 days; 1-node SSD cluster, up to 500 GB storage); Looker free trial (30 days, all standard features). Each card notes 'Plus, new Google Cloud customers get $300 in free credits on signup'.
  - *Eligibility:* Trial-eligible per product docs (generally one trial per new project/billing account pattern; an enabled Cloud Billing account is required, which for new customers means the $300 trial signup with payment method). No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* From cloud.google.com/free, click the relevant 'OFFER' card → product console trial creation flow; upgrade to paid before the trial window to keep resources.
  - *Expiry:* Fixed windows: GKE 90 days, AlloyDB 30 days, Bigtable 10 days, Looker 30 days.
  - *Gotchas:* These are evaluation windows (10–90 days), not free tiers; exceeding the stated size or outliving the window requires upgrading to paid; Bigtable 10 days is very short.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program (credits: Start $2,000 / Scale up to $200,000 / Scale AI up to $350,000)** (high) — Replaced the older 'Google Cloud Startups Accelerator' (old /startups now serves this program). Tiers as of today: Start ($2k) — $2,000 in credits, for startups founded in the past 2 years with a working MVP, clear business model, and plans to seek VC funding soon; Scale — Year 1: 100% of GCP spend up to $100,000; Year 2: 20% up to an additional $100,000 (up to $200,000 total), for Pre-Seed to Series A funded startups (founded within last 3–5 years, tier-dependent); Scale AI — same structure but Year 1 up to $250,000 (up to $350,000 total) for startups using Gemini as a foundation of their primary product (Pre-Seed/Seed within last 5 years, Series A within last 12 months, founded within last 5 years). Credits cover most GCP pay-as-you-go products incl. Gemini/Gemma model usage; third-party models (e.g., Anthropic) are billed directly and NOT covered. Additional benefits: 12 months Google Workspace Business Plus at no cost (separate application, requires not having paid Workspace within 31 days of applying), monthly Google Maps Platform credits (separate application), Google Skills training, dedicated Startup Success Managers as you grow.
  - *Eligibility:* VC-funded for Scale tiers: verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding from a known investor within last 3–5 years (tier-dependent); NOT yet received more than $5,000 in GCP credits (non-venture funding — PE, bank loans, grants, crowdfunding, friends-and-family — does NOT qualify for Scale). Exclusions: IPO'd/acquired companies, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, dev shops/consultancies/agencies, crypto mining, token distributors against local rules. Must be in a Google-Cloud-GA region with self-service purchasing; company email domain must match the public website domain; a valid 18-char Cloud Billing account ID is required. Acceptance at Google's sole discretion; not for regulated industries.
  - *How to claim:* 1) cloud.google.com/startup → 'Apply to the program' (AI-focused: cloud.google.com/startup/ai); 2) apply with a company (non-personal) business email matching your website; 3) provide an active 18-character GCP billing account ID; 4) complete onboarding checklist at cloud.google.com/startup/onboarding; 5) on approval credits are deposited to that billing account.
  - *Expiry:* Credits structured as Year-1 (100% up to cap) + Year-2 (20% up to cap) windows over two years; annual re-evaluation of eligibility — prior enrollment doesn't guarantee current-year eligibility; rejected applicants may re-apply later as they raise funding.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit cap 'up to' amounts assume sustained GCP spend; the $350k is AI-tiers only (other startups cap at $200k total); credits cannot be shared, split, or resold (violation terminates program); 're-apply if you got credits before but didn't use them' is allowed only if you still meet in-year requirements; the $5,000-prior-credits ceiling blocks heavy past GCP usage from Scale tiers (a $300 trial is fine). There is also a separate 'Ecosystem tier' (startups in select accelerators/incubators: $25k–$250k credits) per Google's two-pager.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup(AItier:https://cloud.google.com/startup/ai;onboarding:https://cloud.google.com/startup/onboarding)
- **Google for Startups Accelerators (equity-free, region-specific)** (medium) — Global series of regional accelerator programs (Americas, EMEA, APAC; e.g., AI-focused cohorts). Each cohort is 10–15 startups doing intensive mentorship/training from Google experts; benefits: equity-free support, dedicated mentoring, bootcamps, access to Google Cloud credits applied for during the accelerator (subject to eligibility review), free Cloud TPU access for open-source ML research, Early Access Program/Trusted Tester benefits for Google AI products. Regional variants run in partnership with governments/VCs (e.g., Komdigi Indonesia May 2025: up to $350k credits upon graduation; Singapore 'AI First').
  - *Eligibility:* Startups demonstrating traction, ideally Seed to Series A, deeply technical (AI/ML strongly preferred in current cohorts), scalable product, commitment from CTO/technical team to required sessions; selection at Google's discretion per regional program. No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* 1) startup.google.com/programs/accelerator → find your regional accelerator; 2) apply to the cohort (deadline-driven, limited seats); 3) participate in program sessions; 4) apply for Cloud credits during the accelerator; 5) TPU access via program.
  - *Expiry:* Cohort-based (e.g., 10–15 weeks / 3 months); credits and TPU access tied to program participation and eligibility review; no standing monthly free tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Seats are competitive and now effectively AI-focused (2025 cohorts are ~100% AI-native/AI-enabled startups); availability varies by region and year; credits are not automatic — applied for and subject to approval.
  - *Source:* https://startup.google.com/programs/accelerator/
- **Google Cloud for Education — Faculty/teaching credits (up to $100 per teacher, $50 per student)** (high) — Verified faculty can apply for Google Cloud teaching credits: up to $100 in GCP credits per teaching staff member plus up to $50 in GCP credits per student, for hands-on classroom learning. Credits are redeemed and managed in a billing account; guides cover billing management, budgets, group projects pooling student credits. Faculty community (verified faculty) access via school-issued email.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified faculty/teaching staff at educational institutions; school-issued email address used; students then use their individually allocated credits instead of signing up for the credit-card Free Trial.
  - *How to claim:* 1) cloud.google.com/edu/faculty → 'Apply for teaching credits'; 2) verify faculty identity/school affiliation; 3) on approval, receive/manage credits in your Cloud Billing account; 4) allocate student credits for classroom use.
  - *Expiry:* Issued education credits have expiry/renewal governed by the education credits program (re-apply per program cycle); exact credit shelf-life is set in the application/FAQs.
  - *Gotchas:* Amounts are modest ($100 + $50/student) — designed for learning, not production; faculty must verify before students get credits; Google explicitly advises preventing students from signing up for the $300 Free Trial (which requires a credit card) when they have education credits.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty
- **Google Cloud for Education — Students (200 Google Skills credits, no GCP dollar credits)** (high) — Student offer today is learning-focused, not dollar credits: 200 free Google Skills credits (online labs, skill badges, courses, Google Cloud Certificates) that expire one year after issuance; Google Skills Arcade (gamified challenges, badges, swag); Google Cloud Certificates (shareable digital credentials, no prerequisites); Developer Student Clubs (DSGs) and Cloud OnAir events. There is no general GCP dollar-credit free license for students on these pages — students instead get faculty-allocated credits or the standard $300 trial (which needs a card).
  - *Eligibility:* Enrolled students/learners; request access to Google Skills; .edu or school affiliation typically used for DSG chapters; no credit card required for the learning credits.
  - *How to claim:* 1) cloud.google.com/edu/students → 'Request credits' for Google Skills; 2) complete labs/paths to earn badges; 3) optionally join a Developer Student Club or apply to start one; 4) earn Google Cloud Certificates for credentials.
  - *Expiry:* 200 Skills credits expire 1 year after issuance; program is renewable/re-requestable.
  - *Gotchas:* If the goal was free GCP dollar credits for students, they don't exist outside faculty-allocated teaching credits (up to $50/student) and the standard $300 Free Trial (credit card required); the student offer has shifted to 'Google Skills' (rebranded Cloud Skills Boost) credits.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Cloud for Researchers — GPAR research credits ($5,000)** (high) — cloud.google.com/edu/researchers: researchers apply for $5,000 in Google Cloud research credits. GPAR (Google Program for Accelerated Research, via Google Public Sector) adds: co-investment opportunities with industry-leading pricing for AI accelerators (GPUs/TPUs), early access to new-gen TPUs, foundational models and datasets (AlphaFold, Earth AI, agentic AI like Co-scientist), grant-lifecycle AI tooling, compliance support (HIPAA, CMMC 2.0, FedRAMP), and Google/expert access. Noted on the page as currently only available in the United States.
  - *Eligibility:* Researchers at research institutions (US for GPAR); institutional/academic affiliation; application-based via cloud.google.com/edu/researchers; GPAR accelerator benefits noted as US-only.
  - *How to claim:* 1) cloud.google.com/edu/researchers → 'Apply for your credits'; 2) submit research-credit application (institutional context); 3) for US institutions, engage GPAR benefits (co-investment pricing, early TPU access, on-campus research days).
  - *Expiry:* Applied per program cycle; $5,000 credit issuance and validity governed by the program; GPAR benefits note US-only availability as of today.
  - *Gotchas:* US-only for GPAR; $5,000 is per researcher application, not an org-wide license; heavy HPC/TPU workloads go far beyond $5,000 — co-investment/pricing options are where the real scale is.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/researchers
- **GCP Free Trial — $300 credit, 90 days** (high) — New-customer Welcome credit of $300, valid 90 days from signup, in a preloaded Free Trial billing account; not billed unless the $300 is exhausted; trial access to products may be limited to prevent abuse. During the trial the customer also has access to the Always Free tier. Verified on the official docs page read 2026-10-22: 'The Free Trial gets you a $300 Welcome credit to spend over 90 days... you won't be billed' and 'preloaded with $300 in free Welcome credit which is valid for 90 days'.
  - *Eligibility:* Must have never been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and must not have previously signed up for the Free Trial (effectively one trial per person/company; students qualify the same way). A credit card or other valid payment method IS required at signup (bank-account verification may also be needed in some countries). No .edu requirement, no region stated on the page. The 'credit card NOT required' claim is FALSE for the trial: signup docs state 'you must provide a credit card or other payment method that is valid for the period of the Free Trial'.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to cloud.google.com/free (or Console) and start the Free Trial; 2) add a valid payment method; 3) $300 is preloaded into the trial billing account; 4) optionally upgrade to a Paid billing account during the trial — unused credit is then retained until 90 days from signup.
  - *Expiry:* Credit expires 90 days from signup. If you do not upgrade to a Paid billing account before 90 days, or spend the whole $300, the Free Trial billing account is closed and all its projects/resources are stopped (i.e., your VPS dies and you lose unspent credit). Free Tier access continues afterwards only with an active billing account.
  - *Gotchas:* Card required even if usage is $0 (temporary authorization hold on signup). Trial end = account closure + stopped resources, not a silent conversion. $300 counts toward the '<$5,000 previously received GCP credits' cap of the startup program. One trial per customer (never a paying GCP/Maps/Firebase user).
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **GCP Always Free tier — free VPS (e2-micro) + 20+ products** (high) — Free-forever monthly quotas (no expiration, subject to change). VPS-relevant Compute Engine: 1 non-preemptible e2-micro VM per month in us-west1 (Oregon), us-central1 (Iowa) or us-east1 (South Carolina), plus 30 GB-months standard persistent disk, plus 1 GB/month outbound egress from North America (excl. China/Australia). VM limit is by time, not instance count; GPUs/TPUs are never free. Other verified quotas: Cloud Run 2M requests/mo + 360,000 GB-seconds memory + 180,000 vCPU-seconds + 1 GB North America egress; Cloud Run Functions 2M invocations + 400,000 GB-seconds + 5 GB egress; Cloud Shell 5 GB persistent disk; Cloud Source Repos 5 users/50 GB; BigQuery 1 TiB query + 10 GiB storage; Cloud Build 2,500 build-minutes; Artifact Registry 0.5 GB; Firestore 1 GiB storage + 50k reads/20k writes/20k deletes per day + 10 GiB egress; GKE 1 free Autopilot or zonal Standard cluster per month (credit excludes compute/networking); Pub/Sub 10 GiB messages; reCAPTCHA 10k; Cloud Vision 1,000 units; Cloud Natural Language 5,000 units; Cloud Monitoring API 1M time series; Cloud Storage 5 GB-months Standard (free-tier benefits only in the 3 US regions); Datastream 100 GiB CDC (AlloyDB/Spanner→BigQuery only).
  - *Eligibility:* Active billing account (Paid OR Free Trial) in good standing, and no negotiated pricing contract. A payment method is effectively required because the billing account comes from card signup (trial) or a paid account; no .edu or age requirement. No per-country limit stated, but free Compute/Storage capacity is limited to the 3 US regions.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up for GCP (Free Trial or paid account), create a project, launch an e2-micro VM in us-west1/us-central1/us-east1 with ≤30 GB standard pd-SSD — usage within the quotas is never billed. No separate activation step.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; quotas refresh monthly and 'do not expire, but are subject to change'. Free VM regions are fixed (us-west1, us-central1, us-east1 only).
  - *Gotchas:* e2-micro is a 2 vCPU / 1 GB burstable micro instance — hobby VPS, not production. 30 GB disk + 1 GB egress are small; egress beyond 1 GB/month is billed. Stopped-VM disk still consumes the 30 GB-months quota. Free GKE cluster credit does not cover node compute or networking. No free bare-metal or dedicated-server option exists at GCP at all — the e2-micro (or Cloud Run) is the only 'free server'.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — up to $350,000 GCP credits over 2 years (AI & non-AI tiers)** (high) — Successor to the old 'Cloud Startups' program, verified on startup.google.com/cloud (read 2026-10-22): headline 'up to $350,000 in Cloud credits over two years'. AI startups: Year 1 = 100% of GCP spend up to $250,000; Year 2 = 20% of Year-2 spend, up to an additional $100,000 in credits. Non-AI startups: Year 1 = 100% up to $100,000; Year 2 = 20% up to $100,000. Third-party model spend (e.g. Anthropic models on Vertex) is billed directly and NOT covered. Credits may only be used by the original applicant — no sharing/splitting/reselling. Non-credit benefits in the program: 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus at no cost (domain must not have had a paid Workspace plan in the prior 31 days), monthly Google Maps Platform credits (separate application), Google Skills training, dedicated Startup Success Managers/Customer Engineering, and Google Cloud Marketplace co-sell.
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-Seed to Series A stage; founded within the last 3-5 years; previously received less than $5,000 in Google Cloud credits; digital-native startup with a scalable proprietary product, a working MVP, and a verified company domain; company email matching the public website; valid GCP account + Billing ID required. Excluded: companies that IPO'd or were acquired, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs, dev shops/consultancies/agencies, crypto mining. No US-registered-entity or .edu requirement is stated on the current page (the legacy program was US-centric).
  - *How to claim:* Apply at cloud.google.com/startup/apply with your company email that matches your website; have a GCP account + Billing ID ready; pass program review; credits are applied to your billing account and usable for the Year-1 window plus the Year-2 top-up.
  - *Expiry:* Two-year structure: 100% coverage window in Year 1 (up to the tier cap), then a Year-2 top-up of 20% of spend up to $100,000. Unused Year-1 credit is not carried into Year 2.
  - *Gotchas:* The '<$5,000 previously received GCP credits' cap means heavy trial/legacy-program users can be disqualified. 'Founded within the last 3-5 years' is tier-dependent — older startups may not qualify. Third-party (non-Google) model spend is out of scope. Credits cannot be resold or shared with partners.
  - *Source:* https://startup.google.com/cloud/
- **Gemini API free tier (Google AI Studio) — no credit card required** (medium) — Free tier of the Gemini API / Google AI Studio, verified at ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits (2026-10-22): qualification = 'Active project or free trial' (i.e., no billing account and no credit card needed) with no spend cap; paid tiers are Tier 1 ($250 cap, active billing account), Tier 2 ($2,000 cap, $100 + 3 days from first payment), Tier 3 (higher, $1,000 + 30 days). Free usage is rate-limited per model on RPM (requests/min), TPM (input tokens/min) and RPD (requests/day, reset midnight PT). Available to students and to bootstrapped/self-funded startups — the startup program also advertises 'free access to the Gemini API in Google AI Studio' on its Prototype track. Not a VPS offer, but a distinct card-free free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Google account that creates a GCP project; no payment method, no .edu, no startup status required. Students: same access.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in at aistudio.google.com (or use the API key from AI Studio) — free-tier rate limits apply automatically; no billing setup.
  - *Expiry:* No expiration; limits are per-minute/per-day and may change by model. Exact per-model free RPM/TPM/RPD numbers are in a JS-rendered table I could not extract today — treat specific figures as unverified.
  - *Gotchas:* Free tier excludes batch/long-context features of paid tiers; spend-based limits only protect paid accounts. Do not confuse 'free tier' with the $300 trial (which does require a card).
  - *Source:* https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits
- **Product-specific free trials (GKE, Cloud SQL, Spanner, AlloyDB, Bigtable)** (high) — On cloud.google.com/free (read 2026-10-22), new-customer product trials beyond the $300 credit: GKE — 90 days, full capabilities, no fee (fleet-based team management, GitOps config, GKE on hybrid/multicloud); Cloud SQL — 30-day trial of an 8 vCPU Enterprise Plus instance, 64 GB memory, 100 GB storage; Spanner — 90-day trial instance with 10 GB storage; AlloyDB for PostgreSQL — 30-day trial, 8 vCPU basic primary instance, up to 1 TB storage capacity; Bigtable — 10-day trial, 1-node SSD cluster, up to 500 GB. Each states 'new Google Cloud customers get $300 in free credits on signup' alongside.
  - *Eligibility:* New Google Cloud customers (same trial-eligibility rules: never a paying GCP/Maps/Firebase user; card required for the underlying account). No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Create the product instance from the product's console quickstart / the 'Free Trial' callouts on cloud.google.com/free; the trial period runs from instance creation.
  - *Expiry:* Fixed per-product windows (10-90 days) as listed; after expiry the instance converts to standard pay-as-you-go billing.
  - *Gotchas:* These are trial periods on paid products, not free tiers — after the window you are billed in full. GKE trial cluster is a different thing from the '1 free GKE cluster per month' Always Free allowance (that one is free forever but excludes compute/networking charges).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Education / student offers: no dedicated GCP compute credits or education license** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — As of 2026-10-22, GCP has no student-only free compute credits or education license: students get the same $300/90-day trial (card required), the Always Free tier, and the card-free Gemini API free tier. Google's education-related cloud offering is Google Cloud Skills Boost (free skills/training platform referenced by partner pages; the startup program's perk list says 'training via Google Skills'). NOTE: the historical domain skills.cloud.google.com failed to resolve (DNS) today, suggesting it was renamed or retired — the free-learning offering now appears to live under 'Google Skills' / cloud.google.com/learn; not fully verified.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — no .edu-gated GCP compute offer exists.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim beyond the standard trial/free-tier/Gemini-API routes above.
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse free 'learning' access with free 'compute' access; certification fee waivers (when offered) are unrelated to VPS usage.
  - *Source:* https://startup.google.com/cloud/
- **GCP Free Trial: $300 Welcome credit for 90 days** (high) — Free Trial billing account is preloaded with a $300 Welcome credit, valid for 90 days, spendable on any Google Cloud products (including Compute Engine VMs as VPS). You are not billed for any usage during the trial. If you upgrade to a Paid billing account you keep unused credit until it expires 90 days from signup; if you neither upgrade nor spend the $300, the trial billing account is closed and all associated projects/resources are stopped.
  - *Eligibility:* New customers only: you must never have been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and must not have previously signed up for the Free Trial. One trial per person/payment method. A valid credit card or other payment method is REQUIRED at signup (plus bank-account verification in some countries) even though you aren't billed during the trial.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to cloud.google.com/free (or console.cloud.google.com/freetrial). 2) Create a Google account + GCP project. 3) Add a credit card/payment method and complete identity verification. 4) Trial starts after verification; $300 credit and 90-day clock begin. No card is charged during the trial.
  - *Expiry:* Credit and trial both last 90 days from signup. Unused credit expires 90 days from trial signup even if you convert to paid. After that, account is closed (projects stopped) unless converted to a paid account before expiry.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required (unlike AWS, you cannot get the GCP trial without a payment method; Google runs an authorization hold to validate it). $300 burns fast on VMs (e.g. an e2-micro VPS for 90 days costs only ~$10-15, but anything bigger eats it quickly). Trial ends -> resources stopped, data at risk: upgrade before day 90 or export/stop instances. The trial credit does NOT cover Always-Free tier usage; after trial, usage outside the Free Tier is billed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free/docs/gcp-free-tier
- **Compute Engine Always-Free tier: 1 e2-micro VM + 30 GB disk, perpetual (no card required)** (high) — Free Tier, no expiration, available to all GCP customers: 1 non-preemptible e2-micro VM instance per month (1 vCPU / 1 GB RAM), time-based limit - free until cumulative hours equal the total hours in the month; usable in us-west1 (Oregon), us-central1 (Iowa), or us-east1 (South Carolina), used combined across those regions. 30 GB-months standard persistent disk. 1 GB/month outbound egress from North America to all destinations except China/Australia. Related free items on the same page: Cloud Storage 5 GB regional storage, Cloud Run 2M requests/mo, App Engine F1 + 9 h/day B1, Cloud Build, Cloud Functions. GPUs/TPUs are never free.
  - *Eligibility:* Any GCP customer (including trial users, post-trial, or accounts that skip the trial - the Free Tier applies as long as you use products within the monthly limits). A billing account must exist; the Always-Free usage itself does not consume trial credit. Region lock-in: e2-micro free limit only in the 3 US regions; you can still place a paid VM anywhere.
  - *How to claim:* Create a GCP project (billing account, no card needed if you stay within free tier; a trial or a card is only needed the moment you exceed limits), go to Compute Engine -> Create VM, pick e2-micro in us-west1/us-central1/us-east1, attach a 30 GB or smaller standard PD, and run it - compute+disk+1 GB egress stay $0 each month indefinitely.
  - *Expiry:* No expiration - Free Tier has no stated end date, limits reset monthly. Google reserves the right to change/eliminate usage limits with 30 days' notice.
  - *Gotchas:* The '1 TB egress/mo' in the task's known-info is WRONG: official limit is 1 GB/mo outbound from NA (China/AU excluded). The limit is by hours, not by instance - running multiple e2-micros in parallel still costs $0 up to full-month hours. Disk must be STANDARD type, 30 GB or less. Egress >1 GB, GPU/TPU, larger disks, non-US regions for e2-micro, and any usage over the time limit bill at standard rates. A billing account without a payment method: free tier usage is permitted, but exceeding it will suspend service until a card is added.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free/docs/gcp-free-tier
- **GKE Free Trial: 90 days of full Kubernetes Engine capabilities at no fee** (medium) — Separate from the $300 trial: a GKE trial card on cloud.google.com/free - 'Try GKE with the full set of product capabilities for 90 days for no fee', deep-linked to console.cloud.google.com/freetrial?redirectPath=/kubernetes/overview. Use it to run a managed Kubernetes cluster (worker VMs) free for 90 days; useful as a VPS/container-hosting front.
  - *Eligibility:* GCP account holders (trial signup path); subject to abuse prevention - 'Access to products may be limited to prevent abuse'. New-customer trial rules apply for the $300 companion credit.
  - *How to claim:* From cloud.google.com/free, click the GKE Free Trial card (or console.cloud.google.com/freetrial?redirectPath=/kubernetes), complete trial signup with a card, and create a GKE Autopilot/Standard cluster for 90 days without paying.
  - *Expiry:* 90-day window from signup; after that standard GKE pricing applies (control-plane fee, node hours).
  - *Gotchas:* Requires the same card-verified trial signup; GKE Autopilot still charges per-node-hour for the 90-day trial at list price after the window; the free GKE *tier* (always-free cluster) also exists but only the cluster fee, not compute/networking. If your real goal is a cheap VPS, raw Compute Engine e2-micro is simpler than GKE.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program (rebranded/expanded GCP for Startups): $2K Start tier, $200K-$350K Scale/Scale AI tiers** (high) — Official page cloud.google.com/startup ('Google for Startups Cloud Program'). Tiers: Start = $2K in credits for startups founded within the past 2 years with a working MVP, clear business model, and plans to seek VC funding. Funded tiers 'Scale' and 'Scale AI' range $200K-$350K in GCP credits for growing startups with verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding, founded within the last 3-5 years. Footnote structure on the page: AI startups - Year 1: 100% coverage up to $250,000 GCP credits; Year 2: 20% coverage up to an additional $100,000. Other startups - Year 1: 100% up to $100,000; Year 2: 20% up to an additional $100,000. Also includes Startup Success Managers, Customer Engineering support, and AI/DeepMind build benefits; a separate Maps Platform benefit exists (separate application).
  - *Eligibility:* Startups: founded within 2 years (Start) or 3-5 years with Pre-Seed to Series A funding (Scale/Scale AI). Exclusions: companies that have IPO'd or been acquired, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content, dev shops, consultancies/agencies, cryptocurrency mining companies, or token-distributing companies. Acceptance at Google's sole discretion. No .edu email or revenue cap stated - 'funding stage' (Pre-Seed to Series A) is the cap. No funding needed for the $2K Start tier.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the 'Apply to the program' button on cloud.google.com/startup (links to cloud.google.com/startup/apply). Fill in company info, stage, funding, website. Stealth (funded) founders: get your accelerator/VC to verify/referral you; unfunded stealth founders must wait to apply until product launch with a live website. If accepted, credits apply to a GCP billing account; check 'Billing -> Cloud Credits' in console for balance/expiry. Third-party (partner) model usage is billed directly, not covered.
  - *Expiry:* Year-1 credits (100% coverage up to the tier cap) then Year-2 credits at 20% up to $100K additional. A separate footnoted rule: your company domain must not have been on a paid Google Workspace plan within 31 days of applying. Exact per-tier dollar caps beyond '$200K-$350K' and credit validity windows are not spelled out on the page I read - treat specific amounts as up-to tier cap.
  - *Gotchas:* The page's marketing ('Up to $350k') and the footnote caps ($250K AI / $100K non-AI Y1 + 20%/$100K Y2) don't fully reconcile - confirm the exact amount in your acceptance. Credits are for GCP products, not cash; third-party model calls bypass the credit. Not available to your own personal account - must be the startup's billing account. Old 'GCP for Startups' URLs (cloud.google.com/programs/startup, /boost) 404; use cloud.google.com/startup (gcp-startups still resolves, 200).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup
- **Google Cloud for Education - Faculty Teaching Credits: up to $100/staff + up to $50/student** (high) — From cloud.google.com/edu/faculty + edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching: eligible faculty (university faculty, not students) can apply for GCP credits to give students hands-on experience: up to $100 in Google Cloud credits per teaching staff member and up to $50 in Cloud credits per student, for up to 3 courses per application. The faculty page also links 'Manage Google Cloud credits' and 'redeem education credits' guides.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty

- **Google Cloud Free Trial: $300 credit, 90 days (one-time, new customers)** (high) — One-time $300 welcome credit valid for 90 days; the account is a non-billable free-trial account during that window. If the $300 is spent or 90 days pass without upgrading to a paid billing account, the account auto-closes; a 30-day grace period then allows recovery of stopped resources/data before permanent deletion. GPUs/TPUs cannot be added on a non-billable trial account; the $300 cannot pay Gemini API-in-AI-Studio costs or managed generative-AI partner-model (Model-as-a-Service) usage.
