  - *How to claim:* 1) cloud.google.com/free → select the free trial. 2) Create a Google Cloud account and enter a credit card (verification only; $0.00–$1.00 auth hold, not charged during trial). 3) A new GCP project is created; the $300 applies to eligible pay-as-you-go products for 90 days. 4) Before day 90, upgrade to a paid billing account to keep the account open.
  - *Expiry:* Credit valid 90 days from account creation, not renewable; 30-day grace period after expiry; the always-free tier continues indefinitely on the account's products.
  - *Gotchas:* CC required despite the free label; GPUs/TPUs not allowed on the non-billable trial account; Gemini API costs and managed partner AI models excluded from the $300; unused credit lapses at 90 days; after the grace period resources are stopped and data deleted.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-trial(redirectedfromcloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-trial)
- **Google Cloud Always-Free tier (free forever, no credit card, no trial needed)** (high) — Indefinite, no-card free allotments across 20+ products; no end date but Google can change limits with 30 days' notice; unused monthly limits do not roll over. Compute Engine: 1 e2-micro (non-preemptible, 2 shared vCPU / 1 GB RAM) per month + 30 GB-months standard persistent disk + 1 external IP free + 1 GB outbound transfer/month — only in us-west1 (Oregon), us-central1 (Iowa), us-east1 (South Carolina); all eligible hours across those 3 US regions are free up to the month's total hours. Cloud Storage: 5 GB-months regional storage (US regions only), 5,000 Class A + 50,000 Class B operations, 100 GB outbound from North America to any region except China/Australia. Cloud Run: 2M requests/month, 360,000 GiB-s memory, 180,000 vCPU-s compute, 1 GB outbound. Cloud Functions (1st gen): 2M invocations/month, 400,000 GB-s, 5 GB outbound. BigQuery: 1 TiB queries/month free. GPUs and TPUs are explicitly NOT in the free tier — 'you are always charged for GPUs and TPUs that you add to VM instances.' Verified 2026-10-22.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Google Cloud account (including trial accounts); no credit card required for the free allotments themselves. Region limits: free Compute VM and GCS storage are US-3-regions-only; egress exclusions apply to China and Australia destinations.
  - *How to claim:* No claiming step: free-tier allotments apply automatically to eligible products/regions; create a GCP project and stay under the caps. For e2-micro: pick one of us-west1 / us-central1 / us-east1, instance e2-micro, standard persistent disk ≤30GB total, one external IP.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry (indefinite); monthly caps reset each month with no rollover; Google may change limits with 30 days' notice.
  - *Gotchas:* Only 1 free e2-micro per account per month, only in the 3 US regions; any other machine type, region, GPU/TPU, or extra disk is billed; outbound beyond 1 GB/100 GB quotas is billed; the GCP project still has a billing account with a payment method attached, so a cent over any cap hits the card — set budgets/alerts. Free tier is 'always free'; the time-limited product trials below are separate.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-tier-features
- **Time-limited product trials bundled with the free tier (Spanner 90d, Cloud SQL 30d, AlloyDB 30d, Looker 30d, etc.)** (high) — Separate, time-boxed product trials listed alongside the free tier: Spanner 90-day, Cloud SQL 30-day, AlloyDB 30-day, Looker 30-day; they require a billing account (unlike the always-free allotments which don't). These cover database/BI experiments for 30–90 days without per-use cost. The broader 'free $300 and 20+ free products' hub is cloud.google.com/free. Note: I read these durations from the official free-tier docs today; per-product trial terms (auto-renew, conversion) were not individually re-verified — treat specifics as medium.
  - *Eligibility:* Requires a Google Cloud billing account (payment method on file). One trial per product/account.
  - *How to claim:* From a GCP project, enable/try the product (e.g., Cloud SQL) during its trial window via the console; the trial covers normal usage for the stated days; ensure a billing account with a payment method is in place beforehand.
  - *Expiry:* Spanner 90 days; Cloud SQL/AlloyDB/Looker 30 days each; not renewable (re-apply rules vary per product).
  - *Gotchas:* After the window, full pay-as-you-go applies; Cloud SQL/AlloyDB can get expensive (instance + storage + egress), so terminate or set budgets before expiry; trials need a billing account where the free-tier items don't.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-tier-features
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program (up to $350,000 GCP credits over 2 years + 12 months Google Workspace Business Plus + monthly Google Maps Platform credits)** (high) — Successor of the old 'Google Cloud Startup Program'. Tiers per the live page (fetched 2026-10-22): Start tier = $2,000 credits for startups founded in the past 2 years with a working MVP and plans to seek venture funding. Funded tiers = Scale and Scale AI, $200K–$350K total credits, for startups with verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding (founded within the last 3–5 years). Footnoted math: AI startups get 100% of GCP spend up to $250,000 in Year 1 and 20% (matching) up to an additional $100,000 in Year 2 (max $350K); non-AI startups get 100% up to $100,000 Year 1 and 20% up to $100,000 Year 2. Perks also include credits for frontier models like Gemini and Gemma (third-party models in Model Garden/Marketplace billed directly), Google Skills training, Startup Success Managers + Customer Engineering, and 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus at no cost (separate application; company domain must not have had a paid Workspace plan within 31 days before applying; a separate Maps application is required for monthly Google Maps Platform credits). Credits cover most Google-owned pay-as-you-go GCP products; subscription/prepay products and 3P Marketplace/Model Garden items are excluded. Acceptance is discretionary (funding stage/type, business model, founder experience, product usage, vertical, age, growth); response in 5–7 days. Non-VC funding (private equity, bank loans, government programs, grants, crowdfunding, friends & family) does not qualify for the funded tiers; re-application is allowed if prior credits went unused and current-year criteria are met.
  - *Eligibility:* For-profit, digital-native company; not IPO'd/acquired; excludes educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content, dev shops, consultancies, agencies, crypto-mining companies, and token distributors in violation of local regulation; must operate in a region where GCP is GA and self-service. Funded tiers: verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding from a known investor, founded within the last 3–5 years. Requirements: GCP billing account ID (18 chars), business email whose domain matches the company website domain, active website (heavily AI-generated content screened and may be rejected). The application itself is free and needs no credit card, but a GCP billing account — which normally carries a payment method — is the delivery vehicle for the credits. Credits are non-transferable and cannot be shared, split, or resold; doing so terminates enrollment.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Prepare the 18-character GCP billing account ID, a business email whose domain matches the company website domain, and a live, non-placeholder company website. 2) Apply at cloud.google.com/startups ('Apply now'), pick tier (Start $2k, or Scale/Scale AI if funded; stealth founders via investor/VC referral). 3) Wait 5–7 days for the automated accept/reject email (check spam). 4) Credits deposit into the selected billing account; request the Workspace and Maps benefits separately.
  - *Expiry:* Credits granted over 2 years: Year 1 100% (up to $250K AI / $100K other), Year 2 20% matching up to an additional $100K; Workspace benefit = 12 months; program qualifications change annually and previous enrollment does not guarantee re-enrollment.
  - *Gotchas:* Funded tiers require VC/known-investor funding — non-venture money disqualifies you from Scale; company email domain must equal the website domain or extra proof is requested; third-party models and subscription-billed products are excluded; past GCP credit usage is a (discretionary) acceptance factor; don't expect the generic $300 trial to auto-upgrade into this program — it is a separate application; AI-generated-heavy websites are screened and can cause rejection.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startups
- **Google for Education faculty ('teaching') credits: up to $100 per teaching staff + up to $50 per student, for classroom use** (high) — Faculty of accredited institutions can apply for GCP credits for themselves and students so students get hands-on classroom experience. Verified numbers from cloud.google.com/edu/faculty: up to $100 per teaching staff member and up to $50 per student in Cloud credits. Terms (from the official application form, read today): up to 3 courses per application; course start date no later than 1 year from application; credits redeemable from approval until 16 weeks after the course start date, and valid up to 1 year after the course start date; confirmation emails come from CloudEduGrants@google.com (whitelist that alias); group projects can pool students' individual credits. Related: 'Career Launchpad Program' gives students access to Google Cloud Certificates/credentials content.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty only (students should have their faculty apply) at an accredited institution; official school-issued email with a valid .edu/.ac domain required (a nominated alternate email is possible — credits attach to it and are non-transferable); available only in the listed ~65 countries (incl. US, UK, EU, India, Japan, Brazil, others). No credit card required — these are promotional education credits, distinct from the paid-card $300 trial.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Faculty complete the form at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/faculty). 2) Provide the official school email, faculty directory link, institution + department. 3) Per course (up to 3): course name, expected start date, abstract + how credits will be used, a URL that proves you teach the course, student email domain(s), expected student/TA counts. 4) Submit; only the most recent submission is reviewed; on approval, credits attach to the institution's email domains.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid up to 1 year after the course start date; must be redeemed by 16 weeks after course start (from approval); non-transferable, educational use only.
  - *Gotchas:* Google's own faculty docs explicitly warn: keep students off the $300 Free Trial (it requires a credit card) — use these course credits instead; country list is closed; resubmitting a form replaces the previous submission (the most recent is the one reviewed); multiple sections of the same course in the same period count as one course.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/facultyandhttps://edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching/
- **Google for Education research credits ('Academic Research Grants'): up to $5,000 in GCP credits for 1 year; plus GPAR collaboration program** (high) — For accredited higher-education and eligible nonprofit research institutions: up to $5,000 in Google Cloud credits (cloud.google.com/edu/researchers says 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'). Terms from the official application form (read today): credit must be activated within 60 days of the project start date; it expires 365 days from the coupon redemption date or when the amount is fully used; non-transferable, no commercial value or commercial use; faculty accept on the institution's behalf, PhD students only for the described project. Related: GPAR (Google Public Sector — Program for Accelerated Research) gives expert/researcher collaboration from Google + partners and on-campus research days (a services program, not a credit pool).
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty, PhD students, and postdoctoral researchers from accredited higher-education institutions or eligible nonprofit research institutions, in the approved-country list (~65 countries; undergrad/master students are NOT eligible for research grants). Institution email preferred. No credit card required for the credits themselves (a GCP billing account ID is still needed to receive them).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Fill the form at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/researchers). 2) Provide job title (faculty/postdoc/PhD/IT admin), organization, directory or candidacy link, project name + start date, a 250-word proposal (Google Doc link or paste) covering problem, GCP tools, timeline, milestones, and future GCP use, a field of research, a pricing-calculator estimate with its URL, a GCP billing account ID, and your post-credit funding plan. 3) On approval, redeem within 60 days of the project start date.
  - *Expiry:* Redeem within 60 days of project start; valid 365 days from redemption or until $5,000 used, whichever first; renewal not guaranteed.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are for the described project only — no personal or commercial use; you must declare how you'll fund the project after the credit expires; proposals without a GCP cost estimate get bounced; GPAR is a separate expert-collaboration program, not a second credit pool.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/researchersandhttps://edu.google.com/programs/credits/research/
- **Free student learning offers: 200 Google Skills credits + free hands-on labs, skill badges, Google Cloud Certificates (preview), Google Skills Arcade, Developer Student Groups** (medium) — cloud.google.com/edu/students (read today): students can request 200 free Google Skills credits which expire one year after issue; no-cost access to hands-on labs; skill badges (complete a lab series + challenge-lab final assessment for a shareable credential); Google Cloud Certificates (preview free on YouTube, no prerequisites); Google Skills Arcade by Google Cloud (gamified learning, swag rewards); free Developer Student Groups (join or create a campus chapter); free Cloud OnAir events and YouTube tutorials. There is no .edu-gated GCP compute-credit pool for individual students — students' actual GCP compute comes from faculty teaching credits, the generic $300 trial (CC required), or institutional agreements; the notebook/lab front on GCP therefore runs through faculty credits or trial, while self-serve free lab environments come from Google Skills' sized sandboxes. Confidence is medium on exactly what 200 Skills credits purchase: the page does not publish a per-course price list.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to students (no .edu email explicitly required to request the Google Skills learning credits or badges; DSGs are campus-based). The $300 trial and free tier remain available to students but the trial needs a card. Google's own faculty docs steer students toward faculty-issued credits instead of the trial.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to cloud.google.com/edu/students → 'Request credits' for the 200 Google Skills credits (learning-platform credits; expire 1 year after issue). 2) Use Google Skills to open the free hands-on labs and earn skill badges. 3) Join/apply to a Developer Student Group or attend Cloud OnAir events (both free). 4) For real GCP compute in coursework, use your faculty's teaching credits or a campus institutional agreement.
  - *Expiry:* Skills credits expire 1 year after issue; labs/badges are free as long as offered.
  - *Gotchas:* '200 Google Skills credits' are learning-platform credits, not GCP $-credits — don't conflate with the $300 trial; Skills GCP sandboxes are time-boxed/limited; there is no free GCP GPU/TPU access for students (GPUs/TPUs are explicitly outside the free tier).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Gemini API / Google AI Studio free tier (the ML-inference + notebooks front; no credit card)** (medium) — ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits (read today; page 'Last updated 2026-10-15 UTC'): usage tiers are Free, then paid Tiers 1–3 (with $10/$50/$200 rolling 10-minute spend caps and escalating limits). Free tier qualification: 'an active project or free trial' — no active billing account required, in contrast to Tier 1+ which require a linked billing account. Per-model free RPD/RPM/TPM numbers are not published on that page; they are shown per-project in the Google AI Studio rate-limit dashboard. Batch API free-tier table starts at Tier 1 (no free batch numbers shown). No free fine-tuning/training credits are listed on the page. Related: the GCP $300 trial credit cannot pay Gemini API-in-AI-Studio costs (trial docs), and the startup program's 'prototyping/Start' offering gives pre-funded builders instant AI Studio access (API keys + Gemini, Gemini Omni, Nano Banana, etc.) plus 'credits for frontier models like Gemini and Gemma' in funded tiers, while third-party partner models are billed directly. Google Colab remains the classic free notebooks front (CPU and quota-limited TPU runtimes), but its current exact quotas could not be re-verified in this session (web-search budget was exhausted) — treat remembered Colab numbers as low confidence.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Google account; the Gemini API free tier needs no credit card and no billing account (Tier 1+ does). GCP trial users: AI Studio Gemini costs are excluded from the $300 trial credit.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign in at aistudio.google.com (or colab.research.google.com for notebooks). 2) Create/select a project, generate an API key, pick a model — free-tier quotas apply automatically. 3) Check per-model free limits in the AI Studio rate-limit dashboard; exceeding them moves you to paid tiers (which require a billing account).
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiry for the free tier (ongoing, subject to Google's policy changes).
  - *Gotchas:* Free-tier rates are per-project, per-model and can change; the dashboard is the source of truth; no free batch API at the Free tier; no free fine-tuning stated; Colab TPU sessions are quota/time-limited — don't assume old Colab quotas are current; past free limits, paid tier 1 applies its $10 rolling spend-cap structure rather than unlimited use.
  - *Source:* https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits
- **Google Cloud Free Trial: $300 credit for 90 days (new customers)** (high) — Signup creates a Free Trial billing account preloaded with a $300 Welcome credit valid for 90 days (cloud.google.com/free; signup FAQ). The trial ends at the first of: $300 spent, 90 days elapsed, or upgrade to a paid account (Free Trial Terms section 1.3, last modified 2026-02-12). Free Tier usage is not charged against the credit, and the credit can cover usage above Free Tier limits. On the trial, Compute Engine is capped at 8 concurrent cores (section 3.1) and some services are unavailable (section 3.3). Upgrading keeps unused credit, which still expires 3 months after the trial start (section 4.2). When the trial ends, access stops; there are 30 days to migrate data (except Compute Engine) or upgrade (section 5.2).
  - *Eligibility:* New Google Cloud customers only: never a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform or Firebase, and never previously signed up for the Free Trial (official docs). US and/or Mexican federal, state or local government entities are excluded unless Google agrees in writing (section 1.2). No .edu or student-email rule; a student qualifies as an individual. No startup age or funding rule applies. A payment method is required (see gotchas). Some countries also require bank account verification.
  - *How to claim:* Open cloud.google.com/free and click Get started for free (or go to console.cloud.google.com/freetrial), sign in with a Google account, complete the Free Trial signup, add a valid payment method and verify identity, then create a project. The 90-day clock starts when the billing account is created. The Firebase console also routes eligible users into this signup (since March 2025).
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from billing account creation, or earlier at $300 spend or upgrade. Unused credit expires at day 90 (3 months after start if upgraded).
  - *Gotchas:* A credit card or other valid payment method is REQUIRED. It is not charged, but authorization holds can stay 1 to 14 business days. This is the key difference from the education credit programs, which need no card. Google may modify or terminate the trial at any time (section 3.6). An expired or invalid card can suspend the account (section 3.5). Faculty pages warn that this trial needs a card, so students should be steered to the education credits instead.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google Cloud Free Tier: always-free monthly limits (20+ products)** (high) — More than 20 products have monthly free usage. The allowance does not expire but is subject to change, and it is not charged against the $300 trial credit (cloud.google.com/free). Examples from the current list: Compute Engine 1 e2-micro instance per month; Cloud Storage 5 GB-months Standard; BigQuery 1 TB of queries per month; Cloud Run 2 million requests per month; one GKE Autopilot or Zonal cluster per month; Cloud Run functions 2 million invocations per month; Pub/Sub 10 GB of messages per month; Firestore 1 GB storage; Cloud Build 120 build-minutes per day; Speech-to-Text 60 minutes per month; Vision AI 1,000 units per month; Natural Language API 5,000 units per month; Secret Manager 6 secret versions per month; Cloud Shell 5 GB disk; Artifact Registry 0.5 GB per month; Workflows 5,000 steps per month; Agent Engine 180,000 vCPU-seconds and 360,000 GiB-seconds per month. Compute Engine detail (official docs): the e2-micro is free only in us-west1 (Oregon), us-central1 (Iowa) and us-east1 (South Carolina); it includes 30 GB-months of standard persistent disk and 1 GB per month of outbound North America data; the limit is by time, not per instance; GPUs and TPUs are excluded. Cloud Storage free usage applies to US regions (the docs name us-east1, us-west1, us-central1).
  - *Eligibility:* Any Google Cloud customer; the trial is not required, and the docs say existing customers can use it too. No .edu or student requirement; no startup rules. Region limits: the e2-micro and Cloud Storage allowances apply only in the listed US regions.
  - *How to claim:* Create a project and Cloud Billing account in the console, create resources only in the free regions, and stay within each monthly limit. Set a Cloud Billing budget with alerts so overages are noticed.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry. Monthly limits reset each month. Google says the limits can change.
  - *Gotchas:* Usage above a free limit is billed at standard prices. Creating the e2-micro outside the three US regions is billed. GPUs and TPUs are never free here. Egress beyond 1 GB per month is billed. Whether a card is needed for free-tier-only use was not verified in this run.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Product-level free trials (GKE, Cloud SQL, Spanner, AlloyDB, Looker, Bigtable, Gemini Enterprise)** (medium) — Trial offers listed on cloud.google.com/free: GKE, full capabilities free for 90 days with no fee; Spanner, 90-day trial instance with 10 GB storage; Cloud SQL, 30-day trial instance with 8 vCPU Enterprise Plus, 64 GB memory and 100 GB storage; AlloyDB, 30-day trial with an 8 vCPU basic primary instance and up to 1 TB storage; Looker, 30-day trial instance with standard features; Bigtable, 10-day trial with a 1-node SSD cluster and up to 500 GB; Gemini Enterprise Plus edition, 30 days at no cost. The Cloud SQL, Spanner, AlloyDB and Bigtable entries also say new customers get $300 credit on signup.
  - *Eligibility:* Set per product on each trial page; not verified one by one. No .edu requirement. Startup age and funding rules do not apply.
  - *How to claim:* From cloud.google.com/free, open the product's trial link ('Explore more offers') and create the trial instance in the console.
  - *Expiry:* Trial instance periods are 10 days (Bigtable), 30 days (Cloud SQL, AlloyDB, Looker, Gemini Enterprise Plus) or 90 days (GKE, Spanner).
  - *Gotchas:* Trial instances end when their period ends; check each product page for what happens next. Card requirements and region limits for each trial were not checked individually. Trial instances are separate from Free Tier usage.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Students: 200 Google Skills training credits** (high) — Currently enrolled college and university students can request 200 Google Skills credits for hands-on labs, skill badges and courses on Google Skills, the platform formerly called Google Cloud Skills Boost (cloud.google.com/edu/students). Credits expire one year after they are issued. The same page lists Google Skills Arcade (no-cost gamified labs) and a no-cost YouTube preview of Google Cloud Certificates.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified college or university students at regionally accredited institutions in the listed countries. The list is long and includes the US, UK, Canada, Germany, India, Japan, Brazil, Mexico, Australia and Singapore. For-profit institutions are not supported (support.google.com/google-cloud-higher-ed/answer/10322652). The help text does not spell out a .edu rule or the verification method. No card requirement is stated.
  - *How to claim:* Submit the Google Skills training-credits application form (services.google.com/fb/forms/googlecloudskillsbooststudenttrainingcreditsapplication/) with your institutional details. Requests can take up to two weeks. For more credits, use the same form. Then use the credits on skills.google.
  - *Expiry:* One year after issue.
  - *Gotchas:* These are lab and training credits for Google Skills. The official pages do not describe them as Cloud Billing dollars, so they do not fund your own Cloud projects. The portal domain changed to skills.google in October 2025 (see sunset_notes).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Skills no-cost Starter subscription: 35 credits a month** (medium) — The Starter subscription costs nothing and gives learners 35 credits each month for hands-on labs in Google Skills (support.google.com/qwiklabs/answer/9139481). Google's Skills announcements and the Developer Program FAQ also cite 35 monthly credits for GEAR program participants and Google Cloud Innovators community members. Paid Monthly Pro and Annual Developer Premium plans sit above it.
  - *Eligibility:* Any learner with a Google account. Not limited to students, and no .edu rule. Innovators membership is joined through My Communities and Programs on the developer profile. The card requirement for the no-cost plan was not verified.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in at skills.google, open the profile menu, go to Settings, then Subscriptions, and select the Starter (no cost) plan. For the Innovators or GEAR route, join through the program pages.
  - *Expiry:* Monthly allotment. Whether unused credits roll over was not verified.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are for labs only. The exact reset and rollover rules, and whether the free plan asks for a card, were not checked directly.
  - *Source:* https://support.google.com/qwiklabs/answer/9139481?hl=en
- **Google Career Launchpad: no-cost AI and cloud training for higher education** (medium) — Career Launchpad gives higher-education institutions (and government and nonprofit workforce organizations) no-cost generative-AI and cloud training. It includes DeepMind's AI Research Foundations curriculum, Google Cloud Certificates, hands-on labs and Educator Guides. Faculty also get Google Skills credits to add labs to coursework. Google Skills Arcade is always-on and no-cost. Google Cloud Certificates carry an ACE recommendation of up to 10 college credits (page footnote). Career Dreamer is US-only. The page mentions a 50% certification discount for some learning-path completers; conditions were not checked.
  - *Eligibility:* Institutional program run by faculty facilitators at higher-education institutions. The page sets no .edu rule. Career Dreamer is US-only. The country list was not checked. No card requirement is stated.
  - *How to claim:* Faculty apply through the Apply button on cloud.google.com/edu/faculty/career-launchpad (faculty form: services.google.com/fb/forms/googlecloudcareerlaunchpadapplication/).
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the page. Expiry for the Skills credits within this program was not confirmed.
  - *Gotchas:* This is non-cash training, not a credit balance. The certification discount and ACE college-credit conditions were not verified.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty/career-launchpad
- **Education (teaching) credits: up to $100 per teaching staff member and $50 per student** (high) — Eligible faculty can apply for up to $100 in Google Cloud credits per teaching staff member and up to $50 in Cloud credits per student (cloud.google.com/edu/faculty). Approval issues coupons: faculty get codes for themselves and staff, plus a URL to send to students. Each course's credit is tracked in its own Cloud Billing account. Credits cover most Google Cloud products, and free-tier usage does not count against them (Cloud Billing edu-grants doc). Credits may not be used for course infrastructure, commercial use or personal use.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty only; students cannot apply but can ask faculty to apply. Faculty must use an official school email on a valid .edu domain (or nominate another email if they have none). In the US the institution must be a regionally accredited program; outside the US it must be a higher-learning institution that awards degrees in an eligible country. For-profit institutions are excluded. The application form has a country list (includes the US, UK, Canada, India, Japan, Germany, Australia and Brazil). Up to 3 courses per submission, with course start no later than one year away. Students must confirm a school email to redeem. The support page says sign-up needs no credit card.
  - *How to claim:* Faculty apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching (up to 3 courses, expected student and TA counts, school faculty-directory link). After approval, faculty get coupon codes and a student URL. Students verify their school email, then click Redeem now in the coupon email and accept the terms in the Google Cloud console. Questions go to CloudEduGrants@google.com.
  - *Expiry:* Redeemable from approval until 16 weeks after course start. Validity sources conflict: up to 1 year after course start (program page), 12 months after course start (help page), and 1 year after redemption (Cloud Billing docs). Confirm against the approval email.
  - *Gotchas:* The official amounts are per teaching staff ($100) and per student ($50). The '$100 per course' wording in the brief is not how the official page states it. Credits are non-transferable and tied to the school email. Google may revoke awards or change criteria at any time. Check spam for the confirmation email, which should arrive within 24 hours.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty
- **Research credits: up to $5,000 for faculty, PhD students and postdocs** (medium) — Researchers can apply for up to $5,000 in Google Cloud research credits (cloud.google.com/edu/researchers says 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'; the application page text retrieved did not restate the figure). The credit must be activated within 60 days of the project start date on the application. It may be issued in increments and is for proof of concept, evaluation or comparison, workshops or training, and open-source development. The application asks for a funding plan for after the credit expires.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty, PhD students and postdoctoral researchers at accredited higher-education institutions, plus eligible nonprofit research institutions, in approved countries (the list includes the US, UK, Canada, Germany, Japan, India, Australia, Singapore and South Africa). An institution account is preferred but not strictly .edu. PhD students may not use the credit for personal use. The GPAR program (Google Public Sector Program for Accelerated Research) is US-only and gives pricing co-investment, not credit. No card requirement is stated.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research. Provide personal and organization details, a project proposal (up to 250 words), the total from your pricing calculator with a link to it, a Google Cloud billing account ID, and a funding plan for after the credit expires.
  - *Expiry:* Expires 365 days after coupon redemption or when fully used, whichever comes first.
  - *Gotchas:* Non-transferable, with no sale or barter and no commercial use. The $5,000 headline comes from the researchers landing page. Confirm the cap on the form before relying on it, since 'up to' means awards can be smaller.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/researchers
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program, Start tier: up to $2,000 in credits** (high) — The Start tier gives up to $2,000 in Google Cloud credits valid for one year (cloud.google.com/startup/benefits). Also: $200 in Google Skills training credits; 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free for new sign-ups (conditions apply); AI webinars and content; and Web3 partner perks for Web3 startups. This is the self-serve entry tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Technology startup with a working MVP, a clear business model and a well-defined product roadmap, that plans to seek venture funding soon. Founded within the last 24 months. Has not received Google Cloud credits beyond the free trial. The business email must match the company website domain, and the billing account must be owned and administered by someone on that domain. Not open to educational institutions, nonprofits, government entities, dev shops or consultancies, companies that have IPO'd or been acquired, or crypto mining. Google decides acceptance at its sole discretion. A Cloud Billing account is required; card requirement not stated.
  - *How to claim:* Create a Cloud Billing account in the console and note its 18-character billing account ID, since credits attach to it. Confirm a business email on your website domain. Apply through the Apply now button at cloud.google.com/startup. On approval, credits are issued to the billing account named in the application.
  - *Expiry:* Valid one year (official page). Some third-party sites say two years; the official page does not.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits apply only to Google first-party pay-as-you-go or consumption products. Subscription, prepay and annual-license products are excluded, as are third-party Marketplace and Model Garden offerings. Third-party models (such as Claude) are billed directly. Credits cannot be shared, split or resold. Workspace is free only if the company domain had no paid Workspace plan in the 31 days before applying. Re-applying is possible if earlier credits went unused.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program, Scale tier: up to $200,000 (AI-first up to $350,000)** (high) — Scale tier: up to $200,000 in Google Cloud credits over two years. Year 1 covers 100% of spend up to $100,000; year 2 covers 20% of spend up to a further $100,000. AI-first startups: up to $350,000 (year 1 100% up to $250,000; year 2 20% up to $100,000 more). Also: 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free (separate application), $600 per month of Google Maps Platform credits (separate application), up to $12,000 in Google Cloud Enhanced Support credits for one year, Google Skills training credits (the benefits page lists $500 for this tier; verify), and a Startup Success Manager as you grow.
  - *Eligibility:* Venture-backed startups. Pre-Seed or Seed rounds within the last 5 years; if a Series A has been raised, it must be within the last 12 months. Founded within the last five years (benefits page; the program footnote says 3 to 5 years depending on tier). Has not received more than $5,000 in Google Cloud credits before. Non-venture funding (private equity, bank loans, government grants, crowdfunding, friends and family) does not qualify. Stealth startups are accepted on investor or accelerator verification. The AI-first tier requires Gemini or Gemma as a foundation of the primary product (AI program page). Must be in a region where Google Cloud is generally available and sold through self-service purchasing. Acceptance is at Google's discretion, weighing funding stage and type, business model, founder experience, product usage, age, growth and total funding. No .edu rule. A billing account is required; card requirement not stated.
  - *How to claim:* Have your funding round details, any investor or accelerator affiliation (enter these for stealth verification), billing account ID and website-domain business email ready. Apply at cloud.google.com/startup (Apply now). Credits are issued to the billing account after approval. Maps and Workspace benefits need separate applications.
  - *Expiry:* Credits run over two years in year-1 and year-2 tranches. The exact expiry of unused balances was not in the official text read.
  - *Gotchas:* Same first-party-only exclusions and third-party model billing as the Start tier. Credits cannot be shared, split or resold. Prior credits above $5,000 disqualify. Some third-party guides say the Scale tier is usually reached through a partner investor or accelerator; the official page does not say so. Approval is not guaranteed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Startups in prototyping: free Gemini API access in Google AI Studio** (medium) — The prototyping track of the Google for Startups program gives free access to the Gemini API in Google AI Studio, with API key management, access to Google models such as Gemini, Gemini Omni and Nano Banana, and Build mode (cloud.google.com/startup and startup.google.com/cloud). This is product access, not a Cloud Billing credit.
  - *Eligibility:* Developers and builders at small businesses, bootstrapped startups and self-funded ideas, which are the stated criteria. No venture funding is required. No .edu rule. Card and regional requirements were not verified.
  - *How to claim:* Use the Start building in AI Studio link on cloud.google.com/startup and sign in with a Google account to create an API key.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated. Free-tier quotas can change.
  - *Gotchas:* Rate limits and data-use terms for the free tier were not verified in this run. Third-party models are not covered by program credits.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup
- **2025–26 change:** 2024-11-08 (AI Startups Summit, Bengaluru): Scale tier was restructured/raised — the prior program's 'up to $150,000 over 2 years' became $200,000 (or $350,000 for AI-first) with the Year-1 100% match / Year-2 20% structure; the pre-2024 tiered Grow/Scale legacy offers (which varied ~$5k-$150k by funding stage) were folded into the current Start ($2,000) / Scale ($200k/$350k) two-tier model — the earlier larger multi-stage tiers are gone. The standalone self-serve 'Google Cloud for Students' $300/12-month credit program is no longer advertised on current official pages (cloud.google.com/edu/students now lists faculty-applied course credits + free Google Skills access, 200 credits); it appears sunseted in favor of the faculty/course-based Education credits program (medium confidence — university IT pages still cite the old $300 offer). Historical note: GCP Free Trial was extended from 30 to 90 days back in Aug 2020 (unchanged since). Vertex AI's older '1M free GenAI units' promotional offer has not been re-announced in 2025-2026; AI free access now flows through the Gemini API Free tier and GCP Free Tier instead. 2026 changes worth flagging: Gemini API Free-tier rate limits moved to per-account dashboard (static per-model table removed from docs), unrestricted API keys blocked from June 19 2026, and paid tier now requires min $5 prepay.
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes observed today (2026-10-22) on live pages: (1) The startup program is now branded "Google for Startups Cloud Program" at cloud.google.com/startups; the task's "known so far: up to $200,000" equals the current non-AI "Scale" funded-tier cap, but the headline cap is now up to $350,000 over two years ("Scale AI" tier: Year 1 100% coverage up to $250,000; Year 2 20% up to an additional $100,000; non-AI "Scale": Year 1 up to $100,000 + Year 2 up to $100,000). Legacy partner-accelerator network (Y Combinator/500/Techstars-era "Accelerate" listings) is replaced on the page by generic "equity-free accelerators" cohorts plus a "Gemini Startup Forum." Old URL cloud.google.com/programs/startups now 404s. (2) The Always-Free tier was restructured on cloud.google.com/free: the legacy free allotments for Cloud SQL (micro instance 750h/mo + 20GB SSD/375GB HDD), Memorystore, Spanner, and Dataflow (9,600h/mo) are no longer listed, and BigQuery no longer lists the 10GB storage free allotment (only 1TB/mo queries); Cloud Storage was cut to "5 GB-months Standard" and Cloud Functions was replaced by "Cloud Run functions." GCP now pairs this with finite product-specific free trials (30-90 days) for managed databases/data platforms. (3) The $300 trial page no longer states a day limit or "60 days" wording; it says "You won't be charged until you activate your full paid account" (activation-based; historical 60-day term not visible today). (4) "Cloud Skills Boost" is rebranded as "Google Skills" (student offer is "200 free Google Skills credits"). (5) New 2025-2026 offers not previously on GCP: Gemini Enterprise Plus edition 30-day trial, Agent Engine free allotment (180,000 vCPU-s/360,000 GiB-s per month), Datastream free 100 GiB CDC/month, GKE 90-day trial. No GCP free offer appears to have been fully closed in this window - the major move is shrinkage of the always-free tier plus product trials and a bigger AI-leaning startup program. All findings above were verified by reading the cited official pages directly on 2026-10-22.

(2) CLOUD SQL ALWAYS FREE: the old 'Cloud SQL Always Free plan' (free shared-core instance, 750 h/month, storage/backup/egress allowances in select regions) is NO LONGER on the Cloud SQL pricing page — the only official free Cloud SQL offer today is the 30-day free trial instance (Enterprise Plus, 8 vCPU/64 GB/100 GB) plus the $300 trial credit; treat the 750 h C2D claim as stale. (3) SPANNER FREE 96 GB NODE (us-west1): not present on the current Spanner pricing page; replaced by the 90-day free trial instance (10 GiB, Standard edition + Graph, regional-only) usable by both new and existing customers, with 30-day grace before data deletion. (4) BIGQUERY '100,000 slot-hours/month free': not published on the current BigQuery pricing page (which lists only 1 TiB free on-demand queries + 10 GiB free storage per month, plus free batch loads and first 2 TiB/mo of gRPC Storage Write API) — likely subsumed by the 2023–2024 move to Editions/slots pricing; treat the 100K slot-hour figure as superseded. (5) STARTUP PROGRAM REBRAND: 'Google Cloud Startups Accelerator' (cloud.google.com/startups, previously up to $150k single tier) is now 'Google for Startups Cloud Program' at cloud.google.com/startup — the old /startups URL serves the new program. Current structure: Start $2k tier (founded <2 years, MVP, pre-VC) + Scale (Y1 up to $100k + Y2 up to +$100k = $200k) + Scale AI (Y1 up to $250k + Y2 up to +$100k = $350k), with the $5,000-prior-credits gate and non-venture-funding exclusion for Scale tiers; marketing copy on cloud.google.com/free says 'up to $200,000 USD (up to $350,000 for AI startups)'. (6) EDUCATION: no general free GCP dollar credits for students; student offer is 200 Google Skills credits expiring after 1 year; the substantive education offers are faculty teaching credits ($100/faculty + $50/student) and GPAR research credits ($5,000, US-only). As read today (2026-10-22), cloud.google.com/startup and startup.google.com/cloud both present the restructured "Google for Startups Cloud Program": up to $350,000 over two years, split into AI (Year-1 $250K) and non-AI (Year-1 $100K) tiers, eligibility "Pre-Seed to Series A, founded within the last 3-5 years, <$5,000 previously received GCP credits", with no US-entity requirement stated on the page. - **2025–26 change:** 1) Startup program renamed/restructured: the legacy 'Google Cloud Startup Program' (commonly cited as up to $200K/2-year funded track plus a YC-alumni track) is gone; cloud.google.com/startups/program and cloud.google.com/programs/startup-program now 404. The live offer (2026) is the 'Google for Startups Cloud Program': Start tier $2k; Scale/Scale AI $200K–$350K over 2 years, with AI startups capped at $250K Year 1 (100% match) + $100K Year 2 (20% match), other startups $100K Y1 + $100K Y2; new 12-month Google Workspace Business Plus benefit and monthly Google Maps Platform credits added. 2) Trial: current official terms are $300/90 days (not the 30/60-day figures seen in third-party summaries, including some of today's truncated page renders); a 30-day recovery grace period after expiry is now documented. 3) Trial restrictions are explicit and new-ish: GPUs/TPUs cannot be added on non-billable trial accounts; the $300 cannot pay Gemini API-in-AI-Studio or managed partner-model costs — so the 'GPU/ML-training' front on GCP trial means CPU (e2s) + free-tier e2-micro, with real GPU/TPU access requiring a paid account. 4) Docs domain moved: cloud.google.com/free/docs/* now 301-redirects to docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/*. 5) Method caveat: WebSearch was unavailable for this run (shared session search budget exhausted), so 2025–2026 change-detection relied on the live official pages themselves; items I could not re-verify (Colab free quotas, Google Skills credit prices, per-product trial renewal rules) are marked medium/low confidence rather than carried from memory. 6) No evidence found this session that the always-free e2-micro or the education faculty/research grants were sunset — all confirmed live on 2026-10-22.
- **Critic correction** (Google Cloud Free Trial + always-free tier (Cloud SQL, BigQuery, Spanner)): Duration is wrong. The $300 Welcome credit is valid for 90 days from signup, not 60. Free-tier products do not draw down the credit. The trial billing account closes when the credit is spent or 90 days pass, whichever comes first.
- **Critic correction** (Google for Startups (Cloud)): Term length is wrong. Scale-tier credits are paid over two years: Year 1 is 100% of spend up to $100,000 (up to $250,000 for AI-first startups), and Year 2 is a 20% credit on spend up to an extra $100,000. The Start tier's $2,000 is valid for one year.
- **Critic correction** ($300 credit for 60 days (200+ products)): The $300 Free Trial credit is valid for 90 days, not 60. Google's free-tier page describes 20+ free products; '200+ products' is not stated.
- **Critic correction** (Up to ~$200,000 in GCP credits ... typically on 12-month terms): The $200,000 Scale-tier credit runs over 2 years: up to $100,000 in year 1 plus 20% (up to an additional $100,000) in year 2. The 12-month term applies to the $2,000 Start tier. AI startups can reach $350,000.

### Google Gemini API

- **Gemini API Free Tier (Google AI Studio)** (high) — Free input AND output tokens, no billing account or credit card required. New accounts start on the Free tier automatically; qualification = an active GCP project or free trial, no spend-based cap. Free-tier models (official pricing page, fetched 2026-10-22): Gemini 3.8 Flash, 3.6 Flash, 3 Flash Preview, Gemini 3.5 & 3.1 Flash-Lite (all service tiers), Live/audio models (3.8 Live, 3.5 Transcribe, 2.5 Flash Native Audio), TTS (3.8 Flash TTS, 3.8 Flash-Lite TTS, 2.5 TTS previews), Gemini Embedding 2 (Standard tier only), Gemma 4 (all capabilities incl. tuning), and Robotics ER 2 previews. Context caching free on 3.8/3.6 Flash and Gemma 4. Limits are per-project RPM/TPM/RPD (RPD resets midnight Pacific); the docs no longer publish static per-model numbers — exact quotas are shown live in the AI Studio dashboard for your project. Free tier is indefinite, no renewal mechanism.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a Google account + an active Google Cloud project; no billing account, no card, no .edu, no startup status. Region: Gemini API availability is limited to Google's supported-countries list (list not re-verified today).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign in at aistudio.google.com with a Google account; 2) select/create an active GCP project; 3) an API key is auto-provisioned and the Free tier applies automatically — nothing to enable. To upgrade later: link a billing account and prepay a minimum of $5 (new 2026 paid flow).
  - *Expiry:* No expiry/renewal — free tier is permanent; per-model quotas are live in AI Studio. Note: free tier is 'limited access to certain models' — Pro/image/video models are NOT free-tier eligible.
  - *Gotchas:* Free-tier prompts and responses ARE used by Google to improve products (only paid tiers, except invite-only Contributor, exclude your data from product use). Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview, Gemini 4 Argon, Omni Flash, 3 Pro Image, Nano Banana 2/2.1/Lite, 2.5 Pro, Veo 3.1, Lyria 3.x are NOT free-tier eligible. 2.5-generation models are access-restricted to prior active users; new projects are told to use 3.5 Flash-Lite or 3.8 Flash. Static per-model RPM/TPM/RPD figures were removed from the docs — verify quotas in AI Studio. Model lineup churns fast (see deprecations).
  - *Source:* https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing(free-tiermodellist,caching,deprecations);https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/billing(nobillingrequiredforFreetier,$5prepayforpaidtiers);https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits(limitsareper-project,viewinAIStudio)
- **Google Cloud Free Trial — $300 credit for new customers** (high) — $300 USD of free GCP credit for new customers ('You won't be charged until you activate your full paid account') — usable for Vertex AI / Gemini model calls, AI app building, agents. Separately: 20+ products with always-free monthly limits (e.g. 1 e2-micro VM, 5 GB Cloud Storage, BigQuery monthly quota, 'AI APIs' free usage) that do not draw from the $300 and never expire ('the free usage limit does not expire, but is subject to change'). Also listed on the same page: GKE full-capability 90-day trial, Cloud SQL 30-day trial (8 vCPU Enterprise Plus instance, 64 GB RAM, 100 GB storage), and a 30-day free trial of Gemini Enterprise Plus (the agentic enterprise platform). Core terms read from the page today (high); exact card requirement and 90-day credit duration live in the sign-up flow (medium sub-parts, flagged in gotchas).
  - *Eligibility:* New Google Cloud customers only (one trial per person; household/organizational duplicates are detected and denied). A payment method is required at sign-up (card/PayPal; standard verification hold) — unlike the Gemini API free tier this one DOES need a card. Trial duration for the $300 credit is not stated on the marketing page (historically ~90 days; verify in the sign-up flow).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to cloud.google.com/free; 2) sign up for Google Cloud and add a payment method; 3) the $300 credit is granted automatically to the new billing account — no coupon. Use it on Vertex AI/Gemini workloads; the separate always-free tiers apply on top.
  - *Expiry:* Credit expires at end of trial window if unused (duration stated in sign-up flow); always-free tiers: no expiry, subject to change. No auto-renewal — continuing requires activating a paid account.
  - *Gotchas:* Card required (vs. zero-card Gemini API free tier). GCP Marketplace third-party SKUs excluded from credit. After trial end, if a payment method is on file and you continue using billable services, you will be charged — cancel or switch the account if you don't want that. The $300 credit covers GCP/Vertex AI charges; it is a different entitlement from the free Gemini API (ai.google.dev) tier.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free(creditamount,'notchargeduntilyouactivateyourfullpaidaccount',20+free-tierproducts,GKE/CloudSQL/GeminiEnterprisePlustrials—pagefetched2026-10-22)
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program (startups)** (high) — Rebranded/expanded program: up to $200,000 USD in GCP credits (up to $350,000 for AI startups) over two years, plus credits for frontier models (Gemini, Gemma), 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus at no extra cost, monthly Google Maps Platform credits, dedicated technical support, and accelerator residencies. Tier structure (official FAQ): 'Start ($2k)' tier = $2,000 credits, for startups founded in the past 2 years with a working MVP, a clear business model, and plans to seek venture funding. Funded tiers 'Scale' and 'Scale AI' = $200K–$350K credits, for startups with VERIFIABLE Pre-Seed to Series A funding. Credit mechanics (footnote on the page): AI startups get Year 1 100% coverage up to $250,000 + Year 2 20% coverage up to an additional $100,000; non-AI startups Year 1 100% up to $100,000 + Year 2 20% up to an additional $100,000. Targeted at 'new Google Cloud customers founding digital-native products'.
  - *Eligibility:* For-profit startup, digital-native product, new Google Cloud customer. Excluded: companies that have IPO'd or been acquired, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content, dev shops, consultancies/agencies, cryptocurrency mining, direct competitors/resellers of GCP. Scale/Scale AI tiers: venture funding must be verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A; non-VC funding (private equity, bank loans, government grants, crowdfunding, friends & family) does NOT qualify for Scale — such startups 'may be evaluated for other program tiers' (i.e., typically the $2,000 Start tier). No hard dollar funding cap stated; stage cap is Series A. Stealth/unfunded founders must wait until product launch and need a working website. Company domain must not have had a paid Google Workspace plan within 31 days of applying. Acceptance at sole discretion of Google Cloud.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at cloud.google.com/startups ('Apply now' form: company info, funding stage, accelerator affiliations, use case); 2) acceptance/rejection email typically within 5–7 days (10+ if info missing); 3) credits provisioned to your GCP project on acceptance; the Workspace Business Plus and Maps credits are separate application items ('Separate application required').
  - *Expiry:* Two-year program: Year 1 = 100% of eligible spend up to cap ($250K AI / $100K non-AI); Year 2 = 20% of additional spend up to +$100K. No renewal; alumni can re-apply after completion.
  - *Gotchas:* Year-2 credits are 20% coverage, not 100% — the 'up to $350K' assumes heavy Year-2 eligible spend. Third-party (non-Google) models are billed directly and NOT covered by program credits. Bootstrapped/self-funded startups realistically get only the $2,000 Start tier without verifiable VC money. The '5–7 days' decision clock is typical, not guaranteed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startups(tiers,creditamounts,Y1/Y2mechanics,exclusions,FAQ—pagefetched2026-10-22);programalsoadvertisedathttps://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google Cloud Research Credits ('Academic Research Grants') — up to $5,000** (high) — GCP research credits to advance academic work. Amount advertised: 'Apply for up to $5,000 in Google Cloud credits' (researcher card on the Google Cloud for Education hub). Formal terms on the application page: 'Credit awards must be activated within 60 days of the project start date indicated in the application form. Credit awards expire 365 days from the coupon redemption date, or when the credit amount has been fully used, whichever comes first.' Credits 'do not have commercial value and may not be sold or bartered.' A GCP billing account is required to use the credits.
  - *Eligibility:* Limited to faculty, PhD students, and postdoctoral researchers at accredited higher-education institutions and eligible nonprofit research institutions in APPROVED COUNTRIES (region-limited). Institutional email account required; .edu is the typical but not only qualifying domain (nonprofit research institutions also qualify).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply via the form at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research (institutional email, project details, grant ID, funding plan for after the 1-year credit expiry); 2) on approval, redeem the coupon in the Google Cloud console (billing account required); 3) activate within 60 days of project start.
  - *Expiry:* Credits: 365 days from coupon redemption or full use, whichever first; must activate within 60 days of project start. No auto-renewal — reapply for new projects/years.
  - *Gotchas:* Requires a GCP billing account (payment method on file), so it is NOT zero-card like the Gemini API free tier. Not for teaching — separate 'Teaching Credits' program. 'Approved countries' restriction may exclude some regions. Credits are research-only, not transferable/sellable.
  - *Source:* https://edu.google.com/programs/credits/research/(eligibility+redemptionterms,fetched2026-10-22);https://cloud.google.com/edu('Applyforupto$5,000inGoogleCloudcredits')
- **Google Cloud Teaching Credits (faculty)** (medium) — GCP credits for faculty to give students hands-on cloud experience: 'Apply for GCP credits for you and your students to give them real, hands-on experience with Google Cloud Platform. It is the intent of the program that the credits are used by students to learn about how to use the cloud.' Application covers 1–3 courses per entry (different courses/time periods listed separately); form captures institution, department, course name, expected course start date, and a link to your school's faculty directory profile. Program is advertised alongside 'up to $5,000 in Google Cloud credits' on the Google Cloud for Education hub; the exact per-course credit amount is not printed on the application page (hence medium confidence).
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty ONLY ('Eligibility is limited to faculty only. If you are a student, you can encourage your faculty members to apply for your next course.') Requires an academic institution (US states list on the form; international institutions via the academic-institution field).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Faculty apply via the form at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching (institution + 1–3 courses with start dates); 2) on approval, students use the GCP credits for course work; 3) review the program FAQ on the page for current amounts.
  - *Expiry:* Per program terms (not printed on the fetched page; typically tied to the course term — confirm in the FAQ). No auto-renewal; reapply each course/year.
  - *Gotchas:* Students cannot apply for themselves — the faculty member must apply. Credits are intended for learning use only. Amount is not stated on the application page, unlike the research program's 'up to $5,000'.
  - *Source:* https://edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching/(form+faculty-onlyrule,fetched2026-10-22);https://cloud.google.com/edu('ApplyforfreeGoogleCloudcredits','upto$5,000'programfigure)
- **Gemini for Education (free education AI license)** (high) — Google's education-license front: 'Gemini for Education' — Gemini (premium Gemini 2.5 Pro with LearnLM 'world's leading model for learning' claims) delivered free of charge to educators, students, and staff: 'Your institution can get Gemini for Education at no cost by signing up for Google for Education Fundamentals, even if you use a different primary productivity suite,' and 'Gemini for Education is also included in all Google Workspace for Education editions free of charge.' Included in the free offering: default access to premium AI models 'with significantly higher limits than what consumers get at no cost,' limited-access Deep Research, custom Gems ('AI experts' per topic), Gemini Canvas, Gemini Live, Gemini Notebook (documents-to-podcasts), enterprise-grade data protection — 'Data is not human reviewed or used to train AI models' (Core Service under Workspace for Education terms).
  - *Eligibility:* K-12 and higher-education institutions (school/university domain; 'Google for Education Fundamentals' sign-up, or any Google Workspace for Education edition). Not an individual-offering — the institution signs up and admins enable the Gemini app. No payment method required. The upsell 'Google AI Pro for Education' (expanded premium-model access + full Gemini across Workspace apps, Gmail/Meet/Docs) is a paid add-on, contact-sales pricing.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Institution signs up for Google for Education Fundamentals (free, edu.google.com) even if another suite is primary, and admin enables ONLY the Gemini app; 2) or it is already bundled in a Workspace for Education license; 3) educators/students sign in with their school Google account to gemini.google.com / Gemini mobile app.
  - *Expiry:* Not a credit — it persists as long as the (free) GFE Fundamentals membership or the institution's Workspace for Education license is active; annual license renewal applies to paid GWE editions. No separate renewal for the free app.
  - *Gotchas:* Free tier is 'limited access' to Deep Research vs. the paid Google AI Pro for Education add-on (expanded limits + full in-Workspace Gemini). Consumer 'Gemini Free' at gemini.google.com is a separate, lower-limited product — the education version needs the institutional sign-up. Admin-managed: usage/rollout is controlled by school IT. LearnLM claims are Google marketing, not benchmarks.
  - *Source:* https://edu.google.com/ai/gemini-for-education/(fetched2026-10-22)
- **2025–26 change:** WebSearch was unavailable for this agent (shared session budget exhausted); all findings above were read directly from official Google pages fetched 2026-10-22 (cloud.google.com/startups, cloud.google.com/free, cloud.google.com/edu, ai.google.dev pricing/billing/rate-limits/deprecations, edu.google.com credits pages), so 'high' confidence means the page was read today. Programs materially changed in 2025–2026: (1) The startup program was restructured into tiered 'Start ($2k) / Scale / Scale AI' with the headline raised from the old 'up to $200K' framing to 'up to $200K (up to $350K for AI startups) over two years', and large credits now REQUIRE verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A VENTURE funding — bootstrapped/self-funded startups are effectively capped at the $2,000 Start tier unless re-evaluated; non-VC money (PE, bank loans, grants, crowdfunding, friends & family) no longer qualifies for Scale. Year-2 credits are only 20% coverage up to +$100K. (2) Gemini API free-tier model lineup churn: official deprecation page (last updated 2026-10-20) — Gemini 2.0 Flash & 2.0 Flash-Lite shut down Jun 1, 2026; 3.1 Flash-Lite Preview May 25, 2026; 3 Pro Preview Mar 9, 2026; 2.5 Pro previews Dec 2, 2025; Imagen 4 Aug 17, 2026; Veo 3.1 & Omni Flash Preview shut down Oct 22, 2026 (today); 2.5 Flash Image stable Mar 15, 2027; 3.1 Flash-Lite stable May 7, 2027; text-embedding-004 Jan 14, 2026 (→ Gemini Embedding 2). 2.5-generation models are access-restricted to prior active users with no announced shutdown. (3) Docs no longer publish static free-tier RPM/TPM/RPD — quotas are per-project and shown live in the AI Studio dashboard only. (4) Paid-tier billing flow changed 2026: minimum $5 prepay to enter paid tiers; 'Postpay temporarily until Prepay system propagates, starting March 23, 2026'; upcoming paid price doublings Dec 1, 2026 (Gemini 4 Argon) and Jan 1, 2027 (3.8/3.6 Flash, TTS, robotics) — relevant when free-tier users upgrade. (5) GCP free trial marketing now leads with AI ($300 credit usable for Gemini/Vertex; new 30-day Gemini Enterprise Plus trial, GKE 90-day trial, Cloud SQL 30-day trial); the trial's exact day-count for the $300 credit is not printed on the public page.
- **Critic correction** (Google Gemini API (AI Studio) Free Tier): Partly stale. Google's pricing page now describes the free tier as limited access to certain models, with free input and output tokens. Free-tier RPM and RPD figures are no longer published; limits are shown in AI Studio. The Sept 2026 changelog limits 2.5-series access to users who used those models before and tells new projects to use 3.5 Flash-Lite or 3.8 Flash. Gemini 4 Argon is not on the free tier.
- **Critic correction** (Gemini API Free Tier (Google AI Studio)): Same change as the AI Studio entry. Free-tier limits are not published in Google's docs and 2.5-series access is restricted to returning users (changelog, Sept 2026).

### GroqCloud Free Plan

- **GroqCloud Free Plan (ongoing free tier)** (high) — Genuinely free, rate-limited inference on open models (gpt-oss-120b/20b, qwen3.8-27b, Whisper, TTS, safety classifiers), no subscription. Per-model limits from the official table as of 2026-10-22: openai/gpt-oss-120b and openai/gpt-oss-20b: 30 RPM / 1,000 RPD / 8,000 TPM / 200,000 TPD; openai/gpt-oss-safeguard-20b: 3 RPM / 1,000 RPD / 2,000 TPM / 200K TPD; qwen/qwen3.8-27b: 30 RPM / 1,000 RPD / 8K TPM / 200K TPD; whisper-large-v3 and whisper-large-v3-turbo: 20 RPM / 2,000 RPD / 7.2K audio-sec/hr / 28.8K audio-sec/day; meta-llama/llama-prompt-guard-2-22m and -86m: 30 RPM / 14,400 RPD / 15K TPM / 500K TPD; canopylabs/orpheus-v1-english and -arabic-saudi (TTS): 10 RPM / 100 RPD / 1.2K TPM / 3.6K TPD. Limits apply at the organization level (all keys in an org share one pool); cached tokens do not count; exceeding any limit returns HTTP 429 with a retry-after header and x-ratelimit-remaining-* headers. Docs call the table a high-level summary — the org's exact limits live on the Limits page in console settings.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone (students and startups alike) with a GroqCloud account; email or GitHub sign-in; one free account per person. No .edu email, no funding/age cap, no referral, no approval step. No region limit is documented. A credit card is NOT required for the free plan — the official community help center states free signup gives immediate free-tier access 'without requiring a credit card', and the Billing FAQ only requires a payment method (credit card, US bank account, or SEPA debit) when UPGRADING to the Developer tier.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at console.groq.com (email or GitHub) — you start on the $0 Free plan automatically, no card. 2) Open the API Keys page (console.groq.com/keys), create a key, store it as GROQ_API_KEY. 3) Call the OpenAI-compatible endpoint https://api.groq.com/openai/v1/chat/completions with a model ID such as openai/gpt-oss-120b or qwen/qwen3.8-27b. Higher limits require upgrading to the Developer plan (paid, pay-per-token, progressive billing thresholds at $1/$10/$100/$500/$1,000 cumulative; can downgrade to Free at any time after settling the final invoice).
  - *Expiry:* No expiry: the free plan is ongoing and refills daily within its per-model limits. No renewal needed. Note the catalog changes frequently (models are deprecated on a rolling basis — see sunset_notes), so 'free tier' content drifts even while the plan itself is permanent.
  - *Gotchas:* (1) Llama chat models (llama-3.1-8b-instant, formerly ~14,400 RPD; llama-3.3-70b-versatile) are NOT on the free tier as of Aug 16, 2026 — they are enterprise-only; the 14,400-RPD figure now belongs to the llama-prompt-guard safety classifiers, not general chat. (2) The 8,000 TPM cap binds long before 1,000 RPD for long prompts — one ~6K-token request consumes a whole minute's quota. (3) TPD on gpt-oss models is 200K = ~200 tokens/request average across 1,000 requests. (4) Limits are org-wide: one busy script burns the quota for all teammates/keys. (5) Upgrading to Developer requires adding a card/bank/SEPA payment method; if you only need free usage, do not upgrade or a small bill can appear. (6) A promo-code redemption page exists (Settings > Billing > Promo) where free credits can be redeemed, but there is no standing public free-credit promotion; the 'Groq for Startups' $10K program that used to provide credits was unpublished (see sunset_notes). (7) Preview models (e.g., qwen3.8-27b is listed as Preview in some sources) can be discontinued at short notice. (8) Groq's Service Credit Terms (eff. 2025-10-15) cap any prepaid/promo credits at 1 year from issuance.
  - *Source:* https://console.groq.com/docs/rate-limits
- **Groq for Startups ($10,000 API credits) — SUNSET** (medium) — Former startup program: $10,000 in GroqCloud API credits granted to the account (not a code), expiring 90 days after grant, approval at Groq's discretion; no published funding cap, founding-date cutoff, or VC-referral requirement — the form asked for a working product/use case and an expected inference-volume estimate. The program page groq.com/groq-for-startups no longer publishes the offer (as of 2026-10-22 it serves a generic marketing page with no program content, funding announcement, or application form), and console.groq.com/docs/startups returns 404 per third-party checks. Multiple independent trackers (Perkstack, checked 2026-08-05; guptadeepak.com, checked 2026-10-10; PerkBook) recorded the program as unpublished/removed on 5 August 2026 with no closure notice from Groq. Not claimable today.
  - *Eligibility:* Before it closed: early-stage AI companies/startups with a GroqCloud account and a genuine use case; stages Bootstrapped through Series A per trackers; no partner referral required. Now: nobody — the application form is gone.
  - *How to claim:* No longer claimable. Historical flow: create a GroqCloud account, complete the application at groq.com/groq-for-startups describing the startup, use case and expected inference volume; on approval, $10K landed on the account and the 90-day clock started. Do not rely on third-party directories still listing this offer as live.
  - *Expiry:* Unpublished as of 2026-08-05 (tracker-recorded date; Groq published no formal closure notice). Any previously granted credits follow the 90-day-from-issuance program window, not the 1-year default in the Service Credit Terms.
  - *Gotchas:* The dead program still appears in several 'free AI credits' aggregators with the old $10K/90-day terms — treat those listings as stale. The console's promo-code page (Settings > Billing > Promo) remains the only live credit-redemption mechanism, and no public startup-credit promotion is currently linked to it. Successor options in the same niche per trackers: Fireworks for Startups, Together AI for Startups (up to $50K), Baseten for Startups (up to $25K + $2.5K APIs).
  - *Source:* https://perkstack.co/blog/groq-startup-program
- **Groq education / student program — none exists** (medium) — There is no Groq student discount, education license, or academic-access program as of 2026-10-22. The student/academic route is simply the cardless Free plan: the official rate-limit and billing documentation contains no education, .edu-verification, or academic-program reference, and independent verification (Noizz, 2026-09-04 check of Groq's docs) confirms 'no student, academic, education or research programme is mentioned anywhere in Groq's rate-limit or billing documentation.'
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — nothing to qualify for. Students use the standard Free plan (open to anyone, no .edu required, no card).
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim. A student claiming 'Groq free access' means the same no-card Free plan: sign up at console.groq.com, create a key, call the OpenAI-compatible endpoint under the per-model limits.
  - *Expiry:* N/A.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not expect a .edu discount on the paid Developer plan; if an education offer ever appears it would likely surface as a promo code (Settings > Billing > Promo), but none is publicly published today.
  - *Source:* https://console.groq.com/docs/billing-faqs
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes to watch: (1) Groq for Startups ($10K credits, 90-day validity) was unpublished on 2026-08-05 — the program URL now serves a generic marketing page and the docs page 404s; no closure notice was published. (2) llama-3.1-8b-instant and llama-3.3-70b-versatile were shut down for Free and Developer tiers on 2026-08-16 (enterprise committed-spend customers kept them) — this killed the '~14,400 RPD llama-3.1-8b' free-tier figure that earlier trackers (including this task's 'known so far') still cite; today's official table's 14,400-RPD row is for llama-prompt-guard safety classifiers. (3) groq/compound and groq/compound-mini were deprecated and decommissioned on 2026-09-21. (4) qwen/qwen3.6-27b was deprecated on 2026-09-14 in favor of qwen/qwen3.8-27b. (5) Other 2026 deprecations per Groq's deprecations page: Llama Guard 4 12B (2026-03-05, replaced by gpt-oss-safeguard-20b), Llama 4 Maverick 17B (2026-03-09), Kimi K2 (2026-04-15), Qwen 3 32B and Llama 4 Scout 17B (2026-07-17). (6) No education/student program or trial-credit offer exists; only a generic promo-code redemption page in the console. Bottom line: one genuinely free, cardless, ongoing offer (the Free plan above) plus a sunset $10K startup program whose terms still circulate in stale third-party listings.
- **Critic correction** (No-card free plan: gpt-oss-120b & qwen-3.8-27b at 30 RPM / 1,000 RPD / 8K TPM / 200K TPD): Groq's rate-limit page shows these exact values under Developer Plan Limits and says the table gives the Developer plan's base limits. No free-plan table was captured, so the free-plan figures and the no-card claim are not supported by this page.

### Hugging Face

- **Inference Providers free account: NO monthly credits (as of Oct 2026)** (high) — Official pricing docs (read 2026-10-22): Free Users row in the included-credits table is 'None' — free accounts get no monthly compute credits. Free users can still access Inference Providers (18 providers, 200+ models: Baseten, Cerebras, Cohere, DeepInfra, Fal, Featherless, Fireworks, Groq, HF Inference, Novita, Nscale, OVHcloud, Public AI, Replicate, Scaleway, Together, WaveSpeed, Z.ai) via pay-as-you-go 'credits purchase required', or via a custom provider key. The PRO marketing page claims free-tier users get a 100k token/month inference usage cap vs 2M for PRO, but the pricing docs define no free credit and I could not find a published limits table — treat the 100k figure as marketing, not docs.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Hugging Face account (free, global, no .edu, no credit card to create the account or to browse; a credit card IS required before any pay-as-you-go usage, since extra usage requires purchasing credits via Stripe). No student or startup special tier.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at huggingface.co, generate a fine-grained token with 'Make calls to Inference Providers' permission; add a credit card in Billing settings and purchase credits to exceed the free usage cap.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing; no stated expiry
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: there is no trial-credit offer on Inference Providers — the only ever-free allowance was $0.10/month for free accounts, discontinued 2026-10-07 (docs update). No credit card stated as required at sign-up, but one is required to buy pay-as-you-go credits. Monthly tokens cap (100k, unverified in docs) is a usage limit, not a free grant.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/inference-providers/en/pricing
- **Custom Provider Key: $0 on Hugging Face (bring-your-own provider key)** (high) — Documented in the official pricing page (read 2026-10-22): you can set a custom provider key in HF settings (or pass it to the InferenceClient / OpenAI-compatible client) and HF routes requests for free — 'Hugging Face will not charge you for the call'; billing goes directly to the provider you already pay. This is the only genuinely card-free-on-HF way to use Inference Providers.
  - *Eligibility:* All account types incl. free. You need your own account/key with the provider; no region limit stated; no credit card needed on HF.
  - *How to claim:* Settings → Inference Providers → add custom provider key, or pass the key to InferenceClient; code and OpenAI-compatible SDK usage are unchanged.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing
  - *Gotchas:* Included monthly credits do NOT apply to custom-provider-key usage. The provider's own free tier/trial terms now govern cost — this is a 'no markup' pass-through, not a free allowance. If the provider later requires its own payment method, the 'free' path ends there.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/inference-providers/en/pricing
- **PRO plan: $9/mo subscription including $2.00/mo compute credits (paid, not free)** (high) — PRO ($9/month, credit card only, Stripe, no stated trial) includes $2.00/month of general-purpose compute credits usable on Inference Providers, Inference Endpoints, upgraded CPU/GPU Spaces hardware incl. ZeroGPU usage beyond the 5-min free daily quota, and Jobs. PRO also raises the inference usage cap to 2M tokens/mo (vs 100k free claim), 1 TB private storage (base price $12-18/TB/mo above), 10 ZeroGPU Spaces, 8× daily ZeroGPU quota (40 min RTX Pro 6000 Blackwell), dev-mode Spaces. No student discount, no free PRO tier, no student-specific enrollment found on huggingface.co/pro or /pricing today.
  - *Eligibility:* Any user globally; credit card required for the subscription. No .edu requirement, no regional restriction stated.
  - *How to claim:* Subscribe at https://huggingface.co/subscribe/pro; credits are credited monthly and applied automatically before pay-as-you-go billing.
  - *Expiry:* Renews monthly; cancel anytime in billing settings. Docs say credits are 'credited every month' but do not promise rollover of unused credits.
  - *Gotchas:* The $2 credit does not cover the $9 subscription. 'Pay Pro with credits' was reportedly possible in the past and now appears removed (HF forums, Feb 2026). Not a student/startup offer.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/pro
- **Team plan: $20/user/mo incl. $2/seat/mo shared credits (paid, not free)** (high) — Team orgs ($20/user/mo; self-serve, credit card or AWS/Google Cloud Marketplace billing) get $2.00/month compute credits per seat, shared across members, spendable on Inference Providers, Inference Endpoints, Spaces upgrades and Jobs. Org admins can set spending limits and disable providers; usage billable centrally via X-HF-Bill-To header or bill_to client parameter.
  - *Eligibility:* Orgs globally; card or cloud-marketplace billing. No startup/education discount published.
  - *How to claim:* Subscribe Team in org settings; members specify bill_to org (or X-HF-Bill-To header) to draw on the shared credit pool.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits only apply to HF-routed usage, not custom provider keys. Not free; included only for completeness of the 'credits' picture.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/inference-providers/en/pricing
- **Six months free PRO for startups — third-party claims only, unverified official** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Several affiliate/promo-code directories (SaaSTweaks 'verified May 2026', xraise.ai 'verified 2026-05-15', startup-perks.com 'audited Feb 2026') claim a 'Hugging Face for Startups' offer of ~6 months of free PRO plus Inference Endpoints credits, applied at huggingface.co/startups. But huggingface.co/startups is actually an org profile page ('startupsdao') with zero program content, and two independent checks of HF-owned pages found no program: guptadeepak (checked 2026-09-13: 'No startup program appears on any Hugging Face owned page') and CloudRoute's 2026-10-09 catalog, which excluded Hugging Face from its 62 verified startup programs because 'no official source confirmed a current program'.
