  - *Gotchas:* Headline 'up to $150,000' is from the official page today; the specific tier dollar amounts ($1,500 entry, ~$25,000 mid, $150,000 scale) and the age/funding caps come from third-party/community sources, so treat them as medium/low confidence. Credits do not apply to Azure Marketplace offers; the program is a customer-acquisition channel, so post-credit consumption is what Microsoft monetizes; co-sell/transaction fees (3% new / 1.5% renewal) attach to the co-sell program, not to credits.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups
- **Azure free account: $200 credit, 30 days** (high) — New Azure customers get $200 credit usable for 30 days (or until credit is exhausted), plus the 12-month free service amounts and 65+ always-free services on top. After 30 days / credit exhaustion the account and services are DISABLED (not auto-converted); you must move to pay-as-you-go to continue. Unused credit stays available for the full 30 days from creation. Credit cannot be applied to Azure Marketplace offers or Azure Spot VMs. Sovereign cloud products in US Government, Azure China, and Azure Germany are excluded. No $100 12-month credit is listed on the current pages — the standard trial is $200/30 days only.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only (one free account per new customer). Requires phone number, a credit or debit card (non-prepaid), and a Microsoft or GitHub account. A temporary ~$1 authorization is placed on the card and reversed on verification; the card is not otherwise charged. Only credit cards accepted in Hong Kong and Brazil. Offer unavailable in countries not on Microsoft's list; 'If your country is not listed, the offer is not available in your region.'
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free → verify phone + card (temporary $1 hold) → $200 credit active for 30 days → within 30 days move to pay-as-you-go (portal: Subscriptions → Upgrade subscription) to keep the 12-month free amounts; otherwise services are disabled at day 30.
  - *Expiry:* Credit: 30 days from account creation (unused credit lost). Free service amounts: 12 months after sign-up for the trial account. Renewal: none — after 12 months, used services bill at PAYG rates.
  - *Gotchas:* VPS angle: the 12-month free VM allowance covers B1s, B2pts v2 (ARM) and B2ats v2 (AMD) burstable VMs only — 750 hours each per month. That is roughly 24h/day for one VM; larger/Standard VMs are NOT covered. Data transfer/egress beyond free allowances and storage beyond free quotas bill at PAYG rates even in the trial. Account is disabled (not billed) if you do nothing — but resources are inaccessible until upgrade.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/
- **12-month free service amounts (new customers)** (high) — Free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months: VMs 750 hours/month each of B1s, B2pts v2 (ARM-based), B2ats v2 (AMD-based) burstable VMs; Azure Container Registry: 1 Standard registry, 100 GB storage, 10 webhooks; PostgreSQL Flexible Server: 750 hours Burstable B1MS + 32 GB storage + 32 GB backup; MySQL Flexible Server: same 750 hours B1MS + 32 GB + 32 GB; Azure SQL Managed Instance: 720 vCore hours of 4 or 8 vCore General Purpose (64 GB data + 64 GB backup); Azure Vision (Foundry Tools): 5,000 transactions per S1/S2/S3 tier. Can be consumed in any region where the service is available and split across instances (e.g. 5 VMs × 150 h). Services in use after 12 months continue running and bill at PAYG.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only — 'not previously had an Azure account or received 12 months of free services.' NOT currently available to customers who sign up directly for pay-as-you-go in China and India. Trial signups must move to pay-as-you-go within 30 days to keep receiving them.
  - *How to claim:* No separate claim — included automatically when creating any Azure account (free trial or PAYG, new customer). Create resources via the 'Free services' page in the Azure portal with free tiers selected to avoid accidental billing.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months from account creation; unused monthly amounts do not roll over; after 12 months everything in use bills at PAYG rates. No renewal.
  - *Gotchas:* For a free VPS: 750 h/month is the total shared across B1s/B2pts v2/B2ats v2 — a single always-on B1s VM works for ~12 months, but nothing above B-series burstable is free. 'Always-free' services (65+) do NOT include VMs, so a pure PAYG account beyond 12 months has no free compute; only the free service tiers listed remain. Egress/storage beyond free quotas bill.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/free-services
- **Always-free services (65+, all accounts)** (high) — Permanent free monthly amounts for every Azure account: App Service (10 web/API apps, 1 GB storage, 1 hour/day compute), Functions (1 million requests), Container Apps (180,000 vCPU-s, 360,000 GiB-s, 2M requests), Static Web Apps (0.5 GB/app, 100 GB bandwidth/subscription), AKS cluster management (nodes bill), Cosmos DB (1,000 RU/s + 25 GB), Azure SQL serverless (100,000 vCore-s + 32 GB/month), DocumentDB (32 GB cluster), Azure Data Factory (5 low-frequency activities), API Management Consumption (1M calls/month), Logic Apps Consumption (4,000 actions), Notification Hubs (1M pushes), IoT Hub (8,000 msgs/day), AI/Foundry services (Speech 0.5M chars, Translator 2M chars, Document Intelligence 500 pages S0, AI Search 50 MB/10k docs/3 indexes, Vision/Content Safety tiers), Azure DevOps (5 users), Azure Machine Learning (free, egress may apply), plus free/unlimited management services (Monitor control plane, Arc, Advisor, Migrate, Cost Management, Lighthouse, etc.).

  - *Expiry:* No expiry while the account exists; monthly allowances reset each month. Cloud Shell's 5 GB free storage in Azure Files lasts 12 months only.
  - *Gotchas:* App Service '1 hour per day' makes it usable as a very small always-on worker but it's not a VM. Egress, premium storage and anything above free tier limits bill. AKS is 'free' only for cluster management — node VMs bill.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/

'Stakeholders (Free)' tier: free read-only participants. - **Azure Free Account — $200 credit for 30 days + 12-month free services (B1S / B2pts v2 / B2ats v2, 750 hrs/month)** (high) — Confirmed today on official pages: $200 credit (in your billing currency) usable for 30 days on any Azure service except third-party Marketplace purchases. From signup up to 12 months: free monthly amounts of ~25+ services, including 750 hours/month EACH of the three free burstable VM SKUs — B1S, B2pts v2 (ARM-based), B2ats v2 (AMD-based), Linux or Windows. 750 hours can be split across instances (e.g. 5 VMs x 150h) and created in any region where B-series is available. After day 30 only the free-limited services remain free; unused $200 credit is lost at day 30 unless you upgrade to pay-as-you-go before 30 days (then credit stays usable for the full 30-day window). Must move to pay-as-you-go within 30 days or account/services are disabled. 12-month offer is new-customers-only (never had an Azure account / 12-month benefits before) and NOT available to customers who sign up directly for pay-as-you-go in China and India.

After day 30 without PAYG upgrade the whole account is disabled; resuming requires PAYG. HK/Brazil prepaid cards rejected.

- **65+ always-free Azure services (incl. notebook/ML-adjacent: Synapse Spark, Databricks, AI/ML categories)** (medium) — The free-services overview page (read today) states '65+ always-free services with an Azure account' with 'free monthly amounts... for as long as you have an Azure account', spanning AI/ML, compute, databases, IoT, management. The per-service limit tables on that page are JS-rendered and could not be extracted today, so the specific figures below are the long-standing published limits, unconfirmed in today's fetch: Azure Synapse Spark SQL pool DW1000c 250 hrs/month, Azure Databricks 32 SQL DBU/month, Data Factory 5,000 slicing units/month, App Service 1 shared instance + 60k req/day, Functions 1M req + 400k GB-s, Storage 5 GB LRS + 5 GB premium, Cosmos DB 500 RU/s + 400 GB, 100 GB egress/month, App Insights 5 MB ingest/day. Always-free is available with ANY account, including pay-as-you-go accounts — you do not need the trial.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Azure account (free or paid); no card required to exist, but creating resources needs a valid billing profile. No student/startup status needed.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up for any Azure account; create services via the 'Free services' page in portal selecting the free tier. No separate application.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite — free monthly amounts continue for life of account; amounts reset monthly; exceeding limits triggers PAYG billing.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free confusion: the 12-month tier is a separate, larger allotment that expires at 12 months; the always-free tier persists. Free Spark/Databricks limits are small — fine for notebook experiments, not sustained GPU/ML training. Outbound data and data at rest beyond free storage bill separately. Per-service figures above were not re-verifiable today (JS-rendered table), treat exact numbers as medium-confidence until checked at azure.microsoft.com/pricing/free-services in a browser.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/free-services
- **Free GPU / ML-training & notebooks compute: what actually exists** (medium) — Azure has NO free-tier GPU VMs and no free Azure Machine Learning compute in the free account. The only free paths for GPU/ML work: (1) the $200 trial credit (30 days) can buy any compute incl. GPU VMs (e.g. NC/NV/A-series) — a few hours of a mid GPU VM is typical of what $200 buys, exact durations vary by SKU/region; (2) always-free notebooks/data engines: Synapse Spark SQL DW1000c 250 hrs/month and Databricks 32 SQL DBU/month (medium confidence, see above) — CPU-based, not GPU; (3) 750 hrs/month B-series burstable VMs for light CPU training/inference. For sustained GPU/ML training the realistic free routes are the startup credits (up to $150K, finding 8) or Azure Spot/standard pricing — there is no recurring free GPU offering at Azure as of 2026-10-22.
  - *Eligibility:* Same as each underlying offer: new customers for the $200 credit; any account for always-free Spark/Databricks.
  - *How to claim:* Use the $200 credit within 30 days on GPU VMs; enable always-free Spark/Databricks via the 'Free services' page in the portal.
  - *Expiry:* Credit: 30 days. Spark/Databricks free amounts: monthly, indefinite.
  - *Gotchas:* GPU hours under the credit are finite and CPU-free tiers can't replace them; egress and managed-disk I/O billed; Databricks free DBUs cover SQL warehouse only, not full DBU workspace runs; don't confuse trial (30d) with always-free.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/create-free-services
- **Azure for Students — $100 credit for 12 months, no credit card required** (high) — Confirmed today: $100 in Azure credits usable within the first 12 months (non-rollover), plus 'select free services' (FAQ states the offer includes 25+ free services incl. compute/network/storage/databases). Applies to most Azure products; NOT usable for: Azure support plans, Azure DevOps, Visual Studio subscriptions, App Center, Express Route, Marketplace items, separately licensed products (e.g. Entra ID Premium). No credit card at signup; subscription disabled if $100 exhausted (then upgrade to PAYG via support). One subscription per person, non-transferable, cannot combine offers. Renewable annually (Microsoft emails near the anniversary; re-signup). 140+ countries where Azure is commercially available; excludes US Government sovereign cloud and Azure Germany regions. Inactive >90 days: Microsoft may remove compute resources (with notice).
  - *Eligibility:* Age 18+, FULL-TIME student at an accredited degree-granting 2- or 4-year institution; verify via your organization's (school) email address — not strictly '.edu'; MOOCs and for-profit professional training excluded. (Faculty/teachers: not on this offer; see Dev Tools for Teaching instead.)
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to the 'Apply now' link on the offer page (go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=2244463). 2) Register with school email to verify academic status. 3) No card needed. 4) Track remaining $100 in the Azure Sponsorships portal (microsoftazuresponsorships.com).
  - *Expiry:* Credit and free-service window: 12 months from signup; renew once more by re-signup if still a student. 90 days after free-subscription end to upgrade before products decommission.
  - *Gotchas:* If credit runs out, subscription disables — nothing kept running for free; you must upgrade to PAYG (which requires a payment method). Credits cannot buy Marketplace/DevOps/support. One sub per person — a second school email won't work around it. Not valid in US Gov/Germany regions.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/students(FAQ:https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/education-hub/azure-dev-tools-teaching/program-faq)
- **Azure for Students Starter — free-tier services, no credit card, no time limit** (high) — Confirmed today (Learn FAQ, updated 2026-08-03): free tier of Azure App Service, Azure Functions, Azure Notification Hubs, Azure Database for MySQL, Application Insights, and Azure DevOps Services — no cost, no commitment, no time limit, and no credit card required. Upgrade to PAYG at any time to unlock all services.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified students: 13+ in the US, 16+ elsewhere; verify academic status via organization email or Shibboleth. MOOCs/for-profit training excluded. One subscription per person, non-transferable, no offer stacking.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up via the Azure Education Hub in the portal (aka.ms/azure4students / Education Hub > offers) with school email; no card.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite while account exists; services capped at free-tier levels forever.
  - *Gotchas:* Very small service set (no VMs, no databases beyond MySQL free tier, no GPU/ML) — useful for always-on web/hosting, not training workloads. Free-tier tiers are the cheapest paid tiers, not full service; upgrading is required for real workloads.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/education-hub/azure-dev-tools-teaching/program-faq
- **Azure Academic Grant / Azure Sponsorship (course credits) — 100% discount for approved-course students** (medium) — Confirmed today (Learn FAQ): students enrolled in approved courses (e.g. via training partners) get 'Azure course credits' as a 100%-discount 'Azure Sponsorship' subscription: portal shows $0.00 charged, balance tracked in the Azure Sponsorships portal (microsoftazuresponsorships.com/balance). Credits cover usage only from activation; cannot pay prior balances; cannot cover Marketplace items; cannot attach to Enterprise Agreement or CSP subscriptions (creating association early terminates sponsorship funds). Course credits can be moved between accounts via support.
  - *Eligibility:* Students of approved courses/partners; verify with the sponsoring program; one activated 'Azure Sponsorship' offer (ms-azr-0143p) subscription.
  - *How to claim:* Activate the 100% discount button in the sponsorship approval email, or have support convert an existing subscription; associate course credits to the entitled account.
  - *Expiry:* Set by the course sponsorship period (varies by partner; not stated on the FAQ page).
  - *Gotchas:* Sponsorship + EA/CSP association kills the funds; Marketplace purchases still hit the card; $0 balance display is a discount, not a stored dollar balance.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/education-hub/azure-dev-tools-teaching/program-faq
- **Azure Dev Tools for Teaching — education license: free dev software, NO cloud credits** (high) — Confirmed today: academic institutions with a Microsoft Volume Licensing agreement (Education Solutions/Open Value/School Agreement) enroll via a promo code from the license welcome email and get free, unlimited (noncommercial) access to dev tools: Visual Studio Enterprise/Code/Community, SQL Server Developer & Standard, Windows Server, Windows 10/11 for Education, SharePoint/R Server, System Center, Visio Professional, Hyper-V, Azure DevOps Server; students download via the Education Hub Store with school credentials; faculty install on personal PCs. Explicitly does NOT include Azure credit (FAQ points students to Azure for Students for $100). Renewal: email reminders start 60 days before expiry; does not auto-renew; non-VL existing customers may still renew.
  - *Eligibility:* K-12/academic institutions under a Volume Licensing agreement; 140+ countries where Azure is commercially available; noncommercial educational use; students may not commercially sell products built with the tools (Windows Store apps excepted).
  - *How to claim:* Institution admin enrolls with the promo code from the VL welcome email at azureforeducation.microsoft.com; students/faculty then download via Education Hub Store or the VS portal.
  - *Expiry:* Subscription term per institution license; renews manually via admin.
  - *Gotchas:* Zero cloud/ML value — it's an on-prem/dev-tools license bundle; don't count it as free compute; no Office included.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/education-hub/azure-dev-tools-teaching/program-faq
- **Microsoft for Startups — base $1,000 (90 days) + $4,000 (180 days) Azure credits; up to $150,000 via investor referrals** (high) — Confirmed today on Learn (page dated 2026-05-21/22) and the marketing landing page: single-entry program at startups.microsoft.com. (a) No-referral 'Azure startup credit offer': $1,000 for 90 days, then an additional $4,000 for 180 days after business verification. (b) Investor offer (referral code from a partner in the Microsoft for Startups Investor Network — investor, accelerator, incubator, angel network, or similar): most startups begin with $100,000 in Azure credits with the opportunity to earn more; program 'unlocks up to $150K in Startup credits over time' as verified progress/adoption/sustained usage; landing page advertises 'up to $150,000 in credits'. Credits apply across eligible Azure services; NOT usable for support plans, Marketplace, or non-covered services; when exhausted, subscription auto-transitions to pay-as-you-go. Activation: must activate within 90 days of accepting the program agreement, or reapply; once activated, credits valid up to 2 years, no extensions; tied to one MSA + subscription (non-transferable). Dedicated MFS point of contact available for eligible startups; go-to-market/discounts on GitHub Enterprise, LinkedIn Premium Business, M365 Business Premium, VS Enterprise, Dynamics 365, Power Platform included in the investor offer. Applications reviewed in ~3 business days; declined applicants may reapply after 14 days.
  - *Eligibility:* Company: develops a software-based product/service it owns; privately held, for-profit; HQ in a country where Azure is available; NOT an educational institution/government/consultancy/agency; no cryptocurrency mining; has not raised Series C or later; has not received more than $350,000 in lifetime free Azure credits. No explicit company-age cap in the current criteria (the old 5-year age limit is gone from the current overview page). CC is required for credit activation (identity verification + transition to PAYG).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at startups.microsoft.com ('Get started'). 2) Optionally enter an Investor Network referral code for the $100K+ tier. 3) Wait ~3 business days for approval email. 4) Activate credits on the MSA used to apply (within 90 days) via the Azure portal; check under Cost Management + Billing > Payment methods.
  - *Expiry:* Base credits: $1,000 for 90 days, $4,000 for 180 days. Investor credits: up to 2 years from activation, no extensions. Program benefits scale/unlock over time.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card required. Credits not transferable between MSAs/subscriptions. 'Already redeemed' conflicts with other Microsoft programs' credits. Billing profile country/region can't be changed later — use the right region before activation. Auto-transition to PAYG when credits run out = unexpected bills. Base no-referral offer is only $5K total, so most 'up to $150K' value depends on getting an Investor Network referral.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview(+https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/mfs-faqs,https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups)
- **Microsoft for Startups — Startup credits, milestone-based (the current Founders Hub program)** (high) — Read today (ms.date 2026-05-27): a single program now replaces the old Founders Hub tier system. You enter at Milestone 1 = up to $200 in Startup credits immediately after signing up for Azure + identity verification via a PERSONAL LinkedIn account. Milestone 2 = $5,000 after business verification (registered legal entity). Milestone 3 = $25,000 (5+ distinct Azure workloads each >= $1 spend, held ~60 continuous days; one-time 2-year credit-usage extension granted at this milestone). Milestone 4 = $50,000 (7 workloads, ~60 days). Milestone 5 = $150,000 (10+ workloads AND ~$3,000/month Azure spend sustained ~60 days). Upgrades are automatic — no separate applications; you can skip tiers if you meet higher requirements directly. The marketing page still advertises 'up to $150,000 in credits'.
  - *Eligibility:* Privately held, for-profit; software-based product owned by the company; HQ in a country where Azure is available; no more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits; NOT Series C funding or later (tightened from Series D in 2025); not an educational institution, government org, consultancy or agency; no crypto mining. A company that already burned >$350k in free Azure credits (or reached Series C) is ineligible. No .edu requirement; 'open to anyone with an idea' per the signup page.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to startups.microsoft.com (Founders Hub) > Get started. 2) Submit application (personal LinkedIn at identity step). 3) Decision ~3 business days by email; reapply allowed 14 days after a rejection. 4) In the Azure Portal, activate credits (cost management > payment methods). 5) Credits accrue automatically as your workloads/usage hit each milestone — no separate applications.
  - *Expiry:* Activated credits are valid for up to 2 years from activation (per FAQ); no extensions except the one-time 2-year extension at the $25K milestone. You must activate within 90 days of accepting the program agreement or the offer lapses.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required to activate credits (identity verification + pay-as-you-go fallback). Credits are permanently tied to one MSA/subscription — no transfer between accounts. Pay-as-you-go kicks in automatically when credits run out. The $150K is not guaranteed: it requires ~$3k/month sustained Azure spend on 10+ services for 60 days. Discrepancy: this page says Milestone 1 is '$200', while the FAQ and Azure's own page say self-serve starts at $1,000 (see separate finding) — Microsoft's pages are out of sync. Existing participants from the 2025 two-track system keep old credits until expiry, then convert to pay-as-you-go.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/getting-started-mfs
- **Azure for Startups self-serve credits ($1,000 instant, up to $5,000) — no application** (high) — Confirmed on two pages read today: 'Up to $5,000 in Azure credits: $1,000 available immediately, plus up to $4,000 more after verification'. Initial $1,000 is valid 90 days; the post-verification $4,000 is valid 180 days. No application required — just signup. This matches the pre-known 'self-serve Azure for Startups' offer. If you later get an investor/accelerator/university referral code you can switch to the investor offer instead.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only. .edu not required. The FAQ says 'open' to teams without investor backing; it coexists with the milestone program — same signup flow.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at startups.microsoft.com or Azure's 'Azure for Startups' offer (link from azure.microsoft.com/free/students or the MFS portal). 2) $1,000 credits apply instantly to your new Azure subscription. 3) Complete business verification to unlock the additional $4,000. No application, no review queue for the self-serve tier.
  - *Expiry:* $1,000 valid 90 days from activation; $4,000 valid 180 days from business verification. After expiry: automatic conversion to pay-as-you-go.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card required for identity verification and for spend beyond credits ('spending protection' only covers the card from unauthorized billing). Directly contradicts the Learn milestone page's '$200 immediate' Milestone 1 — treat $1,000/90d as the self-serve floor and $200 as possibly the current live figure; verify in-portal at claim time. One startup account per person effectively; existing enterprise accounts can't redeem.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Microsoft for Startups Investor Network track (~$100,000+ Azure credits, referral-gated)** (high) — From the MFS FAQ read today: investor-backed startups enter with 'most startups begin with $100,000 [in Azure credits], with the opportunity to earn more' plus Azure Standard Support, technical experts, go-to-market support, and free/discounted third-party benefits: GitHub Enterprise, LinkedIn Premium Business, Microsoft 365 Business Premium, Visual Studio Enterprise, Dynamics 365, and Power Platform. Referral codes come from affiliated investors, accelerators, incubators, angel networks, or universities in the Investor Network. This track started at $100,000 when the 2025 two-track system launched; amounts can vary by referral source and engagement.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup must be affiliated with a Microsoft for Startups Investor Network partner AND hold that partner's referral code. Company must otherwise meet MFS eligibility (for-profit, privately held, not Series C or later, < $350k lifetime free Azure credits). Universities now also count as referral sources, so a university-backed startup can use this path.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Get a referral code from your affiliated investor/accelator/incubator/university. 2) Enter the code during the startups.microsoft.com application. 3) Upon approval, activate credits and redeem third-party benefits in the MFS portal (Azure Portal > Get started/benefits).
  - *Expiry:* Same rules as Startup credits: activate within 90 days of agreement; valid up to 2 years once activated. Third-party benefits (GitHub Enterprise etc.) have their own redemption windows.
  - *Gotchas:* Without a referral code you cap out at the self-serve/milestone path. Benefits can't be redeemed on accounts that already redeemed the same benefit or on existing enterprise accounts. Pegasus (dedicated architect/sales program) continued after the 2025 changes but is invitation-only and no longer referenced in current FAQ — do not plan around it.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/mfs-faqs
- **Azure Free Account (open to everyone, incl. students) — $200 trial + 12-month free services** (high) — Read today at azure.microsoft.com/free: $200 credit usable within 30 days; free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (e.g., 750 hrs each of B1s/B2pts v2/B2ats v2 VMs, 32 GB PostgreSQL/MySQL storage, 720 vCore-hrs SQL Managed Instance, 100 GB Container Registry, 5 GB Cloud Shell/Storage); plus 65+ always-free services (e.g., 0.5M Speech chars/mo, 500 pages/mo Document Intelligence, 50 MB AI Search, 5,000 Vision transactions/mo). One free account per person. Spending protection: card won't be billed while covered; $1 temporary authorization possible.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers, worldwide (card policy varies: HK and Brazil accept only credit cards). Students can claim this too, but if you're a student the Azure for Students offer below is better (no card, annual renewal). Not for people who already have a paid Azure account.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at azure.microsoft.com/free with Microsoft account. 2) Add a phone number and a credit/debit card (non-prepaid). 3) $200 credit applies immediately; free monthly amounts auto-attach. 4) After 30 days you're notified to convert to pay-as-you-go or the account is disabled.
  - *Expiry:* $200 credit: 30 days (unused credit does not extend, even if you convert early). Free monthly service amounts: 12 months, then pay-as-you-go rates apply. Always-free tiers have no time limit but usage is capped.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial, not free: card required, and pay-as-you-go starts the moment the credit or the 12-month window ends. Free credit cannot be applied to Azure Marketplace offers, Spot VMs, or Hybrid Benefit. 6-month AI-services window previously advertised is no longer on the page — only the 12-month/always-free tiers are.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free
- **Azure for Students — $100 credit for 12 months, no credit card (school email required)** (high) — Read today at azure.microsoft.com/free/students: $100 in Azure credit usable within 12 months, NO credit card required; free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months plus 65+ always-free services; full service catalog up to free amounts; 'renew your subscription annually and continue to get free access to Azure as long as you're a student'. A 'Starter' tier for younger users gives free service access only (no credit). The GitHub Student Developer Pack also routes 18+ students to this $100 offer (ages 13–17 get a reduced service set instead: App Service, Functions, Notification Hubs, MySQL for apps, App Insights, DevOps).
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time university students; sign up with a school (.edu/institutional) email for verification. No card needed. One account per person. Age: the pack routes 13–17 to Starter; the $100 credit is for 18+. Third-party trackers (checked Oct 2026) list country restrictions — some regions only allow Starter; check the signup flow.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at azure.microsoft.com/free/students using your school email (or via the 'Microsoft Azure' offer in the GitHub Student Developer Pack after verification). 2) Confirm student status. 3) $100 credit activates immediately; free service amounts attach. 4) Renew annually while enrolled.
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit valid 12 months from signup; annual renewal keeps the free services going while you remain a full-time student. 12-month free-service amounts reset with the renewal.
  - *Gotchas:* The $100 credit is the same offer the GitHub Pack grants — don't claim it twice (one Azure account per person; a second claim fails or voids the benefit). No card = no pay-as-you-go fallback: when the credit or free amounts run out, resources stop rather than bill you. Students who need more than free amounts should use the MFS self-serve $1,000 offer instead (which does need a card).
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **GitHub Student Developer Pack — free Microsoft/educator stack (GitHub + M365 + Azure + tools)** (high) — Verified today on education.github.com/pack: while verified as a student you get free GitHub Pro, free GitHub Copilot 'Student' plan (unlimited completions + monthly GitHub AI credits, limited chat/agent on auto-selected models), 3-month Microsoft 365 Education (Word/Excel/PowerPoint/Teams/1TB OneDrive — duration on the current page says 'free or discounted', historically 3 months), $100 Azure credit (18+), Namecheap free .me domain (1 year) + free SSL, Heroku $13/month for 24 months, free annual-renewable JetBrains licenses, and a GitHub Foundations/Copilot certification voucher via Microsoft Learn (resumed Sept 2025). Enrollment requires being 13+, in a degree/diploma program; under-18s get a reduced offer set.

  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at education.github.com/pack (or github.com/education) with school email or student ID. 2) Approval unlocks the pack page at github.com/features/benefits/students. 3) Redeem each partner offer individually (Azure, M365, Heroku, domain, etc.).
  - *Expiry:* Benefits last while you remain a verified student (GitHub Pro, M365, JetBrains renew annually/on graduation). Heroku credit: fixed 24 months. Domain/SSL: 1 year. Once you graduate, the pack stops renewing.

- **Microsoft 365 Education trial (30 days, schools) + free M365A license for institutions** (medium) — Microsoft offers schools a free 30-day Microsoft 365 Education trial (Word, Excel, PowerPoint, Teams, OneDrive, Class Notebook/whiteboard, Copilot in education apps, no credit card) claimable by an institution admin, and a free Microsoft 365 A1 (M365A) license for eligible K-12 schools and universities — through which most university students already get M365A at no personal cost via their school. Individual students at non-licensed institutions can use the free Office web apps/Word/Excel at no charge. (Official Microsoft page served localized/JS content and timed out, so the trial details are from the well-known program terms rather than a page read today — hence medium confidence.)
  - *Eligibility:* Trial: K-12 and higher-ed institutions (admin-managed). Free M365A: eligible institutions (K-12 schools and universities in supported countries); individual students get it only through an institutional license. No .edu email needed to sign the school up for the trial.
  - *How to claim:* 1) School: admin claims the 30-day trial from the M365 Education 'try it free' link on microsoft.com/en-us/education. 2) K-12: apply for the free M365A K-12 license via the Microsoft Education Store/partner flow. 3) Students: ask IT for the school's M365A license, or use free Office web apps with any email.
  - *Gotchas:* The trial is school-scoped, not individual: a 'free M365 for me' student offer does not exist standalone — students ride on institutional licenses or free web apps. Teams Education and Power Platform for Education trial similarly (30 days, institution-scoped).

- **Azure AI / Microsoft Foundry always-free tiers (available to students and startups alike)** (high) — From the Azure Free and Azure for Students pages read today: monthly always-free AI service amounts included with any free/12-month account: Azure Speech in Foundry Tools 0.5M neural characters/month; Document Intelligence 500 pages/month (S0); AI Search 50 MB storage (10k docs, 3 indexes); Vision in Foundry Tools 5,000 transactions/month per S1-S3 tier; Content Safety 5,000 text records or images/month; Azure DevOps free for 5 users; PostgreSQL/MySQL serverless free tiers. These stack on top of credit programs — useful for AI workloads while credits burn out.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Azure account; free monthly amounts are new-customer 12 months (VMs/databases) or perpetual (always-free services). Students with no card still get these via Azure for Students.
  - *How to claim:* No separate claim — free amounts auto-attach to eligible Azure services; monitor usage in Cost Management to stay under caps.
  - *Expiry:* Always-free: perpetual. 12-month amounts: until 12 months from account creation, then standard pricing.
  - *Gotchas:* Usage above the free amounts bills at normal rates (with no card, it just throttles/stops). Large AI inference (e.g., LLM tokens) is NOT in the always-free list — paid per token even in Foundry; budget accordingly.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025-2026 changes vs 'known so far': (1) The Founders Hub is now a single-entry 'Microsoft for Startups' program (apply at startups.microsoft.com). The no-investor baseline is the self-serve Azure startup offer — $1,000 for 90 days, then up to $4,000 more (180 days) after business verification (= up to $5,000); the older '~$200 starter + $4,000' framing now appears only as the no-code 'Build your startup' entry in the application flow. Investor-referred startups now START at ~$100,000 and grow to up to $150,000 (previously 'up to $150k' was the headline). (2) 2025-2026 rebrand: 'Azure OpenAI' and 'Azure AI Services' are now 'Azure AI Foundry' / 'Microsoft Foundry' (sub-brands Foundry Tools / Foundry Control Plane / Foundry IQ); the Azure free-services list was refreshed to these names. (3) LLM frontier: there is NO free OpenAI/LLM tier — access is paid/application-based and is only covered 'free' via the $200/$100/$1,000-$5,000 credits. (4) Several Azure AI services' older 'Free (F0) resource tiers' were replaced by the current 'always-free' monthly allowances on the free-services page (e.g., Content Safety 5,000/mo, Speech 0.5M chars/mo); exact F0-retirement dates were NOT re-verified this session because the session's web-search budget was exhausted mid-task. Everything else above is confirmed from the official Microsoft pages read today (overview/application/FAQ dated 2026-05 to 2026-10; pricing + free-services + Azure for Students pages current as of 2026-10-22). No major offer has been fully sunset; the main shifts are the rebrand and the consolidation of the startup credit tiers into one program.
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 program changes (verified via press + official MS pages): (1) July 1, 2025 — Microsoft for Startups restructure: the Founders Hub four-tier model (up to $150,000 in Azure credits, applied for with a validated business plan) was RETIRED, as were the 'Founders Hub' label, the Expert Network, and the Champion Network. It was replaced by a two-track system: a self-service track ($1,000 instantly for 90 days → up to $5,000 total for 180 days after business verification, for new Azure customers, credit card required for identity verification only) and an Investor Network track (VC/accelerator/university referral code; press-stated $100,000+ Azure credits, dedicated Microsoft POC, co-sell/Marketplace support). Pegasus (invitation-only dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect) was explicitly unchanged. Existing activated legacy credits stayed valid until their expiry, then auto-converted to pay-as-you-go; a Microsoft Q&A answer (July 2025) confirms non-investor applicants apply via the new 'Microsoft for Startups Credit Portal.' (2) June 2, 2026 — second update (official blog 'Microsoft for Startups: Apply and get credits to grow on Azure'): single entry point at startups.microsoft.com; startups 'start building immediately with Startup credits'; the up-to-$150,000 ceiling was RESTORED but now unlocked incrementally 'over time based on service adoption and sustained Azure usage and verified progress' rather than as a one-time grant; Investor Network partners unlock 'additional Startup credits, expanded support, and third-party benefits' earlier; current eligibility caps: not more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits, no Series C funding or later, private for-profit, HQ in an Azure region, no crypto mining; applications reviewed in ~3 business days (learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview, page updated 2026-08-05). The old portal's specific credit figures could not be verified today (page gone), so treat any pre-2025 "$25,000 M365 + $500 Azure credits" numbers as retired/low-confidence. 2) Startup program consolidation: the legacy Azure Startups Program (historically up to $300K credits with a 5-year company-age and $30M funding cap) and the 'Microsoft for Startups Founders' tier structure are replaced by the current single-entry Microsoft for Startups — cap expressed as 'up to $150K unlocked over time', eligibility now framed as 'no Series C or later' + '<$350K lifetime free Azure credits' rather than age/funding caps. 3) The 12-month free VM list now documents B1S + B2pts v2 (ARM) + B2ats v2 (AMD) burstable VMs at 750 hrs/month each; older doc generations listed B1s/B1ms/B2s — the ARM/AMD v2 SKUs are the current official set (confirmed on the Learn page dated 2026-03-03). 4) The student offer page no longer advertises a fixed '12 months of 25 services' list — it now says '$100 credit + select free services, subject to change' (Microsoft may end/alter it at discretion). 5) Method note: WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task, so all verification used WebFetch of official pages; per-service always-free limit figures (Synapse 250h, Databricks 32 DBU, 100 GB egress) live in a JS-rendered table on the free-services page that could not be extracted and are marked medium confidence.
- **2025–26 change:** 1) July 1, 2025 (announced June 27, 2025): the classic Founders Hub multi-tier program (apply-then-upgrade to $100 → $5,000 → $25,000 → $150,000 with business-plan validation) was RETIRED and replaced by a two-track system: Investor Network track ($100,000+ starting credits, referral from affiliated investors/accelerators) and a self-serve track ($1,000 for 90 days + $4,000 for 180 days after business verification, cap $5,000 without investor affiliation). Existing activated credits of old participants stayed valid until expiry; unfunded participants' accounts converted to pay-as-you-go after expiry. Pegasus (invitation-only, dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect) continued but out of the new funnel. 2) 2026 restructure (MFS 2.0, docs dated May 2026): the program was rebranded 'Microsoft for Startups' with a single entry and automatic milestone upgrades: $200 immediate → $5,000 → $25,000 → $50,000 → $150,000, with the $150K tier gated on 10+ concurrent workloads and ~$3,000/month sustained Azure spend. Eligibility also tightened: funding cap moved from 'not Series D or later' to 'not Series C or later', and a $350,000 lifetime-free-Azure-credits cap was added. So the oft-quoted 'no funding required, up to $150K' is now true only in the narrow sense that early stages need no funding, but Series C+ companies are excluded and $150K requires heavy sustained Azure spend. 3) Legacy partner credits from the old Founders Hub ($2,500 OpenAI, Databricks, Twilio $10,000, Atlassian, etc.) are no longer listed in the current FAQ (read today); the signup-page snapshot still showed 'up to $150,000 in Azure credits, plus $2,500 of OpenAI credits' — treat OpenAI/Databricks/Twilio partner credits as unconfirmed/sunset unless shown in-portal; current investor-track benefits are GitHub Enterprise, LinkedIn Premium Business, M365 Business Premium, Visual Studio Enterprise, Dynamics 365, Power Platform. 4) GitHub Student Developer Pack: DigitalOcean's $200 credit offer left the pack (credits expire 2026-08-01); Bump.sh offer ended 2025-12-09; Appwrite restructured to 6 months (2026-10-02); GitHub Foundations certification voucher was paused 2025-10-25 then resumed 2025-09-30. 5) The Azure Free page no longer advertises the old '6 months free AI services' window — only 12-month free amounts and always-free tiers.

Visual Studio Professional is not in the included product list, which names Visual Studio Code, Visual Studio Community, Visual Studio for Mac, Agents for Visual Studio, Remote Tools for Visual Studio and Visio Professional. The 12-month Cloud Academy benefit comes with paid Visual Studio Professional subscriptions, not Azure for Students.

### Mistral AI Studio

- **Free mode API (default, no card required)** (high) — Every new Mistral account starts in Free mode by default; API access is enabled with no credit card required. Included monthly usage is shared across Studio, the API, and Vibe Code, and is drawn first; when exhausted and pay-as-you-go is off, usage stops until the next billing period. Rate caps (requests/second, tokens/minute, tokens/month, per model) are set per organization and shown ONLY on the Admin Panel Limits page — Mistral publishes no public numeric quota. Described officially as the lowest limits, 'intended for evaluation and prototyping'. Since 2026-10-07 the pricing page no longer states a dollar figure on the Free card (the '$10/mo in API credits' line was removed without announcement); the 'pre' number was archived Sep 1–Oct 7, 2026. Pay-as-you-go is a switch, not a plan: prepaid credits must exist before enabling it, and adding credits does NOT raise rate-limit tiers (tiers move on cumulative billed usage: Tier 2 >€20, Tier 3 >€100, Tier 4 >€500 billed).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone who creates a account (docs: 'Free mode is the default state for new accounts'). No credit card required (stated in the Studio quickstart: 'no credit card required'). Restricted regions per Mistral terms (as read 2026-09-26, medium confidence): Cuba, Iran, North Korea, Syria, and Crimea/Donetsk/Luhansk regions. NOTE: since 2026-08-05 (US) / 2026-08-07 (EU) the consumer terms exclude Mistral Studio/API from individual use — API is limited to business customers under the Commercial ToS, so personal-project use is technically out of scope.
  - *How to claim:* Create an account at console.mistral.ai (Free mode is default, no card) → Studio → API Keys → 'Create new key' → call api.mistral.ai/v1 within your included monthly usage; inspect your own numeric caps on Admin Panel → API → Limits.
  - *Expiry:* Included monthly usage resets each month; Free mode itself has no stated expiration or renewal terms. Rate caps are persistent per org.
  - *Gotchas:* Shared pool: heavy Vibe Code/Vibe app usage eats the same monthly allowance as API calls, so a free account's API budget can vanish. Training/privacy: API calls may be used to improve Mistral models unless the 'Anonymous improvement data' toggle is switched off (default opt-out). Third-party report (Oct 2026): mistral-large-4 on a free key answered 'This model is not available in your subscription tier' even after $10 credit — flagship/labs models may be unavailable on Free. Free-model lineup shifted over 2026 (e.g., mistral-medium dropped 2026-08-14).
  - *Source:* https://docs.mistral.ai/getting-started/quickstarts/studio/activate-and-generate-api-key
- **Free plan for Vibe (formerly Le Chat) consumer app** (high) — Separate from the API tier: the consumer 'Free' plan is 'Your personal AI agent for everyday tasks' — web + mobile access with limited messages, web searches and coding sessions; limited image generation/editing; 100+ connectors. Published multipliers in the pricing table: messages & voice 'Up to 6x free', web searches 'Up to 5x free', image generation 'Up to 40x free', task scheduling 'Up to 5', project folders 'Up to 1,000', model-training data use 'Opt-out', support = help center only. No API credits included (Pro starts at $15/mo in API credits). All subject to fair-usage limits and the ToS; absolute counts are not published.
  - *Eligibility:* Public; no credit-card requirement stated on the page. Subject to Mistral's regional restrictions (embargoed regions excluded).
  - *How to claim:* Sign in to the Vibe console/app — Free plan is the default consumer tier; no application needed.
  - *Expiry:* No expiration; monthly fair-usage caps reset with the billing period; 'Subject to fair usage limits and Mistral's Terms of Service.'
  - *Gotchas:* The 6x/5x/40x multipliers are relative to a baseline, not absolute counts. Free-tier inputs/outputs may train models unless you opt out. This is app-only: it grants no API access — API calls still come from your Free-mode API keys and the shared monthly pool.
  - *Source:* https://mistral.ai/pricing
- **Education plan (student/teacher discount — paid, not free)** (high) — Not a free offer: verified students at accredited higher-education institutions get Mistral Pro at $5.99/month instead of the standard $14.99/month, capped at 12 months of eligibility. Eligibility is limited to students who have NOT previously used Vibe or Le Chat. Per a third-party pricing read (2026-10-11, medium confidence) the Education plan bundles $15/mo in API credits shared across the API, Studio and Vibe Code. There is no fully free education license from Mistral.
  - *Eligibility:* Student verification at an accredited higher institution (the page says 'verified students'; the mechanism is the console's student-verification flow, typically institutional email — the page does not explicitly say '.edu email required'). 12-month cap; new-to-Vibe/Le-Chat users only. No card-free access: it is a paid subscription, though students can pay at the discount price.
  - *How to claim:* Purchase the Education plan from the Mistral console using student verification; help-center article 'Students and teachers can check whether they are eligible for a discount' (referenced from docs.mistral.ai/admin/billing-usage/subscriptions).
  - *Expiry:* Eligibility limited to 12 months; the subscription itself renews monthly at $5.99 while eligible.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not count this as a free tier — $5.99/mo is real money. The 12-month window means the discount does not renew beyond that. Includes limited API credits ($15/mo reported third-party), not the Pro $25.50/mo figure.
  - *Source:* https://mistral.ai/pricing
- **Mistralship startup program (€30K credits) — currently unavailable** (medium) — Launched late 2024: 6-month cohorts of 10 startups; each winner received up to €30,000 in La Plateforme credits, dedicated 1:1 support from Mistral's Solutions & Science team, and early access to new models/products. Original eligibility (per the Dec 2024 announcement press): registered startup with a company website and a business email, founded less than 7 years ago, and no Series B or later round raised (Series B+ companies could apply for a separate offering). First cohort applications closed 2025-01-05. As of today (2026-10-22) both https://mistral.ai/startups and https://mistral.ai/news/mistral-ship return HTTP 404 (verified directly); no replacement public offer or closure notice exists. Third-party trackers (May 2026 and Oct 2026) classify the program as discontinued and route would-be applicants to Mistral sales/partners.
  - *Eligibility:* Historically: early-stage startups (pre-Series B), founded <7 years, registered company with public web presence; competitive selection (10 per cohort). No longer applicable — no live application path as of 2026-10-22.
  - *How to claim:* Cannot be claimed right now: no public landing page or form (both /startups and /news/mistral-ship are 404). The only remaining route is contacting Mistral sales / startup team directly.
  - *Expiry:* Cohort 1 closed 2025-01-05; the program page disappeared by mid-2026 (confirmed 404 today); no stated new cohort or deadlines.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits were La Plateforme platform credits (not cash, not transferable). Absence of a public page does not guarantee the program is dead — but with no closure notice and 404s on all paths, treat it as sunset/unavailable; do not plan around it.
  - *Source:* https://dataphoenix.info/news/less-than-three-weeks-left-to-apply-to-mistralship-mistral-ais-startup-program-2
- **Trial credits — none exist; free 'labs-' models and free Moderation 2 instead** (medium) — Mistral has no published trial-credit program (no dollar grant to new API customers) as of 2026-10-22. Two small free items exist on the API: model IDs prefixed 'labs-' are free but experimental and not for production (third-party pricing read, 2026-10-11), and Mistral Moderation 2 is priced at $0. The legacy free 'Experiment' plan (2024–2026, with published caps around ~1 req/s, 500K tokens/min, ~1B tokens/month) has been replaced by Free mode with unpublished limits; its old help/docs pages now return 404. Open-weight Mistral models (Ministral 3 8B/14B, open-mistral-nemo; Mistral 7B Apache-2.0) can be self-hosted for free, which is the real 'free' route beyond the shared pool.
  - *Eligibility:* labs- models: any account key (free-mode included) — experimental, not production-safe. Open-weight self-hosting: no account needed; Mistral-licensed weights, research/individual use covered, commercial deployment requires a Mistral license.
  - *How to claim:* Call 'labs-*' model IDs with a free-mode key at no token cost, or download open weights and self-host; there is no trial-credit claim flow to follow.
  - *Expiry:* labs- models may change or retire without notice; Moderation 2's free pricing is a list-price item, subject to change.
  - *Gotchas:* Free 'labs-' inference is explicitly for testing; don't build on it. Self-hosting has no egress cost but you own compute. Beware aggregator sites (e.g., 'AI Credits') selling 'Mistral credits' — that is a paid resale, not an official free offer.
  - *Source:* https://agentdeals.dev/mistral-api-pricing-2026
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Mistralship startup program (up to €30K La Plateforme credits, 10 startups/6-month cohort): cohort-1 applications closed 2025-01-05; the program's public paths are dead — https://mistral.ai/startups and https://mistral.ai/news/mistral-ship both return 404 as of 2026-10-22 (verified directly today); Mistral published no closure notice and no replacement offer; third-party trackers mark it discontinued. 2) On 2026-10-07 (no announcement), the pricing page dropped the '$10 /mo in API credits' line from the Free plan and reduced Pro's included API credits from $30 to $25.50/mo — the Free card now states no dollar figure; included monthly usage remains, visible only on the account's Limits page. 3) The legacy 'Experiment' free tier (published caps ~1 rps / 500K tokens/min / ~1B tokens/month) was retired and its pages now 404, replaced by Free mode with per-org unpublished limits. 4) Free-tier model lineup shifted during 2026 — e.g., mistral-medium was dropped from the free tier on 2026-08-14; mistral-large-4 (public preview 2026-10-06) reportedly rejects free-tier keys with 'not available in your subscription tier'. 5) Since 2026-08-05 (US) / 2026-08-07 (EU), consumer terms exclude Studio/API from individual use — API access is for business customers under the Commercial ToS, which quietly constrains who 'free mode' actually serves. 6) No free education license exists: the education offer is a paid $5.99/mo student discount (12-month cap), not a grant.

### NVIDIA

- **Free rate-limited NIM hosted inference (NVIDIA Developer Program)** (high) — Free API access to the NIM API Catalog (160+ models, incl. Nemotron 3 Ultra 550B, DeepSeek V4.x, GLM 5.3, Kimi K3, Llama 3.x) at build.nvidia.com, callable via OpenAI-compatible base URL https://integrate.api.nvidia.com/v1. Officially published ceiling per free personal account: up to 40 RPM and 10,000 requests/day per account; moderators confirm 40 RPM is the published free-tier cap and it is not adjustable per account. Governed by the NVIDIA API Trial Terms of Service (PDF last modified 2025-10-07): trial/prototyping purposes only, 'without use of the API Service or Generated Content in production'; NVIDIA may extend 'trial service credits' but must have a paid Subscription for production use. No credit card required; no per-token price. Limits vary by model, use case, and overall traffic (HTTP 429s occur on throttling).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a free NVIDIA Developer Program account (no .edu email, no corporate email, no funding requirement). API-key issuance requires SMS phone verification in a supported country; mainland-China (+86) numbers frequently fail to receive the code. Not offered in sanctioned/embargoed regions: Russia, Belarus, Iran, Kazakhstan, Ukraine-adjacent set incl. Uzbekistan, Kyrgyzstan, Pakistan, Bangladesh plus 6 others (build.nvidia.com publishes an otp-unsupported-countries list; ~5.5% of developers worldwide excluded).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an NVIDIA account at build.nvidia.com (Developer Program is free). 2) Verify phone number via SMS OTP (country must be supported). 3) Generate a personal API key in the API Keys section. 4) Call https://integrate.api.nvidia.com/v1 (OpenAI-compatible chat/completions) with model IDs copied from the catalog (only endpoints marked 'Free Endpoint'). Support: forums.developer.nvidia.com NIM section.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry while the Developer Program and the offering remain live; individual free endpoints rotate (models get deprecated and replaced by newer versions, routine catalog rotation). Free access lasts for the 'duration of program membership'.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial, not a permanent free tier: production use (serving real end users, business transactions) requires a paid NVIDIA AI Enterprise license. Inputs/outputs are recorded and used to improve NVIDIA products incl. AI models (Trial Terms 3.3); fine-tuning service stores user content 30 days / generated content 90 days. Arbitration + class-action waiver (30-day opt-out window). No official RPM-increase path on the free tier; 'Request More' does not exist in the profile menu. Third-party disputes over whether a legacy 1,000-credit signup pool (expandable to 5,000 with work-email) still exists — official model pages show only the 40 RPM / 10k-day copy; check your account panel. New personal keys can 404 on some chat calls until endpoints provision.
  - *Source:* https://build.nvidia.com/
- **Free self-hosted NIM license (downloadable NIM containers, up to 16 GPUs)** (medium) — Developer Program members may download NIM microservice containers from NGC and self-host them for research, application development, and experimentation on up to 16 GPUs on any infrastructure (cloud, data center, workstation) at no charge from NVIDIA — your only cost is your own GPU infrastructure. Self-hosting 'removes the public-endpoint rate limit entirely' (moderator statement). Additional GPUs 'upon request'.
  - *Eligibility:* Any free NVIDIA Developer Program member (students and startups both qualify; no .edu or funding requirements, no CC needed). License scope is explicitly non-production: research/development/testing/learning only; commercial production use requires NVIDIA AI Enterprise.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Join the free Developer Program. 2) Browse NGC catalog / build.nvidia.com model cards, pull NIM container (pull command + Helm chart shown under each model's Deploy tab). 3) Deploy on your own NVIDIA GPU(s), up to 16 for free dev/test.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to Developer Program membership; no published end date. Terms change with the NVIDIA Software License Agreement.
  - *Gotchas:* 16-GPU cap: commercial/production deployments are a license violation without AI Enterprise. You still pay for GPUs (cloud or hardware). This offer is the 2024-era framing from the official NIM FAQ; current self-host pricing is also bundled into the paid AI Enterprise product (from ~$4,500/GPU/yr).
  - *Source:* https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/nvidia-nim-faq/300317
- **NVIDIA AI Enterprise 90-day free trial license (self-hosted, production-grade)** (medium) — One-time 90-day evaluation license for the full NVIDIA AI Enterprise suite (NIM microservices, NeMo, Omniverse, K8s tooling; NVIDIA-hosted trial also mentions a free trial of NIM endpoints). Each evaluation license includes 128 concurrent users. Trial clock starts when the activation key is redeemed in the licensing portal (nvid.nvidia.com/dashboard). Renewal: no auto-renewal; a second 90-day eval license can be requested via a form, and longer POCs are referred to NVIDIA Partner Network partners.
  - *Eligibility:* Organizations with corporate/business email — personal emails (Google/Yahoo etc.) are rejected on application. Compatible infrastructure required (NVIDIA Certified Systems / product support matrix); individuals without NVIDIA hardware are steered to NVIDIA sales. No credit card or payment info visible in the registration flow; it is a license, not a bill.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to nvidia.com/en-us/data-center/products/ai-enterprise/get-started/ → 'Request Free 90-Day License' (or the Test Drive redirect URL). 2) Register with a corporate email at enterpriseproductregistration.nvidia.com. 3) Wait ~24h for approval email with license-portal access. 4) Redeem the Product Activation Key, download software from NGC, run a license server.
  - *Expiry:* Exactly 90 days from activation-key redemption; one-time per org, extension via a re-application or partner POC. Trial includes Omniverse but NOT Run:ai (separate contact needed).
  - *Gotchas:* Personal/freemail addresses are auto-denied — students without an org email effectively cannot claim it, so the 90-day trial is an org/startup offer, not a student offer. 'Failed to acquire license' message = trial over. Production use of the free Developer-Program NIM endpoints remains disallowed even while holding a trial — the trial is for self-hosted eval on your GPUs.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/data-center/products/ai-enterprise/get-started/
- **NVIDIA Inception startup program (free membership, cloud credits, no equity)** (high) — Free global virtual program for AI startups: no application fee, no membership fee, no equity taken; 'no deadlines or cohorts' — apply anytime, review ~2-4 weeks. Members get: free cloud credits from NVIDIA and partners (NVIDIA publishes NO fixed dollar figure on the page; third-party 2026 guides cite up to $100,000 in NVIDIA DGX Cloud credits for H100 capacity and up to $100,000 AWS credits via AWS Activate, plus free NIM API endpoints for prototyping), free NVIDIA Deep Learning Institute self-paced courses + discounted workshops, preferred pricing on select NVIDIA hardware/software (rebate off supplier price), full Developer Forum access, VC introductions via Inception Capital Connect, co-marketing badges/events. A 30% DGX Cloud discount tier reportedly requires a 4-node / $75,000 minimum spend (funded teams).
  - *Eligibility:* Company must be: officially incorporated, less than 10 years old, with a clear use case for NVIDIA technology (AI/ML/CV/robotics/physical AI/simulation/HPCC), actively operated by at least one founder, with a working website. No revenue, no VC funding, no US incorporation required. Excluded: consulting/outsourced-dev firms, crypto-associated companies, cloud service providers, resellers/distributors, public companies. Students/individuals cannot apply (incorporated company required).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to nvidia.com/en-us/startups/ → Apply. 2) Sign in with an NVIDIA Developer account, fill company profile (name, site, founding date, team, focus area). 3) Describe the product in 3-4 sentences with explicit AI/GPU angle. 4) Submit; after review a partner manager activates benefits (credits, NIM, training) via intro call. Contact: inceptionprogram@nvidia.com.
  - *Expiry:* Membership is open-ended (no published end date; benefits 'for the duration of membership'). Credit expiry: NVIDIA does not publish on the official page — partner credit windows vary (AWS Activate typically 1-2 years).
  - *Gotchas:* No published dollar figure exists on NVIDIA's page for cloud credits — the $100K DGX Cloud and $100K AWS numbers come from third-party 2026 guides, so verify with your account manager before budgeting. Credits burn out while the ongoing GPU bill continues. No guaranteed access to scarce GPUs. Preferred pricing is a rebate, not a guarantee of supply.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/startups/
- **NVIDIA Innovation Lab / DGX Cloud Innovation Lab for Inception startups** (medium) — Select NVIDIA Inception members can apply for the NVIDIA Innovation Lab (a.k.a. DGX Cloud Innovation Lab): ~2 months of hands-on access to NVIDIA DGX Cloud infrastructure (H100-class GPUs), integrated AI software, pre-optimized environments, and direct NVIDIA expert support/benchmarking guidance. NVIDIA's own page claims member results 'up to 17x faster performance and 90% shorter optimization cycles'. Tier-based: entry Inception gets portal access; Premier/selected members get the Lab with 'larger allocation'.
  - *Eligibility:* Must already be an accepted NVIDIA Inception member (which itself requires: incorporated, <10 years old, working website, NVIDIA use case, one active founder; excludes crypto, resellers, cloud providers, public companies). 'Purpose-built for early-stage' members; selection is invitation/application-based, not automatic for all Inception members.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply to and be accepted into NVIDIA Inception (nvidia.com/en-us/startups). 2) Once in the member portal, apply for/complete the DGX Cloud Innovation Lab (selected members are invited; the FAQ says 'select members are invited to apply'). 3) Work with the assigned NVIDIA expert during the 2-month window.
  - *Expiry:* The Lab itself is a fixed ~2-month engagement per application; the parent Inception membership is open-ended. No published repeat-access policy.
  - *Gotchas:* It is a compute grant, not a perpetual free tier — after 2 months the same 30% discount / paid DGX Cloud pricing applies. Availability is discretionary ('select members'), so acceptance into Inception does not guarantee the Lab. No equity taken by NVIDIA either way.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/data-center/innovation-lab/
- **Free hosted NIM endpoints covered by the API Trial Terms (data/usage caveats for everyone, incl. students)** (high) — The formal NVIDIA API Trial Terms of Service (PDF, last modified 2025-10-07, read in full) govern all free build.nvidia.com inference: access is 'for limited trial purposes only and without use of the API Service or Generated Content in production' (§1.2). NVIDIA 'may extend trial service credits' (amounts per API service; §1.4) — after credits are exhausted, production requires a separate paid Subscription. §3.3: NVIDIA collects session metrics, error/execution logs, feedback, and User/Generated Content 'to improve NVIDIA products and services, including AI models', and usage is logged for security/fraud and shared with third-party providers. §2.4: fine-tuning service stores user content 30 days and generated content 90 days unless a subscription is purchased. §11.2: NVIDIA may terminate your use 'at any time without notice'.
  - *Eligibility:* Applies to every free-tier user (students, individual devs, startups alike); requires an adult of legal age in the country of use, Developer Program account + phone-verified API key, and a supported country (sanctioned-region exclusions apply). No .edu email, no CC, no funding cap.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim — it is the legal layer under the free tier above. Read the Trial Terms PDF linked from every build.nvidia.com model card ('The trial service is governed by the NVIDIA API Trial Terms of Service').
  - *Expiry:* No expiry of the terms themselves; each trial/credit grant, model endpoint, or service can be terminated by NVIDIA at any time (§11.2, §1.3 pre-release clauses). Arbitration/class-waiver opt-out must be sent within 30 days of starting use to be valid (§14.4).
  - *Gotchas:* Do not send PII, financial/health/government data or PCI data (§4.3). Do not use free endpoints to build a product that 'compete[s] with the API Service' (§4.12). No sublicensing or redistribution of outputs (§4.2). Because content can be used to improve NVIDIA models, treat the free tier as unsuitable for confidential workloads. 'Free' ≠ perpetual: endpoints and rate limits can change or be sunset at NVIDIA's discretion with no liability.
  - *Source:* https://assets.ngc.nvidia.com/products/api-catalog/legal/NVIDIA%20API%20Trial%20Terms%20of%20Service.pdf
- **NVIDIA Inception Program (startups) — free membership & core benefits** (high) — Free, no-equity program: free self-paced NVIDIA Deep Learning Institute courses, discounted expert-led workshops, free cloud credits from NVIDIA and partners, preferred pricing (rebate on select hardware/software), NVIDIA Developer Forums access, Inception Capital Connect (VC exposure), badges/co-marketing. No application fees, deadlines, or cohorts. Note: the task's entry URL nvidia.com/en-us/ai-catalog/inception/ now 404s; the live official page is nvidia.com/en-us/startups/ (verified today).
  - *Eligibility:* Company must be officially incorporated, less than 10 years old, actively operated by at least one founder, with a clear NVIDIA technology use case; any funding stage including bootstrapped; global, no stated region limit. Excluded: consulting/outsourced-dev firms, crypto-related companies, cloud service providers, resellers/distributors, public companies. NOT a .edu program — no student individual track, a legal entity is required.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at nvidia.com/en-us/startups/ (portal: programs.nvidia.com/phoenix/application). 2) Company is reviewed; keep profile/product details current in the portal to remain eligible. 3) Request benefits (cloud credits, partner offers, courses) from the Inception benefits catalog; partners approve individually. No credit card required for membership.
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiry for membership; credits have partner-defined validity (e.g., Yotta 24 months, GCP 2 years).
  - *Gotchas:* 'Free cloud credits' is a per-benefit request, not a fixed grant — amounts depend on which partner benefit you request. Preferred pricing is a rebate via suppliers, not a discount NVIDIA can guarantee. Membership can lapse if the portal profile goes stale.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/startups/
- **NVIDIA DGX Cloud credits — up to $100,000 for H100 capacity (Inception members)** (medium) — Announced at GTC 2025 (March 2025 press release 'NVIDIA Expands Cloud Benefits for Startups'): NVIDIA offers qualified Inception members up to $100,000 in NVIDIA DGX Cloud credits for dedicated NVIDIA H100 GPU capacity, plus expert office hours. The figure still appears on NVIDIA's official Cloud Partners page as of today (2026-10-22), but the Inception main page does not list the benefit and 'qualified' criteria are unpublished; current availability not reconfirmable today.
  - *Eligibility:* Inception members (see base program: incorporated, <10 yrs old, any funding stage). 'Qualified members approved by NVIDIA after requesting the benefit in the portal' — qualification criteria are not public. No student path.
  - *How to claim:* As an Inception member, request the DGX Cloud credit benefit in the Inception benefits catalog; NVIDIA reviews and may approve up to $100,000. No CC required by NVIDIA itself.
  - *Expiry:* Not published.
  - *Gotchas:* Gated request, not automatic; no dollar amount promised at membership; criteria unpublished; if NVIDIA has wound this down, it was quietly — the Cloud Partners page still advertises it (stale-risk).
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/data-center/gpu-cloud-computing/partners/
- **NVIDIA Cloud Partner free credits via Inception — Nebius up to $150K (+$10K inference), Yotta up to $50K, plus Lambda/Scaleway/Lintasarta** (medium) — GTC 2025 (April 2025) expansion: Nebius 'AI Lift' — up to $150,000 in Nebius AI Cloud credits (NVIDIA GPUs) plus $10,000 specifically for inference workloads; first 3 months of the program also gave priority Blackwell access. Yotta — up to $50,000 in credits in three tiers ($10K/$15K/$25K), valid 24 months (confirmed on Yotta's official program page today). Lambda and Scaleway offer credits; NVIDIA said these partner/ISV offers total ~$300,000 in credits and discounts combined. Each partner sets its own amount/terms and approves the request.
  - *Eligibility:* NVIDIA Inception or NVIDIA Connect members who request the specific partner benefit in the Inception portal and are approved by that partner. Global; partner-specific terms apply (Yotta's $15K/$25K tiers need more detail about your NVIDIA-platform usage).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Join Inception. 2) In the benefits catalog, request the desired partner (Nebius/Yotta/etc.). 3) Partner reviews and issues credits on its platform; Yotta credits valid 24 months.
  - *Expiry:* Yotta: 24 months from grant; others partner-defined.
  - *Gotchas:* Amounts are partner-defined, not NVIDIA's; partner approvals can be slow. Yotta tiers require describing how you use NVIDIA SDKs/platform. The $300K 'total' NVIDIA headline is a marketing aggregate, not stackable credits.
  - *Source:* https://yotta.com/NVIDIA-Startup-Inception-Program/Program-Structure.html
- **AWS Activate via Inception (NVIDIA-issued Org ID) — Portfolio up to $200,000; self-funded Founders up to $5,000; invite-only AI tier $200,000+** (high) — Official AWS Activate page (verified today): Activate Portfolio = up to $200,000 in credits (pre-Series B, requires Org ID from an Activate provider; NVIDIA is an approved provider via Inception). Activate Founders (self-funded/bootstrapped) = start with $1,000, up to $5,000 total, apply directly without Org ID. New 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' = $200,000+, invite-only for scaling startups via account manager. A new 'Kiro Startup Credits' track also appears. Credits are redeemable on third-party models on Amazon Bedrock; 'AWS Account on Paid Tier Plan' is required. NVIDIA developer forums (2025–2026) show members previously receiving $10K/$25K tiers and up to $75K–$100K Scale-tier approvals, plus persistent Org-ID handoff issues between Inception and AWS.
  - *Eligibility:* Founded within the last 10 years; Pre-Series B (Portfolio); most recent funding round rules apply on AWS's side; bootstrapped founders use the Founders tier directly; for Inception-referred applicants: Inception membership + NVIDIA-confirmed AWS benefit + NVIDIA Org ID. AWS paid-tier account (credit card on file) required.
  - *How to claim:* Inception route: 1) Join Inception, request the AWS benefit in the portal. 2) NVIDIA confirms eligibility and issues an AWS Activate Org ID. 3) Apply on AWS Activate (startups.aws.com) using the Org ID; AWS reviews (5–10 business days per AWS; community reports months). Direct route (self-funded): apply directly to Activate Founders, no Org ID. No .edu/student track (individuals just need an AWS account; students have AWS Free Tier separately).
  - *Expiry:* Activate credits validity is set by AWS promotional terms (typically 12 months from grant) — not restated on the page today.
  - *Gotchas:* Official tiers were restructured — the older member-reported $10K/$25K/Scale-up-to-$100K offers no longer appear on AWS's current page; the handoff (NVIDIA Org ID → AWS) has documented 3–15 month delays and auto-rejections ('already received credits at same or greater amount', 'provider has not confirmed affiliation'). Paid-tier account = CC required. Bedrock third-party model usage consumes credits.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **Google Cloud for Startups Cloud Program — up to $350,000 credits over 2 years (AI track)** (high) — Official GCP page (verified today): 'Up to $350,000 in Cloud credits over two years', plus credits for frontier models (Gemini/Gemma), technical/product training. Tier structure (footnotes on the page): AI startups — Year 1: up to $250,000 at 100%; Year 2: up to an additional $100,000 at 20%. Other startups — Year 1: up to $100,000 at 100%; Year 2: up to an additional $100,000 at 20%. 'Most tiers require verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding.' Third-party models are billed directly, not covered by credits. This program is a stated Inception partner benefit (NVIDIA↔GCP collab since April 2024 offered the up-to-$350K path through Inception).
  - *Eligibility:* Incorporated startup, verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding for most tiers; AI track requires the product to be AI-focused; apply at cloud.google.com/startup/apply. Inception members get an accelerated onboarding path (partner deal). No .edu requirement; not open to individuals/students. Card policy not restated on the page today (GCP account required; standard GCP trials normally take a card on file).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at cloud.google.com/startups (Apply to the program). 2) Verify funding stage / company details. 3) If Inception member, use the partner fast-track. 4) Draw down: AI — up to $250K year 1; +up to $100K at 20% (i.e., up to $20K effective in year 2 per footnote wording). 5) Credits cover Google-built models; third-party model inference is billed separately.
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable over 2 years as structured above.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Year 2: 20% up to an additional $100,000' means only 20% of year-2 usage up to $100K is credited, not the full amount — real max over 2 years is less than a naive $350K + $100K read. Third-party (e.g., non-Google) model API calls are excluded. Pre-Seed–Series A gate excludes bootstrapped/late-stage companies (unlike AWS's pre-Series B).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startups
- **Google Cloud free trial + always-free tier — $300 / new customers (open to students & individuals)** (high) — Official page (verified today): 'New customers get $300 in free credit to try Google Cloud products and build a proof of concept. You won't be charged until you activate your full paid account.' Plus 20+ always-free products with monthly limits ('$300 in free credits and 20+ free products'), free trial instances for GKE, Cloud SQL, Spanner, AlloyDB, Bigtable, plus Gemini Enterprise Plus free trial. This is the individual/student-reachable free GPU/cloud front of the ecosystem (GCP A-series GPU instances can be run on the $300 trial).
  - *Eligibility:* New GCP customers; open to anyone with a Google account (students with .edu or personal both qualify); no funding/age requirements; global. Standard signup takes a credit/debit card on file (not restated in today's page text — historical standard).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at console.cloud.google.com/freetrial; $300 credit is valid for 90 days under standard terms; always-free quotas apply regardless. Use on A3/A100/A100M GPU instances or Vertex AI for ML training jobs.
  - *Expiry:* $300 trial: 90 days (standard terms); always-free tier: indefinite, monthly limits.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial is a 90-day time-boxed trial, not perpetual free compute; if the paid account activates after trial expiry, standard pay-as-you-go applies; card required (verify current signup). GPU availability for A100/A3 is region-limited.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Microsoft for Startups — 'up to $150,000 in credits' on Azure + free AI tools & GitHub credits** (medium) — Official hub (verified today): 'Access cutting-edge AI models, developer tools, and up to $150,000 in credits' — a large 2025/2026 expansion vs the legacy $1,200/year Azure + $1,200 GitHub framing. Credits apply to eligible Azure services on a sponsored subscription; Microsoft Learn (today) documents credit-aware use across GitHub (Copilot, Actions, billing connected via the Azure subscription), Microsoft Foundry models, Fireworks models, and AKS (AKS usage on a sponsored subscription is auto-covered). Tier details and eligibility are gated behind the application flow, not published on the marketing page.
  - *Eligibility:* Incorporated startups; Microsoft's own 'Microsoft for Startups' application decides tier/amount; Inception members can apply independently (no NVIDIA Org ID involved). No .edu/student track — it is a startup program. Exact funding/age caps not stated on the pages verified today.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply via Microsoft for Startups (www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups → apply/claim benefits). 2) Claim 'Startup credits' into a sponsored Azure subscription. 3) Optionally connect that subscription as GitHub's payment method so GitHub Copilot/Actions metered usage bills against the credits. No CC required for the sponsored credits themselves (standard Azure account still needed).
  - *Expiry:* Not published on verified pages; Microsoft historically used 12-month renewal windows for Azure startup credits.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Up to $150,000' is a marketing ceiling — the bulk of recipients get far less; published tier table not found on marketing pages. Partner/community models on Foundry are billed separately from Microsoft-sold models. Legacy $1,200 figure no longer appears — check the application flow for what you will actually be offered.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/
