- **NVIDIA Inception Innovation Lab — 60 days of hands-on NVIDIA GPU access (selected members)** (medium) — Inception benefit: 60 days of hands-on NVIDIA GPU access on a self-serve platform with pre-optimized environments and expert support, run in cohorts; NVIDIA provides the cloud instance (no dollar value published). Reported in the current (2026) Inception benefits summary; it is a selected-member benefit, not automatic.
  - *Eligibility:* Selected NVIDIA Inception members (cohort-based); same base Inception eligibility (incorporated, <10 yrs, NVIDIA use case).
  - *How to claim:* Join Inception, then be selected into an Innovation Lab cohort; request via the benefits catalog.
  - *Expiry:* 60 days per cohort.
  - *Gotchas:* Cohort selection — approval not guaranteed; time-boxed 60 days; no published dollar value; availability fluctuates.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudroutehq.com/startup-credits/nvidia-inception
- **build.nvidia.com — free NIM inference for open models incl. Nemotron (open access, incl. students)** (medium) — Official build.nvidia.com (verified today): 'Free inference with leading models' — free API endpoints for Nemotron models (and other open models, e.g., DeepSeek) plus pre-built agentic capabilities and blueprints. Open sign-up (no startup status or .edu stated as a requirement), so this is the individual/student-reachable 'free ML compute' front, but it is inference API, not GPU training or notebooks. Community reports a default RPM cap around 40; Inception members can request higher RPM for research workloads (forum, Oct 2026).
  - *Eligibility:* Any NVIDIA developer account; Inception members may request raised rate limits. No funding/institution gate documented. No CC stated for inference use.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign in at build.nvidia.com. 2) Use the model's API endpoint / key to run inference free within rate limits. 3) Inception members: request higher RPM via the Inception portal / developer forums for research benchmarks.
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiry; free tier subject to change.
  - *Gotchas:* Inference-only — cannot train or host notebooks here; rate-limited; free-tier availability of specific models can change; heavier workloads require paid or partner-cloud (Inception credit) routes.
  - *Source:* https://build.nvidia.com/
- **NVIDIA education angle — free self-paced DLI courses; no dedicated student GPU-compute program found** (medium) — NVIDIA's student/education-free offer is educational, not compute: free self-paced Deep Learning Institute courses (course codes distributed via Inception membership; public DLI catalog is also free), discounted instructor-led workshops, free CUDA Toolkit/NGC developer downloads. No NVIDIA 'education license' or .edu GPU quota program (e.g., Colab-style notebooks) was found in the Inception or NVIDIA education pages as of today; student GPU compute in this ecosystem effectively comes through the partner free trials (GCP $300/90-day + free A-series eligibility, AWS Free Tier, Azure) or via starting a company and joining Inception.
  - *Eligibility:* Individuals/students: free DLI courses, build.nvidia.com inference, partner free trials (no .edu email specifically required by NVIDIA; .edu mainly matters at third parties like Google's student pricing elsewhere). Startups: Inception (incorporated entity).
  - *How to claim:* Students: enroll in DLI courses / use build.nvidia.com free inference / claim GCP $300 trial and AWS Free Tier on a personal account. Startups: apply to Inception for the partner-credit stack above.
  - *Expiry:* N/A (courses free; trials per partner terms).
  - *Gotchas:* Do not conflate 'free trials' (time-boxed, card on file, pay-as-you-go after) with 'free' — only DLI, build.nvidia.com inference (rate-limited), and partner credits are genuinely free.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/startups/
- **2025–26 change:** Education/student-specific offers: as of 2026-10-22 I could NOT verify a dedicated NVIDIA education license or student program for inference/AI Enterprise (NVIDIA education-related URLs www.nvidia.com/en-us/education/ and /about-nvidia/education/ return 404; my web-search budget for this session was exhausted, so I could not confirm whether a GTC-era 'AI for Students' pilot at partner universities still exists). What is verifiably available to students is: the free Developer Program NIM tier (finding 1) and the free 16-GPU self-hosted NIM license (finding 2) — both open to individual students with no .edu requirement, plus free DLI courses if their startup is in Inception. A dedicated student free-offer line could not be confirmed and should not be cited. Sunset/changes 2025-2026: (a) The legacy '1,000 signup credits, expandable to 5,000 with work-email verification' mechanism reported on the NIM forum in 2024-2025 has effectively been replaced by the published 40 RPM + 10,000 requests/day rate-limit model — official model pages show no credit counter; 2026-08 forum threads still use the old framing, NVIDIA has not officially confirmed the credits pool was removed (disputed; check account panel). (b) The 2024 official NIM FAQ's framing ('160+ models', '90-day trial' linked off the dev-program page) is superseded by the 2026 catalog rotation (Nemotron 3.x/DeepSeek V4.x/GLM 5.3 generation) and by consolidation of Omniverse terms into 'NVIDIA AI Enterprise' (AI Product Terms last modified 2026-04-15). (c) DGX Cloud's old 'free trial' web page (nvidia.com/.../free-trial-nvidia-ai-enterprise) now 301-redirects to the corporate-email enterprise registration portal, meaning the self-serve free trial path has been tightened to organizations with corporate emails. (d) Rate limits are explicitly non-adjustable on the free tier; moderators direct all RPM-increase requests to paid AI Enterprise channels (2026 forum replies). Inception publishes no dollar figure for its cloud credits; widely-cited 'up to $100,000 DGX Cloud' number is third-party-sourced only. Regional note: the free tier is unavailable in Russia, Belarus, Iran, Kazakhstan, Uzbekistan, Kyrgyzstan, Pakistan, Bangladesh and 6 other countries (NVIDIA's own otp-unsupported-countries config, read 2026-09-26).
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes/sunsets: (1) The task's entry URL https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/ai-catalog/inception/ now returns 404 — Inception's live official page moved to https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/startups/ (verified today). (2) AWS Activate restructured: the member-reported Inception-ecosystem tiers ($10K base / $25K Grow / up to $100K Scale, per NVIDIA developer-forum threads 2024-2025) no longer appear on AWS's current official page; the official structure is now Activate Founders (self-funded, up to $5,000, start $1,000), Activate Portfolio (pre-Series B, up to $200,000, NVIDIA Org ID via Inception), and invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' ($200,000+), plus a new 'Kiro Startup Credits' track. Documented 3-15 month handoff delays (Org ID issues) persist in late-2025/2026 forum threads. (3) GCP for Startups is still up to $350K, now formally structured over 2 years (Year-1 100% / Year-2 20% footnotes, up to $250K Y1 AI / $100K Y1 others) with verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding required for most tiers — the older flat 'up to $200,000' marketing was effectively superseded by the $350K AI headline. The $300/90-day GCP free trial is unchanged. (4) Microsoft for Startups headline credits expanded from the legacy $1,200/yr Azure + $1,200 GitHub to 'up to $150,000 in credits' with Foundry/GitHub credit-aware usage (current official hub). (5) New since 2025: NVIDIA DGX Cloud H100 credit cap (up to $100,000, GTC 2025) and Nebius AI Lift (up to $150K + $10K inference, GTC April 2025). Nothing on the GCP $300 trial, GCP always-free tier, or build.nvidia.com free inference was sunset; Microsoft and AWS program details not published beyond the levels quoted. No confirmed full sunset of any offer in this stack as of 2026-10-22, though the $100K DGX Cloud offer's current operational availability is unconfirmable (still advertised on NVIDIA's Cloud Partners page but absent from the main Inception page).

### OpenAI

- **No free API tier / no signup credits (API is pay-as-you-go)** (medium) — The OpenAI API has no free tier: new API projects get zero free credits; you must add a payment method and prepay a minimum of ~$5 before the first request succeeds. Sign-up for an account is free, but the first API call returns 429 (usage limit) until balance and billing are set up. Credits are prepaid and burn off; they expire ~1 year from purchase. This is the confirmed status as of 2026-10: multiple official OpenAI support replies on the developer forum (May-July 2025 and later) state 'no free credits' and point to prepaid billing. Note: openai.com blocks automated fetches (403 today), so the official quickstart page itself could not be re-read; finding rests on repeated OpenAI staff replies plus platform behavior.
  - *Eligibility:* All developers — N/A, there is nothing to claim. No .edu or startup path bypasses this.
  - *How to claim:* Create an API org at platform.openai.com, add a card, deposit >= $5 prepaid credits, then build. No free-credit step exists.
  - *Expiry:* Prepaid credits expire ~12 months from purchase; unused credits at org close are non-refundable per billing terms.
  - *Gotchas:* 429 on first request is the normal state without billing, not a bug. Higher-security models (o-series, image models, Codex) additionally require org verification. Third-party 'grant' programs occasionally award ad-hoc credits, but there is no standing promo. The old one-time $5 new-user credit has been discontinued — do not expect it.
  - *Source:* https://community.openai.com/t/is-there-a-free-trial-or-developer-credits-for-entrepreneurs/1311550
- **OpenAI Researcher Access Program (up to $1,000 API credits, researchers only)** (high) — OpenAI's only standing subsidized-credits program. Researchers can apply for up to $1,000 in OpenAI API credits, valid for 12 months, applicable to any publicly available model. Credits cannot be extended or renewed, and you must complete your research within the 1 year. Applications are reviewed once every 3 months (March, June, September, December); awarded credits take 4-6 weeks after the decision. Focus areas: responsible deployment of AI, safety/alignment, societal impact. Application goes through a SurveyMonkey form via openai.com/form/researcher-access-program.
  - *Eligibility:* Researchers with an active affiliation to an academic institution, research organization, or qualifying nonprofit (not for-profit startups/companies). OpenAI 'encourages applications from early-stage researchers in countries supported by our API' and prioritizes those with limited financial/institutional resources. API-supported country requirement; no stated funding cap on the applicant. .edu email per se not required, but institutional affiliation is.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Fill the application at openai.com/form/researcher-access-program (third-party SurveyMonkey Apply account needed). 2) Describe research question and planned use of OpenAI products. 3) Wait for the next quarterly review cycle (Mar/Jun/Sep/Dec). 4) If approved, credits land 4-6 weeks later. No credit card required for the credits themselves (API account still needs standard setup).
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire exactly 12 months after grant; no renewal. Quarterly application windows (March, June, September, December).
  - *Gotchas:* This is a research program — commercial product development (e.g. a solo-entrepreneur app) is explicitly out of scope; a 2025 forum thread confirms it's the only alternative to paid credits and it requires an academic/nonprofit affiliation. Subject to OpenAI's Usage Policies and a sharing & publication policy. Review cadence is slow (up to ~3 months between cycles + 4-6 weeks to credit).
  - *Source:* https://openai.com/form/researcher-access-program/
- **ChatGPT Free plan (consumer, rate-limited, no API access)** (medium) — The consumer ChatGPT Free plan exists: unlimited-ish chat on the current frontier models (GPT-5.x family since Aug 2025) with small daily message limits that grow with usage, plus image generation (DALL·E, roughly a few dozen images/month) and video (Sora, ~50 watermarked generations/month, 1080p). No credit card required. Limits are soft and reset daily/monthly; OpenAI adjusts them frequently and by region. This is a product, not a developer offer: it gives no API keys, no fine-tuning, and (on Free) your prompts may be used for training per the consumer terms.
  - *Eligibility:* Any individual; 18+ in supported regions. No .edu email, no credit card, no age/funding cap. Regional availability of some models/features varies.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at chatgpt.com with email/phone or SSO; Free plan is the default. Nothing else to claim; paid plans (Plus $20/mo, Pro $200/mo) are optional upgrades.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry while the Free plan exists; specific model access and limits can be changed or removed at any time (e.g., Sora free-tier limits tightened through 2025).
  - *Gotchas:* Do not conflate ChatGPT Free with API access — the API is a separate paid product. Free-tier limits have oscillated (message caps, Sora caps, image caps) and differ by region; treat the numbers above as approximate. Free-tier conversations may be retained and used for model training under consumer data terms, unlike paid tiers where you can opt out of training use.
  - *Source:* https://openai.com/chatgpt/pricing/
- **OpenAI for Teachers / OpenAI for Schools (free Pro-tier access for verified educators)** (medium) — OpenAI gives verified teachers free access to OpenAI Pro (announced Oct 2024; expanded through 2025 into K-12, college and university programs — 'OpenAI for Schools'), including the full Pro model set, extended context, and classroom/teacher features, with no ads and at no cost to the teacher; schools that adopt it in bulk get a discounted ChatGPT Edu/Teams license for the whole institution. This is the education-license front: the individual benefit is aimed at educators, not students.
  - *Eligibility:* Teachers/educators at schools, colleges or universities (K-12 or higher-ed) in supported regions, verified via school email/institutional ID at openai.com/teachers. No credit card required for the free teacher access. Students do not get personal accounts from this — they benefit only where their institution has licensed ChatGPT.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via OpenAI's for-teachers page/portal, verify with your school email, get Pro at $0. Institutions: school admin purchases ChatGPT Edu/Teams per-seat (institution pays, staff/students access free through the school license).
  - *Expiry:* Free as long as teacher verification remains active; OpenAI has periodically expanded rather than sunsetted it (K-12 push in 2025). No stated end date.
  - *Gotchas:* Verification can be slow and occasional; program terms target instructional use. For students, the free path is only via their school's license — an individual student with just a .edu email cannot self-enroll for free. Not a startup/developer offer at all.
  - *Source:* https://openai.com/teachers/
- **ChatGPT Campus / university partnerships (free-for-student via institutional purchase)** (medium) — OpenAI sells ChatGPT Edu / Teams / Campus licenses to universities (650+ institutions signed deals through 2025; 'ChatGPT Campus' bundles ChatGPT access for enrolled students, typically with Sora/Codex features, paid per-seat by the school). For a student this is a free offer in practice — enrollment in a partner university grants account access at no personal cost, usually via .edu SSO. OpenAI is the one subsidizing nothing directly; the school is the customer.
  - *Eligibility:* Enrolled students/faculty at a partner institution; access typically via .edu SSO or a school-issued license. No personal credit card, no personal payment. Non-partner students get nothing from OpenAI directly.
  - *How to claim:* No personal claim flow: use your university's access (SSO/portal). If your school has no deal, there is no OpenAI student discount page to apply to (unlike some competitors' student programs).
  - *Expiry:* Lasts for the term of the school's license; renews when the school renews. Varies by institution.
  - *Gotchas:* Uneven coverage — availability depends entirely on your school. Some deals are discounted-plus rather than fully free to the student. This is a B2B channel, not a grants program; don't list it as a claimable offer for unaffiliated students or startups.
  - *Source:* https://openai.com/index/chatgpt-campus/
- **Free tokens for data-sharing (opt-in) — SUNSET April 30, 2025** (high) — In December 2024 OpenAI gave organizations that opted in to sharing prompts/completions up to 11M free tokens/day (1M across GPT-4o/o1-class, 10M across mini-class models). Originally ending Feb 28, 2025, it was extended once to April 30, 2025, and then ended. As of 2026-10 there is no successor program. This was the only time-bounded 'free tokens' mechanism on the paid API.
  - *Eligibility:* Paid-API organization owners who enabled data sharing (Data Sharing Settings). Required a nonzero balance and worked for Chat Completions/Batch APIs, not all endpoints.
  - *How to claim:* No longer claimable — the program lapsed April 30, 2025.
  - *Expiry:* Ended April 30, 2025 (extended from Feb 28, 2025).
  - *Gotchas:* Documented footguns: balance had to be >$0 or the free tokens wouldn't apply; applicability varied by API surface. If a dashboard still shows enrollment wording, it is stale UI — no credits accrue.
  - *Source:* https://community.openai.com/t/free-tokens-on-traffic-shared-with-openai-extended-through-april-30-2025/1129643
- **No startup program / no grant credits for startups or students-as-developers** (medium) — OpenAI does not run a public startup, student-developer, or accelerator credit program (no 'OpenAI for Startups', no .edu developer credits, no first-time-founder grants) as of 2026-10. Official OpenAI replies on the developer forum (2025) confirm: 'There is no free credit for individuals' — the only credit path is the Researcher Access Program (academic/nonprofit researchers). Anything else is ad-hoc marketing promos that appear and disappear without standing eligibility.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — no standing program exists to qualify for. The startup-age/funding-cap question is moot.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim. Realistic routes for a cash-poor student/startup: (1) Researcher Access Program if the work is research with academic/nonprofit affiliation; (2) a $5 prepaid API balance with hard usage limits (cost controls) on the paid API; (3) ChatGPT Free consumer plan for interactive use, not for API work.
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* Beware third-party 'OpenAI credits' offers — OpenAI grants arrive only on your own org's billing page. Prepaid API credits expire in 12 months and can't be renewed; the old $5 sign-up credit is dead, so new accounts start at $0.000
  - *Source:* https://community.openai.com/t/is-there-a-free-trial-or-developer-credits-for-entrepreneurs/1311550
- **2025–26 change:** 1) One-time ~$5 API sign-up credit (legacy, ended ~2023) — no new accounts get it; confirmed dead by OpenAI staff replies throughout 2025. 2) Free-tokens-for-data-sharing program: launched Dec 2024, extended to Apr 30, 2025, SUNSET (no successor as of 2026-10). 3) Limited-time free previews (o1/o1-pro, mid-2024) long ended. 4) 2025-2026 expansions: OpenAI for Teachers/for Schools grew from educator Pro-access to a K-12/university push; ChatGPT Campus B2B deals scaled to 650+ institutions (free for students only via the school's license). 5) Consumer side shifts: GPT-5-family models moved to the Free plan (Aug 2025) with daily caps; Sora and DALL·E ride on Free with watermarked/limited generation caps that OpenAI has tightened during 2025 — so 'free' consumer access exists but is rate-limited, region-variable, and changeable without notice. 6) No 2025-2026 change reintroduced any standing free developer credits: as of 2026-10 the API remains pay-as-you-go from the first token, with the Researcher Access Program ($1,000, 12-month, quarterly review) as the only subsidized-credits program and it is scoped to academic/nonprofit researchers, not startups or students-as-builders. Caveat: openai.com and help.openai.com returned HTTP 403 to automated fetches on 2026-10-22, and the shared web-search budget was exhausted, so official pricing/education pages could not be re-read live today; confidence is high only where the page/form text itself surfaced in results (Researcher Access Program) or where OpenAI's official forum post was captured (free-tokens sunset); other findings carry medium confidence.
- **Critic correction** (OpenAI (ChatGPT / API)): Partly contradicted. OpenAI's rate-limit guide (developers.openai.com, read Oct 22, 2026) lists a Free usage tier with no purchase requirement, open to users in allowed geographies and capped at $100 per month. The statement that the API is pay-as-you-go from the first token is outdated. No free-tier model list appeared on that page, and the no free API credits point still roughly holds.
- **Critic correction** (No free API credits for developers; pay-as-you-go from the first token): OpenAI offers complimentary daily tokens to eligible organizations that opt in to sharing API traffic. The help article says some organizations 'may qualify for daily complimentary tokens on traffic shared with OpenAI' (wording quoted in an Aug 21, 2026 community thread). Sept 2025 platform text quoted 1M tokens/day on flagship models and 10M/day on mini models. The help page returned 403 to my fetch, so these quotes are secondhand.
- **Critic correction** (outdated: 'No free API credits for developers' omits conditional complimentary daily tokens): Organizations that opt into sharing API inputs and outputs with OpenAI may get daily complimentary tokens on listed models. Eligibility is not published; the Help Center says only that some organizations may qualify. Limits have changed (1M and 10M tokens per day in 2025; 250K and 2.5M reported in March 2026). Reword the entry as conditional.

### OpenRouter Free Models

- **Free tier: ':free' model variants and openrouter/free Free Models Router** (high) — Any signed-in OpenRouter account gets rate-limited (not credit-limited) access to the catalog of $0/token free models. Limits today: 20 requests/minute in both tiers; 50 requests/day if lifetime purchased credits < $10; 1,000 requests/day once at least $10 of credits has been purchased all-time (the threshold in the docs table is 'at least 10 credits' and the higher tier is granted starting one credit below the threshold, i.e. effectively $10 lifetime spend). The daily counter/ceiling is exposed in `free_model_daily_requests` on GET /api/v1/key. The pricing page (Oct 2026) advertises the Free plan as '25+ free models, 4 free providers, 5 workspaces, 50 requests/day'; the openrouter/free router page lists 29 free-variant models in the router (the catalog rotates). `openrouter/free` is an auto-router that picks a random free model matching your request's capabilities; a :free model's `models` fallback array bills fallbacks at their own price, so keep requests free by listing only :free / openrouter/free entries.
  - *Eligibility:* Any OpenRouter account (email, GitHub, or crypto wallet signup). No credit card required (Free plan lists 'Payment options: No'). No .edu or region requirement. is_free_tier = 'user has never paid for credits'; BYOK requests and exempt accounts/arenas are not gated by the daily counter.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at openrouter.ai (no card needed) and call the API with a model ID ending in ':free' or with model 'openrouter/free' (OpenAI-compatible REST or SDK; Chat Playground at openrouter.ai/chat lists 'Free Models Router'). To raise the daily cap: buy >= $10 of credits once (credits purchased are lifetime-counted; the tier is permanent even if balance later drops below $10). Check remaining free quota via GET /api/v1/key.
  - *Expiry:* Permanent free tier; no expiry or renewal. Daily counter resets each UTC day. The $10 threshold is lifetime (all-time credits purchased), not a subscription.
  - *Gotchas:* 20 RPM cap is fixed even at the 1,000/day tier. OpenRouter charges a 5.5% ($0.80 minimum) card fee or 5% crypto fee on credit purchases, so the effective cost to unlock 1,000/day is $10.80 via card (16% overhead at a $5 top-up; $10 costs $10.80 = 8%). Free-model availability rotates ('which models are free changes frequently'); free models may have reduced context/quality. Free-model providers may log prompts/completions and use data for training unless your account's privacy routing settings exclude such providers; there is also an opt-in logging setting that trades a 1% usage discount for logging. OpenRouter reserves the right to expire unused credits one year after purchase (FAQ), which partially conflicts with third-party claims that 'credits never expire'. Fallbacks to paid models are billed even if the primary model is :free. Mid-stream rate limits arrive as an SSE finish_reason 'error', not a separate HTTP code.
  - *Source:* https://openrouter.ai/docs/api_reference/limits
- **OpenRouter for Startups: up to $5,000 universal inference credits + 12 months of 0% platform/BYOK fees** (high) — Official startup program (openrouter.ai/startup-program, terms last updated Aug 22, 2026). Benefit: up to $5,000 USD of universal inference credits usable across 500+ models, plus 0% processing fees for 12 months (covering both platform fees and BYOK fees); pay only token list price. Credits are promotional: no cash value, non-refundable, non-transferable, not stackable with other promotions; amounts are at OpenRouter's discretion ('up to'). Terms also allow benefits to vary by referral source and available program capacity. Rolling applications; decisions typically within a few days; if approved, credits/fee waivers are auto-applied to the OpenRouter account tied to the applicant's email.
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-Series B stage (or equivalent as determined by OpenRouter); building an AI-native product full-time; referred by an approved OpenRouter partner; public company website; professional email on the company domain (generic aliases like admin@/support@/ops@ rejected); valid OpenRouter account with less than $500 lifetime OpenRouter spend; applicant has not previously received startup-program credits (once per applicant, even across multiple partner affiliations). No .edu requirement, no card requirement, no region cap stated.
  - *How to claim:* Fill out the application form at openrouter.ai/startup-program (or via your partner's perks page, e.g. Mercury/Stripe Atlas); questions to startups@openrouter.ai. Review is rolling; on approval the credits and fee waivers are automatically applied to your account. Ask for the specific credit amount in writing (benefits are 'up to' amounts).
  - *Expiry:* No published deadline; rolling while the program is operational. Fee waiver: 12 months. Credits: promotional, no stated fixed expiry on the program credits themselves, but subject to OpenRouter's general right to expire unused credits one year after purchase.
  - *Gotchas:* Amounts are discretionary and vary by referral source: the public page says 'up to $5K' while the program has also been advertised at $3,000 (third-party trackers noted the page previously showed up to $3,000; terms allow up to $5,000). The <$500 lifetime spend cap and partner-referral requirement exclude users who already spend on OpenRouter or lack a partner referral. 'Program availability may change over time'. Unused purchased credits may expire after one year per FAQ policy, so plan credit burn. 0% fee waiver is time-boxed to 12 months; standard 5.5% (Standard) / 8% (Business) platform fees resume after.
  - *Source:* https://openrouter.ai/startup-program
- **Mercury Perks partner tier: $1,000 in OpenRouter credits (6 months) + 12 months of 0% fees, for Mercury customers** (medium) — A partner-referred variant of OpenRouter for Startups offered through Mercury's perks portal. Benefit: $1K of universal inference credits, valid for 6 months, usable across OpenRouter's full model catalog, plus $0 processing fees for 12 months (platform and BYOK fees). Same rolling application and auto-apply mechanism as the main program. Each applicant may receive startup credits once even if affiliated with multiple partners.
  - *Eligibility:* Mercury customers (online business banking for startups) who are also Pre-Series B, building AI-native products, with a public company website, professional company-domain email, and an OpenRouter account with < $500 lifetime spend. No .edu, no card requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Redeem/apply from Mercury's perks page (mercury.com/perks/openrouter) — OpenRouter reviews the application on a rolling basis and applies credits + fee waiver to the applicant's OpenRouter org account.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 6 months; fee waiver 12 months; rolling application, program can change or close.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit validity is shorter (6 months) than the direct program's open-ended promotional credits; the '300+ models' wording on the Mercury page predates OpenRouter's '500+ models' catalog copy — catalog size has grown. Once-per-applicant rule applies across partners; you cannot stack Mercury, Stripe Atlas, and the direct program credits.
  - *Source:* https://mercury.com/perks/openrouter
- **Stripe Atlas partner tier: $500 inference credits with 0% fees for approved Stripe Atlas founders** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — A smaller partner tier of the same OpenRouter for Startups program, delivered through Stripe Atlas's perks catalog: $500 in inference credits plus 0% fees, redeemed after company formation approval via Atlas. Vendor eligibility and compliance requirements may apply.
  - *Eligibility:* Approved Stripe Atlas founders (company incorporated via Stripe Atlas) meeting OpenRouter's startup eligibility (Pre-Series B, AI-native product, company website, professional email, < $500 lifetime OpenRouter spend).
  - *How to claim:* Redeem from the Stripe Atlas Perks tab after Atlas approval; OpenRouter applies the credits to your account.
  - *Expiry:* Partner perk; no published credit-expiry beyond the program's promotional-credit rules and OpenRouter's right to expire unused credits after a year.
  - *Gotchas:* Smallest of the three official partner tiers; once-per-applicant rule applies. Sourced from a third-party aggregator (heap.page) that also tracks OpenRouter's recorded offers; confirm the current amount on Stripe Atlas's own perks page before relying on it — partner benefit amounts vary and can change.
  - *Source:* https://heap.page/software/openrouter
- **Student/education license: no dedicated program — free tier is the student path; trial credits: none published** (medium) — As of 2026-10-22 OpenRouter runs no student/education license, no .edu-verified grant, and no public trial-credit or welcome-credit offer. The free tier (above) is open to any account including students. Third-party FAQ tracking (Oct 2026) explicitly states OpenRouter 'doesn't offer a dedicated student or education discount' and 'doesn't issue public promo codes, coupon codes, or discount codes' (claims of '$5 welcome credits' are false). An SEO aggregator page (themodelverse.in) markets an 'OpenRouter Free Models Router Free Student Deal / 365-day grant' with .edu verification — this is not an official OpenRouter program; no OpenRouter page describes any student grant, and the claimed 'auto-renew tripwire' contradicts the no-payment Free plan. OpenRouter's FAQ does contain the line 'All new users receive a small free allowance to test out OpenRouter', but no amount, duration or claim process is published anywhere official; treat trial credits as unverified.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A (no student-specific or trial-credit offer exists to claim). The standard free tier requires only an OpenRouter account.
  - *How to claim:* Students should simply create a normal OpenRouter account and use :free / openrouter/free models; startups should use the OpenRouter for Startups program. There is no form, code, or .edu verification to submit for a student benefit.
  - *Expiry:* N/A.
  - *Gotchas:* Ignore third-party 'student deal' aggregator pages claiming 365-day grants, auto-renewals, or .edu-gated perks for OpenRouter — they are not backed by any OpenRouter page and mix in invented renewal mechanics. Verify anything against openrouter.ai only.
  - *Source:* https://costgoat.com/deals/openrouter.ai
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) April 9, 2025 policy shift (announced; widely reported): the daily free-model cap was cut from 200 to 50 requests/day and the '>= $10 lifetime credits -> 1,000 requests/day' incentive was introduced at that time — the 50/day figure has held through 2026. (2) Startup program terms were last updated August 22, 2026, and the public page now advertises 'up to $5,000' — third-party trackers (programscape.com) recorded the startups page at 'up to $3,000' earlier in 2026, indicating the credit ceiling was raised during 2026; the program's partner tiers (Mercury $1K/6mo, Stripe Atlas $500) are the older/lower amounts. (3) June 9, 2025: credit-purchase fee was simplified to a flat 5.5% ($0.80 min) on card / 5% on crypto (removed the old fixed $0.35 Stripe charge) — this affects the effective cost of the $10 unlock. (4) OpenRouter's FAQ reserves the right to expire unused credits after one year of purchase, contradicting common 'credits never expire' lore; confirm current treatment before banking on long-lived startup credits. No student/education license ever existed to sunset; no trial-credit program was found (the FAQ's 'small free allowance' line is unpublished/unverifiable). Free-model catalog size has grown to 25-29 rotating models under 4 free providers as of Oct 2026.

### Replicate 'Try for Free' collection

- **Replicate 'Try for Free' free runs (no-credit-card free tier)** (high) — New accounts can run a rotating set of select models a limited number of times without purchasing credit and without a credit card. Current 'Try for Free' collection: google/imagen-4, black-forest-labs/flux-1.1-pro, black-forest-labs/flux-dev, black-forest-labs/flux-kontext-pro, ideogram-ai/ideogram-v3-turbo, topazlabs/image-upscale, topazlabs/video-upscale, tencentarc/gfpgan, szcho/codeformer, minimax/video-01, luma/reframe-video. Replicate does NOT publish a dollar amount or exact run count; third-party trackers estimate ~50 free predictions total. It is a one-time allowance with no monthly reset.
  - *Eligibility:* Any new user who creates a Replicate account (GitHub, Google, or email sign-in). No .edu, no startup, and no credit card required to start. One account per person (farming multiple accounts to re-collect free runs is against the one-account rule). Global. Verified today on the official collection page + FAQ.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at replicate.com (GitHub/Google/email — no card). 2) Go to replicate.com/collections/try-for-free. 3) Pick a model, upload/provide input, run it in the web playground or via API token (r8_...). 4) When the free cap is hit, Replicate asks you to add a credit card and purchase credit to continue. Claim is automatic on signup; no application.
  - *Expiry:* One-time allowance, no renewal/monthly reset. Models in the collection rotate over time. After the cap you move to pay-per-second or per-output billing (e.g., FLUX 1.1 Pro $0.04/image, FLUX dev $0.025/image, deepseek-r1 $0.01/1k output tokens).
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: these are free RUNS, not a free subscription — once used you must add a card and buy credit. No published $ or exact run count. Each model keeps its own license that can restrict commercial use even when the run is free. Cardless accounts with granted credit are rate-limited to ~1 req/sec / ~6 req/min (no load testing); 600 predictions/min only once you have a paid account. Free set rotates, so a model you want may not currently be in it. A banner ('Wan 3.0 is 30% off this week') is a temporary discount, not a free offer.
  - *Source:* https://replicate.com/collections/try-for-free
- **YC Summer Fellows (2025): Replicate $10,000 credits for students** (high) — In YC's 2025 Summer Fellows Grants, Replicate was one of the YC companies contributing $10,000 in compute credits to selected fellows (alongside Fly.io $50k, Supabase $10k, Resend $3k, plus YC's own $20k cash + $90k AWS/Azure/GCP credits). This is a real student-facing benefit tied to Replicate, but it is delivered through YC's grant, not a standalone Replicate program.
  - *Eligibility:* Undergraduate CS/engineering students, including freshmen, and students outside the US (global). Must be SELECTED for YC's Summer Fellows grant (apply; 15-min interview; YC estimated ~20-50 grants). It is merit/selection-based, not a .edu-email self-serve discount. This is a 2025 cohort (events Jun-Aug 2025).
  - *How to claim:* Apply to YC Summer Fellows Grants (ycombinator.com/blog/summer-fellows; the 2025 application deadline was end of March 2025). If selected, Replicate credits are part of the grant package — there is no self-serve claim on replicate.com. No company/business required; grant is to the individual student.
  - *Expiry:* 2025 cohort only (now closed for that cycle). YC may run a new Summer Fellows round in a later year, possibly with different partner credits — the $10k Replicate figure is not guaranteed to recur.
  - *Gotchas:* It is YC's grant, not Replicate's education program; you cannot claim it by yourself on Replicate. Credits land via YC selection, not a .edu program. Do not confuse with Replit/Google education programs that DO offer .edu discounts.
  - *Source:* https://www.ycombinator.com/blog/summer-fellows
- **Replicate Startup Program (claimed up to $10,000 credits) — UNVERIFIED / DISPUTED** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Third-party aggregators list a 'Replicate Startup Program' of up to $10,000 in inference credits (~$1,000-$10,000), ~6-month validity, pre-seed to Series A/B, no VC referral, global, company-domain email, no equity. HOWEVER: replicate.com/startups returns HTTP 404 (verified today), and at least one tracker states Replicate has no startup program. No official Replicate page confirms the program today, so this cannot be verified or claimed through an official channel.
  - *Eligibility:* As claimed by third parties only: AI/ML startup, pre-Series B / Series A, building a production product (not pure research), company-domain email; not Series C+; not research-only/academic.
  - *How to claim:* Per third parties: (1) create a Replicate account with a company-domain email + API token, (2) submit the startup application (form was said to be at replicate.com/startups), (3) receive credits on approval. Because replicate.com/startups 404s, this path is currently dead and the offer is not confirmable. Do not rely on it; contact Replicate sales if a startup is seeking volume terms.
  - *Expiry:* Unknown — no official terms or live application page. Treat as possibly sunset or never-officially-live.
  - *Gotchas:* CONFLICTING sources: the official URL is gone (404) while aggregators still advertise $10k. Low confidence it is a live, claimable program as of 2026-10-22. If it does exist it likely requires a company email + production use case. The startup-pricing alternative listed by trackers is to use Modal/Together AI/Fireworks programs instead, or ask Replicate for volume discounts.
  - *Source:* https://replicate.com/startups
- **Partner-granted micro-credits (e.g., IBM Granite invite = $0.50)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Replicate invite links can be pre-loaded with a small amount of credit granted by a partner/distributor (e.g., an IBM Granite invite that reads 'you've got $0.50 of credit to run and fine-tune models'). These are partner-specific and modest, not a general student or startup program.
  - *Eligibility:* The holder of the specific invite/partner link; sign-in (often GitHub) required to accept. Not gated on .edu or startup status.
  - *How to claim:* Open the specific partner invite URL and sign in as instructed (e.g., GitHub) to accept the credit.
  - *Expiry:* Per-invite; varies and not a recurring program.
  - *Gotchas:* Tiny amounts (fractions of a dollar); only applies for the specific partner invite; not a free tier you can self-serve. Illustrative only.
  - *Source:* https://replicate.com/
- **2025–26 change:** As of 2026-10-22: (1) Replicate's official free offer is the 'Try for Free' collection — a limited number of free runs on select models with no credit card and no published dollar amount or exact run count; it is a one-time allowance, not a recurring free tier. (2) replicate.com/startups returns 404 — so a live, self-serve Replicate Startup Program is NOT verifiable today, even though aggregators advertise up to $10k; treat that offer as disputed/possibly-sunset and prefer contacting Replicate sales or using Modal/Together AI/Fireworks startup programs. (3) No dedicated Replicate education/.edu program was found (unlike Replit or Google, which do run .edu programs). (4) The YC Summer Fellows 2025 program (Replicate $10k student credits) was a 2025 cohort and is closed for that cycle; it may recur in a later year with different partners/amounts. (5) Recurring 'Wan 3.0 30% off this week' banners on Replicate pages are temporary discounts, not free offers. Net: the only clearly-live, self-serve, card-free offer is the 'Try for Free' free-runs collection; all student/startup credit offers are either YC-cohort-gated (students) or unverifiable (startups).

### xAI Grok API

- **xAI API: no free tier, no trial credits (confirmed non-existent)** (high) — Official pricing page is pure pay-as-you-go from the first call: text models $1.00–$4.00/M input, $2.00–$12.00/M output depending on model and context length (Grok 4.7: $2.00 input/$0.50 cached/$6.00 output below 200k context; $4.00/$1.00/$12.00 above). The word 'credits' appears nowhere on the official pricing page; no free tier, trial credit, startup program, or education license is listed anywhere in the docs (pricing, quickstart, or full 2025-2026 release notes were checked). The quickstart says to 'Sign up for an account at console.x.ai' and 'load it with credits to start using the API' — i.e., you top up paid credits yourself; nothing is granted free. Cost Transparency note: responses include a cost_in_usd_ticks field (added Apr 2026) so each request shows its exact USD cost. WebSearch was unavailable (session budget exhausted), so verification was by direct fetch of official pages.
  - *Eligibility:* No eligibility criteria exist because no free offer exists. API account at console.x.ai; paid credit top-up required for usage.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim — no free offer to enroll in. Paid: sign up at console.x.ai, add an API key (API Keys page), load paid credits, then call https://api.x.ai/v1.
  - *Expiry:* n/a — standing pay-as-you-go model, no expiration; no free tier to renew
  - *Gotchas:* No minimum-charge or no-CC claim is documented anywhere today (console.x.ai blocks automated access, so billing fine print could not be re-read today). Batch API is 20% off standard rates; Priority Processing costs 2x; Files storage $0.025/GiB/day, Collections $0.10/GiB/day, downloads $0.20/GiB; Response-API violation fee $0.05/request. Agent tools priced up to $5 per 1000 calls. Any marketing claim of '$150/month free for data-sharing' does not appear on current official billing docs.
  - *Source:* https://docs.x.ai/developers/pricing;https://docs.x.ai/developers/quickstart;https://docs.x.ai/developers/release-notes
- **Grok Build free tier (coding-agent product, NOT API credits)** (medium) — Grok Build is xAI's agentic coding TUI (launched beta May 2026 with grok-build-0.1 at $1.00/$0.20/$2.00 per 1M tokens if used via the paid API; in-product, Fast model is 'billed through your plan'). The official pricing page explicitly acknowledges a Grok Build free tier, stating only that 'Grok Build's free tier does not include' Grok 4.7 Fast. Exact free-tier limits (messages/model-per-day caps, duration) are not published on any page I could read today (grok.com plan pages and console.x.ai return 403 to automated fetch), so amounts/limits are unverified. This is a free product tier, not free API usage: it does not grant api.x.ai keys or billable credits.
  - *Eligibility:* Unverified beyond the xAI docs; appears available to anyone who installs Grok Build (TUI) / has a Grok account; no .edu or startup requirement documented. Free tier excludes Grok 4.7 Fast (available only in Cursor and Grok Build paid plans, not on the public API).
  - *How to claim:* Install the Grok Build TUI (see docs.x.ai/build/overview), sign in free; the free tier gives access to standard Grok models for coding agents within undocumented limits.
  - *Expiry:* Unspecified; Grok Build 0.1 was 'in early access' (May 2026 release notes) — a beta-era product, limits could change at any time; fast-moving product
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: existence of the free tier is confirmed by xAI's own pricing page but its caps/duration are not stated there — do not report specific numbers without reading grok.com plans in a browser. It is a product tier (like Claude Code Free), NOT free xAI API quota; Grok 4.7 Fast is excluded; heavy users hit caps and must buy a Grok plan (Plus/SuperGrok tiers, prices not verified today).
  - *Source:* https://docs.x.ai/developers/pricing;https://docs.x.ai/build/overview
- **Consumer Grok app free tier (X app) — context only, not an API offer** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Grok inside X has a free consumer tier (rate-limited) plus paid Plus/SuperGrok plans; xAI blocks automated fetches of x.ai/grok.com, so current 2026 plan names/prices could not be re-verified today and are NOT reported here. The consumer free tier gives no API key and no api.x.ai access — irrelevant for the API front except as a reason some users conflate 'free Grok' with 'free Grok API'.
  - *Eligibility:* X account holders (unverified details); no .edu, no startup, no credit card required for the app tier; does not include API access
  - *How to claim:* n/a for API research; claim only by making a free X account
  - *Expiry:* Consumer free tier caps change frequently; not documented on dev docs
  - *Gotchas:* Not a developer offer; do not cite it as an API free tier. Region: consumer Grok availability has been region-limited (EU launch Jul 2026 per docs release notes for Grok 4.5).
  - *Source:* https://grok.com(planpagesreturned403toautomatedfetch—verifyinabrowser)
- **Startup program / education license: none documented for xAI** (medium) — As of 2026-10-22 no xAI startup program (age/funding caps, credits) and no education/student license (.edu) appear on any official page: the full docs navigation (docs.x.ai) exposes only a Pricing page among billing-related pages, and 15+ months of API release notes contain zero entries about credits, free tiers, startups, or education. This differs from OpenAI/Anthropic, which do run startup programs — xAI currently has no documented equivalent (unverified absent, not confirmed extinct, because web search was unavailable).
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — no documented program
  - *How to claim:* n/a
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* 'Medium' only because WebSearch budget was exhausted, so third-party/partnership programs (e.g., a YC-style or partner-distributed credit deal) cannot be fully ruled out; official docs show none.
  - *Source:* https://docs.x.ai/developers/pricing;https://docs.x.ai/developers/release-notes
- **2025–26 change:** 1) xAI API beta free tier: the old API beta granted free usage ($25/month-class credits, tied to data sharing when the API was in beta, launched Nov 2024) ended December 2024 when the API went GA on pure pay-as-you-go; that offer is closed and was not revived in 2025-2026 — no release-notes entry since Dec 2024 re-introduces credits or free tiers. 2) The widely-circulated '$150/month free for data-sharing' claim is not on any current xAI billing page — treat as unsubstantiated. 3) Unrelated sunsets noted: grok-voice-transcribe-1.0 EOL Oct 2, 2026 (routed to v2 at same price), grok-imagine-image-quality retired Nov 2, 2026, Grok Voice 2.0 deprecated (Voice 3.0 same price) — these are pricing continuity changes, not free-offer closures. 4) No startup program or education license was found in 2025-2026; the only free thing xAI ships today is the Grok Build product tier (and the consumer X-app free tier), neither of which is free API quota. 5) Method caveat: session WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task, so verification relied on direct WebFetch of official xAI docs (docs.x.ai accessible; x.ai, grok.com, help.x.com, console.x.ai all 403-bot-blocked) plus official release notes.
